# 🎬 23 Ways to Nudge: A Review of Technology-Mediated Nudging in Human-Computer Interaction — ACM SIGCHI

> And it described any aspect of the choice architecture that can alters people behavior in a predictable way without forbidding any option or changing their incentives so leveraging the concept of nudging researchers have designed it small changes in the way that choices and information are presented with the goal or guiding to decide choices so for instance one of the most popular nigel's has been introduced in a supermarket so usually when you go to the cash register you have like a bus impulse and they put there to locate their very unhealthy items such as cake and snacks and what they did was to replace these unhealthy snacks by fruit and other healthy items and they found that while their post options were still available like cake and fruit just positioning the healthy item nearby at eye level it was enough to motivate people to adopt healthy options.

**Для Напарника:** Выбор без выбора: порядок и расположение вариантов в пикере длительности и шага (легче всего выбрать полезное) без запретов.



# 🎬 4 самых важных аспекта композиции в UX UI дизайне — UPROCK


### Новичок придумывает новое правило в каждом блоке, опытный разнообразит из тех же правил  ·  [09:28](https://youtu.be/RXlAyFbamh0?t=568)
**Мысль:** Минимальный набор правил (направляющая, стили заголовков, текста) переиспользуется, что делает макет понятным и при этом свежим.

> You should not create a design where there is a new visual rule in every block. So, look here, our object is balanced to the left. And here, these photos don't provide any specifics. If these were product photos , I would rank their level of responsibility and importance the same as text information. There. But here, it’s just decor. Here, the object is not centered. We understand that it is separate. When a designer is a beginner , look, it feels as if they are just brushing onto the canvas like this. And it truly is an internal feeling, how a beginner designer draws. I went through this myself. Therefore, I will say again, I am not trolling anyone. I am simply conveying our internal feeling, how, let’s say, well, childlike and sweet they are, like bam, canvas, canvas, here-here, design, back and forth. And each block is the creation of new visual rules. This is the biggest mistake you can make. And let’s look at the work of an experienced designer. Look, there is a minimal set of visual rules here. Here we have a power guide, everything is aligned to it. Here we have the text. That is, say, an intro, and another text style and regular text. Headings. Let’s compare. A heading. Here is a heading. And this description for it. There. That is, we have a reuse of the same styles. Why is this needed, I will explain. On one hand, the human soul is very contradictory. We want it to be clear, simple, and easy. That is, these rules should repeat the rules found on other sites, and at the same time, these same rules are reused here. But on the other hand, everything should be somehow fresh. And here is an important nuance: to diversify the layout, in the first case the person invented rules on the spot, but in our second case, they created something new out of the same rules. It is logical that the second is harder to execute.

**Для Напарника:** Не плодить новые стили в каждом блоке Дома; разнообразие создавать комбинациями существующих токенов.



# 🎬 5 Best Practices for Better SaaS User Onboarding — ProductLed


### Продуктовый тур как пластырь для плохого UX: вредит импульсу  ·  [07:28](https://youtu.be/9kM-UMFIlms?t=448)
**Мысль:** Тур, объясняющий кнопки без «зачем», прерывает моментум и часто признак отсутствия стратегии онбординга.

> Now, I have to warn you, be careful using product bumpers as a band-aid for bad user experience. One of my biggest pet peeves of all time with product tours is that it's often used to point out all the bells and whistles of an app. They tell you what the button or feature does. Click here to do X, and not explain why they are important to helping users achieve their desired outcome. When they're added on as an afterthought, product tours can do more damage by disrupting the momentum for users rather than getting them excited to use a product. Regrettably, they have become deeply associated with user onboarding to the point that many companies believe that the way to improve onboarding is to just add a product tour. This is flat out wrong. Ironically, it's often a sign that someone just slapped on onboarding experience without much talk or strategy.

**Для Напарника:** Не делать обучающий тур по экранам; вести к первому действию и пояснять по ходу.

**Мысль:** Смысловые токены (background primary, border subtle) отделяют от сырых цветов (blue 300), чтобы потом не делать огромный рефакторинг при втором бренде или продукте; в остальном система должна быть минимальной.

> It's pretty easy to go overboard with variables in figma and that's why my default advice has been to build the smallest possible system and to not worry or spend too much time architecting for a future that you might not even know if it's going to happen or not but after talking with designer Advocate Louis oros I realized there's actually one exception to that rule even if you're not sure if you're going to have multiple brands or multiple products in the future it still makes sense to separate your primitive variables from your semantic variables and put them each in their own library from day one so in one file you'll have your color foundations these are your blue 300s your gray 400s and then in a separate file maybe it's your style guide you'll have your border subtle and your background primary and things like that it's not really that much more work than keeping these in the same file and just Des scoping your Primitives and this way you have a much more flexible foundation and you can make sure that you're not going to wake up one day a year from now or something like that and realize that you have this massive refactor on your hands.

**Для Напарника:** Цвета Напарника в oklch лучше держать как сырые значения плюс смысловые токены (muted-foreground и т.д.), как уже делает SKY record; не использовать сырые значения в компонентах.

product so if you were to make that product what would you do you would exactly what you just said you would make a couple of sketches you'd sketch right whether that's sketching in code or sketching in figma and then you would show it to a bunch You' schedule a bunch of conversations you show it to a bunch of people you get feedback on it and then you iterate and you would do that a bunch of times until six times and you would go I think our accordion is ready for prime time you know let's get let's get 30 people in here to use it not 3,000 not 30,000 you know let's get 30 people in here to see if you know see to kick the tires on this the problem is that too many people are looking at systems like Polaris and material and they're letting that shape what their output should be but these are massive systems that have been refined over years but if you treat your system like a product then you're not going to build this V one that is robust and comprehensive it would be the equivalent of launching a new e-commerce startup and looking at Shopify as the measuring stick for what you have to build to launch instead we need to find that minimum viable system to launch with and we're going to do it by following a pretty familiar product process you're going to talk to people do user interviews get feedback on early Concepts and pattern matching and maybe we're going to end up launching with something that's pretty small maybe it's even one set of buttons.

# 🎬 7 UI/UX mistakes that SCREAM you’re a beginner — Kole Jain


### Пропуски в пользовательском потоке: поиск, пропуск, скрытые состояния  ·  [00:23](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=23)
**Мысль:** Экран выбора аллергий без поиска и без «пропустить» упускает случаи. Наброски на бумаге ловят дыры, а чаще всего забывают навигацию, скрытые состояния и динамику.

> Number one, user flow. Let's start with something that quietly ruins your UX, user flow. Here's our allergy screen with six preset options, but no search bar if someone's allergic to something else. We also don't have a skip button either. What if the user doesn't have any allergies? These are things you will miss if you don't plan out your flow, but users, they'll feel it instantly. So, even something as simple as sketching boxes on paper helps you catch major gaps early on. And it's not just all about big stuff. Little touches like a filter icon on the search bar or a save button up top go a long way. The most commonly missed elements, navigation links, hidden states, and dynamic interactions like button hovers or micro interactions.

**Для Напарника:** В онбординге Напарника у каждого вопроса должен быть мягкий пропуск («потом»), чтобы никого не загонять.


### Консистентность: единый радиус, одинаковые кнопки, стили и переменные  ·  [02:47](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=167)
**Мысль:** Разные радиусы и одинаковые по смыслу, но по-разному оформленные кнопки выглядят любительски. Помогают стили цвета, переменные размеров и компоненты.

> This one kills me since it's just so easy to fix. You'll notice throughout our design, we have all sorts of different corner radiuses and components like this back and skip button that are exactly the same but designed differently, which just makes the design feel amateur. Instead, let's make a few changes. First, we'll make the two search bars identical other than the prompting text. We'll set the corner radius of all smaller components to be 10 pixels. This is an important one that most designers miss. And of course, we'll update the skip button and back button to match in size, corner radius, and style. Being consistent means using styles for colors, variables for measurements, and components for UI elements to really bring your design together.

**Для Напарника:** Единые радиусы и размеры кнопок «Назад»/«Пропустить» во всех экранах; токены вместо магических чисел.



# 🎬 8 Point Grid system - Improve your UI designs (Figma file included) — UX Tshili


### Джуниоры гадают размеры; осознанность каждого размера отличает профессионала  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/FhGdLUW_8S0?t=22)
**Мысль:** Сетка из 8 точек даёт единую структуру и сохраняет вид после передачи разработчикам.

> I have realized that there is a common theme in people who are more Junior by just getting started in their design career they are not using grids everyone is just guessing they're spacing the size of the elements and the text that every size has to be intentional and this is where the eight point grid system comes in the a point grid is a layout system used in UI design to create consistent visual structure and hierarchy for your designs this ensures that your work is consistent and aligned use the eight point grid system as a guideline to determine the size of elements on your screen and the spacing between them grids allow you to achieve better looking designs faster and they also ensure that your designs will look as polished once you've handed them over to.

**Для Напарника:** Каждое число отступа в компоненте Напарника должно иметь обоснование в шкале.


### Главный цвет зарезервирован для CTA; проверяй экраны вместе, а не по одному  ·  [06:46](https://youtu.be/FhGdLUW_8S0?t=406)
**Мысль:** Слишком много оранжевого размывает акцент, тогда как оставленный на кнопке действия он работает. Цветовая картина оценивается по связке экранов.

> There is way too much orange on this page and it should be reserved for the most important areas which is the call to action button the add button and that's what I've done I created a color palette around the orange so I've got some harmonious colors here and the green which is a complementary color yes this page looks a bit more green however when we go into the other page where we've got the bigger orange buttons that's where you see more of the orange when you're designing it's not always screen by screen you're looking at how all the screens work together.

**Для Напарника:** Тёплый акцент только на кнопке старта и находках; проверять Дом, Фокус и Мир подряд.



# 🎬 A Beginner’s Guide to CRO: How to Get Started Ft. Carla Quizon — Experiment Nation


### CRO — это не смена цвета кнопки, а поиск трения с точки зрения пользователя  ·  [01:38](https://youtu.be/SDoTB2YINE4?t=98)
**Мысль:** Оптимизация без исследования — это ярлыки; суть в выявлении барьеров и фрикции у клиента.

> Cro is often mistaken it's just a b testing experimenting with landing pages directly changing the conversion page or the most common would be changing the ca CTA buttons color or text yes there are tactics we can try but it's not just that cro is far more complex and beautiful than that believe it or not when conversing during a strategy alignment or a performance review I've heard people say a lot of things about their understanding of cro as you can see right there those are some of the common insights they are all more focused on providing shortcuts for their users that benefit their business without fully understanding why and what the problem is well their tactics may not be entirely wrong the core issue is that the approach to optimization is not grounded on Research yes I agree that we want users to perform a specific action to bring us business but cro is really concerned with determining the blockers and friction points from the perspective of your users and customers and we try to address it these are the things that impede businesses from getting that sale and nurturing a relationship with customers.

**Для Напарника:** Любые правки онбординга и Фокуса начинать с наблюдения за реальным пользователем, а не с догадок о цвете кнопки.


### Пройди продукт свежим взглядом и зафиксируй боли, которых команда не видит  ·  [07:04](https://youtu.be/SDoTB2YINE4?t=424)
**Мысль:** Клиент не замечает собственных сложных и избыточных шагов, пока их не визуализировать.

> First thing I like to do is experience the business in their website without much previous information about it I come in with a fresh perspective a fresh pair of eyes I will try to experience the whole thing with usually just the knowledge of what they do or what their safe the service they provide this way I can also see if there's something in the process that just doesn't make sense to me as a user and probably there are other users as well this will allow me to make sense of the data I will be seeing when I check their data points later on I will also try to determine what are the selling points of the company from my perspective as a potential buyer I have to be careful though because I want to avoid bias as much as possible I just need to make sure that I take note of the pain points they have because more often than not our clients are not aware of it until it's explicitly pointed out take for example the screenshot I have there it's there an e-commerce company that didn't realize how long and redundant their process is until it was visualized.

**Для Напарника:** Регулярно проходить путь «первый визит → первая сессия» как новичок, записывая моменты, где неясно.


### Не лепи заплатки: спроси пользователей и бенчмаркни конкурентов критически  ·  [09:40](https://youtu.be/SDoTB2YINE4?t=580)
**Мысль:** Ограничиваясь интуицией, получаешь пластыри; опрос и юзабилити-тест дают ответ; копируя конкурента, не знаешь, работает ли у него.

> My fourth step is to spend time researching don't just throw well at this it just doesn't work like that sure we've determined the initial friction points and opportunities but if we limit ourselves to just lose information we increase the chances of just doing Band-Aid Solutions sometimes the answer is just there if we ask our users so we do surveys or user testing sometimes we just get blinded by beautiful layouts so we forget to design things to allow the brain to make connections and shortcuts so that's where heuristics come from often we're missing out on opportunities just because we wanted to copy the competition doing competitor benchmarking the most important question we need to ask is how we can improve on what they are doing why because we don't really know if what they're doing is actually working so we want to improve on that.

**Для Напарника:** Перед переработкой экрана Дом задать 3–5 пользователям с СДВГ один вопрос, а конкурентов (Finch, Forest) не копировать вслепую.


### Перед A/B-тестом посчитай MDE, длительность и выборку  ·  [10:35](https://youtu.be/SDoTB2YINE4?t=635)
**Мысль:** Без предварительных расчётов можно запустить недостоверный эксперимент и потратить ресурсы впустую.

> Help decide if experimentation is possible and how you should design it in last year's conference this was one of my key takeaways from Haley Carpenter so be mindful to be mindful of your minimal minimum detectable effect test duration and sample size there are pre-test calculations or a sample size calculator available online just check but this step is extremely helpful to stop you from doing experiment statement that may not be really trustworthy and fleshing out resources that could have been allocated elsewhere.

**Для Напарника:** При малом трафике не гонять A/B-тесты; лучше качественные интервью.



# 🎬 A History and Future of AR and VR | Amber Case | AR in Action — AR in ACTION


### Метаболизм принятия: новые функции вводить медленно, по одной  ·  [08:00](https://youtu.be/WTiakRbUF3g?t=480)
**Мысль:** Люди принимают технологию с задержкой; много функций сразу вызывает фокус на самой пугающей и отторжение.

> Technology should consider social norms there's a metabolism rate at which humans can adopt and adapt to technology when elevators first came out we had to artificially slow them down because they were too fast and people complained about their eardrums feeling horrible they were also fearful that like time and space would be flattened forever and that you would die by being accelerated through a train of course we know that's not true but it still takes a long time when phones came out when basic feature phones started to come out with cameras in them that was a big deal I mean if you remember 15 years ago it was three years of oh my gosh our privacy is dead and everyone's going to take pictures of me when I walk down the street and as you know now our camera phones are just used for taking pictures of food to share on social media and things like that but the idea is that it takes a while and if we release a feature every you know few semesters or every few years we'll have the metabolism rate to see whether people accept or reject the technology things like Google Glass didn't build on anything before them and so they were in instantaneously rejected or feared because with too many features released at the same time people will just focus on the scariest one.

**Для Напарника:** Новые ориентиры и механики Острова вводить по одной (progressive disclosure), не выкладывать всё в день первого запуска.



# 🎬 A Redesign Strategy That Works — Manifesto


### Редизайн: сначала структурные изменения по частям, потом визуальный рефреш разом  ·  [05:22](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=322)
**Мысль:** Разделили проблемы на структурные и визуальные: структуру выкатывали инкрементально в старом стиле, чтобы избежать «Франкенштейна», а визуальный рефреш одним релизом без смены рабочих процессов.

> So when I was approaching the execution of a redesign my first instinct was to break it up I was as a product manager coming at this the same way I would come that building a new feature where we would start with an MVP we would a be test it and it Aurra iterate our way to an optimized end state in fact I could not imagine and the alternative which would be you know going heads down for a month working on new designs until this one big reveal moment where we may find that users absolutely hate it but our design team had very different instincts they recognized that the redesign is more than the sum of its parts and they couldn't imagine launching it piecemeal they did not want to put users through this Frankenstein experience where parts of the new style would exist alongside remnants of the old design they just could not stomach it so we wondered if there was a way to get the best of both worlds in order to figure that out we really took a closer look at the specific user pain points that we were trying to solve and we recognized from there that there are actually two sets of issues there are some structural issues things like broken navigation and a lack of hierarchy and then there were also visual issues which were things like outdated button styles and a drab color scheme across all of our platforms making this distinction allowed us to see that we could incrementally launch structural improvements in the old visual style without having to put users through that Frankenstein experience and then once we had made all the structural changes we could launch a visual refresh in one Big Bang but we'd be limiting the risk of user backlash because we wouldn't be forcing users to change their entire workflows overnight.

**Для Напарника:** Не менять сразу и структуру, и вид Напарника; новые ориентиры/фон выкатывать по одному.


### Запускать первыми части с самыми большими вопросами  ·  [08:44](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=524)
**Мысль:** Если часть сильно отличается или основана на непроверенных допущениях, её выпускают раньше ради инсайтов; ответы на главные вопросы приходят до крупных решений на ложных допущениях.

> Knew what these independent parts were we then had to figure out which ones we wanted to launch first so we considered what we could learn from each into that in Keith if it was a huge departure from the status quo or if it was the design based on assumptions that we weren't able to test with a prototype or get user feedback on then we knew we had a lot to learn from actually launching it and we wanted to optimize for getting those insights early on so that we could course correct our way into a better end state over time so going back to all of the different parts that we launched these two were the biggest learning opportunities for us they changed the entire navigation of the web product so we tested these first two to validate our assumptions early I've said this a couple of times now but this is really the major advantage of an incremental approach you get to answer your biggest open questions before making big decisions based on what may be false assumptions and it really mitigates the risk of major catastrophe down the road.

**Для Напарника:** Рискованные идеи Напарника (новая механика) выпускать первыми малым куском и проверять.


### Мелкие релизы дают импульс и доверие  ·  [09:50](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=590)
**Мысль:** Вместо месяцев в закрытой комнате частые выпуски приносят обратную связь, радость команды и доверие организации; Big Bang без видимого прогресса деморализует.

> But this isn't the only advantage you also when you're taking an incremental approach you get to build momentum along the way so you're not holed up in a room for months with the Beautiful Mind thing happening you can actually ship something get feedback on it and generate some excitement and garner that trust with the rest of the organization so for example that top our navigation piece that you saw that was one of our first launches and that was a huge success which really excited the team and helped the company to rally around the project in general so here's this is just a stuff internal staff announcement when we when we recognize the win and we're getting comments and love from across the company which felt really good and allowed us to build momentum in the right direction had we bundled this top our change in with us several others and waited for sort of a BIGBANG launch at the end we would have gone months without seeing any signs of progress and that can be really demoralizing and erode that trust.

**Для Напарника:** Работать малыми PR с видимыми результатами (как в §7 CLAUDE.md), не копить правки.


### Провал боковой панели: независимый запуск позволил откатить  ·  [10:52](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=652)
**Мысль:** Несмотря на прототип, догфудинг и бету, запуск вызвал шквал недовольства, вдвое больше тикетов и провал A/B; независимость изменения позволила быстро откатить и не провалить весь проект.

> Also hit some road bumps on the other end of the spectrum was a feature a new sidebar navigation which is something that we prototyped and we dog food it internally and we beta tested with customers but we still didn't anticipate what would happen at launch and it was pretty big bummer there was user backlash that ensued almost immediately you can see here that there was a lot of outrage on Twitter we got twice as many support tickets as we would normally get and the a/b test failed so this felt pretty terrible at the time but looking back now we're really glad that we were able to recognize this loss upfront because we had launched it as an independent piece and we were able to see exactly what the problem was and roll back the change that was causing all of this negative feedback had we bundled this in with everything else in one big redesign we wouldn't have been able to fix the problem as easily and it would have made the entire project look like a failure.

**Для Напарника:** Каждое крупное изменение Напарника выкатывать отдельно и обратимо.


### Видеть убывающую отдачу и вовремя переключаться  ·  [11:59](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=719)
**Мысль:** Раз видно влияние каждого куска, понятно, когда изменения становятся мельче и пора идти к более значимой работе.

> Advantage of this approach is really knowing when to move on so because you can see the impact of each individual piece you can recognize when you're starting to hit diminishing returns so as we as the launches went further and further down the line the changes were more subtle and the impacts were more subtle so we knew that it was time to move on to higher impact work.

**Для Напарника:** Полировка Дома/Фокуса имеет предел: фиксировать момент убывающей отдачи, как «done is better than perfect».


### Готово лучше идеального: после 75% списка отдача падает  ·  [14:45](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=885)
**Мысль:** Настройки в глубине приложения не стоило доводить до совершенства; решили выпускать.

> But as we got through about 75% of that list we realized that we were hitting diminishing returns we were working on settings dialogs that were hidden deep and corners of the app so we knew that we didn't really want to chase these diminishing returns into perfection and we agreed that done was better than perfect and it was time to launch.

**Для Напарника:** Не полировать глубокие экраны ценой выпуска; приоритет по влиянию.



# 🎬 Aarron Walter — unmatchedstylereview


### Редизайн как длинный шахматный матч: маленькие шаги за месяцы, а не переворот за ночь  ·  [07:35](https://youtu.be/ZCcMIchXHP0?t=455)
**Мысль:** Изменения вводят постепенно (смена шрифта заголовков, организации), чтобы не пугать людей и не терять привычный поток действий.

> Redesign process has been it's a long chess match so we have hundreds of thousands of people using the app and you can't just like flip it on its head overnight i know that some people felt like we flipped it on its head but all we really did just a few weeks ago was we changed the colors that was that was basically it and i know that seems dramatic but we started last year about seven months ago making small shifts knowing that where we were going was where we are today we're we're moving in that direction and so let's make a change over here to the interface that people might notice they might not we switched from trebuchet for headings to helvetica knowing that the product site was going to be very helvetica based and then we made you know a few organizational changes and stuff so it's like make this move over here so you can make this move to make that move and what you can do is you know that euphemism that you can boil a frog in a pot of water if you put them in while the water's cold and just so slow that's exactly what you're doing it's interesting it's like designing a forest yeah it i mean it really is that's that's the idea because we want we know that change can be a freaky thing for people and that doesn't mean that we're not going to change because although people might not like the idea of change they probably would not like us if we did not change they would they would become disenchanted yes so we have to change but we'll do that in a slow graduate way that helps people keep on doing what they're doing right.

**Для Напарника:** Менять AppBackdrop, навигацию и ключевые экраны мелкими итерациями; людям с СДВГ резкое изменение привычного интерфейса вредно.



# 🎬 ADHD and Focus? Here's What Works — Kati Morton


### Point of performance: подсказка ровно там, где срываешься  ·  [00:25](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=25)
**Мысль:** Напоминания и инструменты нужно ставить в точку, где человек отвлекается или перегружается (чеклист на двери гаража).

> This is where the concept of point of performance becomes invaluable point of performance means setting up reminders or tools in the exact spot where you're likely to get distracted or overwhelmed an example of this would be like posting a checklist on the garage door of things that you need to bring to work each day that ensures that you remember everything before leaving by using this technique you're giving yourself tools that help you stay focused exactly when distractions are most likely to occur.

**Для Напарника:** Подсказка Напарника в момент застревания внутри Фокуса, а не заранее в настройках.


### Раздели и победи: пример тренировки из пяти микрошагов, каждый даёт чувство выполненного  ·  [04:52](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=292)
**Мысль:** Каждый завершённый мелкий пункт даёт ощущение достижения и подпитывает мотивацию; отвлекающий пункт (плейлист) лучше решить заранее.

> Strategy number three divide and conquer for those of us with ADHD breaking tasks down into smaller bite-sized steps is one of the most effective ways to stay motivated and productive instead of looking at a giant task that feels overwhelming divide and conquer it by turning it into a series of small achievable steps for example if you want to work out your list could look like number one I've got to find my headphones number two I got to put my workout clothes on number three I'll pick my playlist now this one could be tricky and we could get distracted so maybe pick that out ahead of time but number four put your shoes on number five start your workout as you finish each one of those items you'll feel a sense of accomplishment which helps keep you motivated.

**Для Напарника:** Пример-шаблон для breakdown (найти наушники, надеть форму, выбрать плейлист заранее) и маленькая награда за каждый шаг.



# 🎬 ADHD and Losing Track of Time: Why Your Day Disappears + What Helps — GenX ADHD


### Capture space: одно постоянное место для идей и задач  ·  [01:53](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=113)
**Мысль:** Структура против дрейфа — место, куда всегда записывается важное; оно не должно быть изысканным, главное — доступное и всегда одно.

> So, how do we handle drift? By having a plan. We need to give ourselves structure. Now, not necessarily like work level structure, but we do need to give ourselves structure. So, this is where the capture space is introduced. So, this is a designated place where you can write down your ideas, your tasks, your to-do's, anything like that's important. You should ideally have two, one for home or one for work. And it does not need to be anything fancy. You don't need to go buy a new planner or anything like that. It needs to be accessible and you always need to the same place.

**Для Напарника:** Быстрый «поймать мысль» в Напарнике: отдельное поле в Доме, чтобы идея не уводила из сессии.



# 🎬 ADHD and Time-Blocking: How does it work?! — Evie Ran


### Записанная задача менее абстрактна, а неоднозначность особенно мучительна при СДВГ  ·  [00:44](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=44)
**Мысль:** Видимый список снижает неопределённость и превращает задачи в конкретные вещи, которые легче выполнить.

> Well for two main reasons first is that you get to visualize your tasks when you have your things written down in front of you they are less abstract they're less ambiguous and just about anybody struggles with ambiguity but for ADHD people this is particularly frustrating so if you can reduce the ambiguity and increase the objective factual real things in front of you such as your tasks then you increase your chances to execute them.

**Для Напарника:** Шаг в Фокусе формулировать конкретно и глагольно, без двусмысленности («открыть файл», а не «заняться отчётом»).


### Планируй день утром 10-15 минут, а не надейся на настроение  ·  [05:25](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=325)
**Мысль:** Дневной план точнее долгосрочного и даёт осознанный старт вместо автопилота и надежды на настроение.

> For the daily task sit down in the morning and think about what would you like to do today what would you like to achieve today what would you like this day to look like and then make the list because first of all this is a lot more accurate than trying to plan it in advance but second of all this is how you start the day in a lot more intentional way because you're spending those 10 15 minutes literally designing your day versus running on autopilot hoping that the right mood will hit you at the right time and you achieve your stuff like honestly I've never seen a sustainable productive technique that relies on hope so okay you may be one of those extreme cases that can achieve that but if you're one of the 99% of the population on this Earth don't rely on Hope just sit down think about yourself think about your goals think about your responsibilities write it down and then go ahead and kick ass.

**Для Напарника:** Утренний ритуал на Доме: напарник предлагает один-два шага на сегодня, без обязательного плана (ноль - тоже нормально).


### Цветовая градация задач: обязательства, дела, «было бы славно», радость  ·  [08:21](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=501)
**Мысль:** Разделение по связи с другим человеком и жёсткости времени снимает вопрос что можно двигать.

> Let's go into my color grading so I use four categories red was for responsibilities yellow for chores I'm going to explain the difference green was for it would be nice if I could and then purple was for things that make me happy now first of all what is the difference between category red responsibilities and category yellow chores responsibilities in red are things that are somehow connected to another human being your appointments at the dentist your work meeting even your appointments and dates with your spouse those are things that you have committed to and they are involving another person you must do them otherwise you're going to lose money respect relationships you're going to lose something because this other person is somehow involved this is red yellow on the other side is chores that you better do but nobody will come and knock on your door if you don't do it for example going to the groceries that's a chore cleaning your house that's a chore paying your rent is also a chore as long as you don't overdo your Rend day but whether you're going to do it on Monday or Tuesday usually doesn't matter whether you're going to do it in the morning or in the evening doesn't matter chores that are not exactly specified in terms of time in another person yellow.

**Для Напарника:** Тип шага (обязательство с другим человеком или свободный) можно различать мягко в очереди шагов и подбирать порядок.


### Тяжёлое утром: утро/день/вечер как фильтр, а не расписание  ·  [13:20](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=800)
**Мысль:** Когнитивно тяжёлое, оставленное на поздний час, чаще не делается; грубые части дня задают направление, не сковывая.

### Потолок сложности: система сложнее рабочей памяти обречена, как 13 чисел вместо 3  ·  [03:01](https://youtu.be/d3bjF_8citY?t=181)

> The next piece of where trying to follow time management strategies that are really more aligned for neurotypical people often fails business owners with ADHD is that they kind of assume a level of mental RAM that you might not have yet. So here's what I want to offer. Everyone has everyone neurotypical ADHD, whatever your brain is, has sort of a complexity ceiling of how many sort of like open tabs they can keep open in their brain where everything sort of like makes sense and you can maintain it all at one spot. >> >> What I would offer is that often it's the case for people with ADHD that they might have a smaller sort of working memory than others. That's okay. There's nothing there's nothing wrong with that. But it becomes a challenge whenever you try to adhere to a system that is more complex than sort of like your brain can hold in one spot at one point. So like let's give an example. Let's give an example of like a filing system. where this goes sideways on people is they say, "Okay, I'm going to have this brand new system for my files and it's going to have like all of these colored tabs and it's going to be like 20 different dividers and it's going to be so wonderful and it's wonderful on day number one." But if that system that you've set up is at least at the moment more complex than your ability to adhere to it, then it's going to fall short. And this often happens with sort of time management systems as well is that people will get hyper granular and create systems that have a level of granularity that their brain might have challenges sort of staying on top of and perceiving at a particular time. So what I mean by hyperranularity and perceiving at a particular time, it's no different than if I said to you, hey, I want you to remember these three numbers. I'm going to tell you three numbers today and you're going to remember them tomorrow. You could probably do that pretty easily. But if I say, hey, I'm going to give you 13 numbers. I'm going to see them once and you're going to remember them and tell me them tomorrow. You might struggle. Now, does that mean that you're good or bad at memory? No. It just means that you need to ensure that any sort of system that you set up is within your it's sort of within your capacity to execute. And often what leads business owners with ADHD to go through these patterns of like adopting a system and failing and adopting a system and failing over and over again while it might work for others is that you're trying to adopt a system that sort of requires and assumes a level of sort of RAM, a level of working memory that you might not yet have.

**Для Напарника:** Дом не должен превращаться в сложную систему: минимум сущностей (один шаг, один таймер, остров); не добавлять уровни и теги.

> The next thing that keeps business owners with ADHD stuck when it comes to time management is that often they have structured their business in a way that inadvertently reinforces time blindness. So, maybe you've noticed this, maybe you've noticed this in your life. Have you noticed that maybe when you were in school, maybe you didn't feel that some of these challenges were as big as they are now? Or maybe when you were in a corporate environment, it's very common that somebody's in a corporate environment and then when they're out on their own, it's very different. And often what they are missing is that they've inadvertently designed the business where they're sort of just like jumping from one project to another. You know what I mean? like it's like one meeting to another to another to another, which introduces switching costs, which might feel like all time starts to blur and blend together. Particularly for business owners, if you're at a stage where you haven't really got out of a firefighting stage, that's a particular time where time starts to blur together, where we're just jumping from one thing to the other. What I would offer is that, you know, if you could think of time awareness as something that we can strengthen in our brain through working it, kind of like working out at the gym, we strengthen it working it, or it can atrophy by not using it. Very common pattern that happens with business owners with ADHD is without sort of common structured sort of markers that allow us to see time, time starts to blur. the more time blurs, the weaker our time awareness gets, the weaker our time awareness gets, the more difficult it is to use any sort of time management system because you're not.

# 🎬 Alex Hormozi's Advice on Landing Pages — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Один сплит-тест в неделю — фокус и привычка тестировать  ·  [02:47](https://youtu.be/CNRiNz0YYlM?t=167)

> Every week what we do is we have our marketing meeting and we do one split test. Just one. And that's because everyone wants to test a 100 things. But what it does is it forces us to be very focused on the one thing that we think is going to drive the highest result for us. This is the biggest thing that I feel holds a lot of brands back from doing more conversion optimization and it's that there's so many different things they want to test and they just don't know where to start. As Alex suggests, just make it simple. Just focus on a couple things at a time and just focus in getting in the habit of testing and becoming consistent in the first place.

### Результаты тестов переносятся: победившая идея работает в рекламе, UX и фичах  ·  [09:29](https://youtu.be/CNRiNz0YYlM?t=569)
**Мысль:** Каждый выигрышный или проигрышный тест — данные о том, что резонирует с аудиторией; их можно применять к рекламе, ценностному предложению и продукту. Пример: карусель с лендинга подняла CTR рекламы.

> The biggest thing it helps with, which is often overlooked, is the data collection portion. When you do CRO and you see tests that win or lose, you're quite literally collecting data on what resonates with your audience and what doesn't. The beautiful thing about that is that insight from your winning test is literally transferable to all other aspects of your business. You can start injecting that into your advertising material, your value proposition, the UX of your product, coming up with new features or ideas, it's transferable everywhere. And on the flip side, if you have data on test at lost, you can then stray away from using that in your marketing efforts. The best example I can give is influence hunter. We took that same concept of the carousel image and we actually applied it to their ads. And we saw a massive increase in the click-through rate of their ads.

**Для Напарника:** То, что сработало на лендинге (формулировки, образы), переносить в онбординг и реплики кота, фиксируя выводы.



# 🎬 Alex Hormozi's Landing Page Strategy for 2026 — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Один сплит-тест в неделю; при малом трафике — крупные изменения по лучшим практикам  ·  [12:05](https://youtu.be/zA0B-VwOPn4?t=725)
**Мысль:** Фокус на одном тесте заставляет выбрать самое значимое; A/B требует 40–50 тыс. посетителей в месяц, иначе нет статзначимости.

> Completely over complicate it, right? They think of hundreds of different things that they can test and they never end up knowing where to start. Alex's rule is super simple. It's just test one thing per week. The process is simple. Every week we have our marketing meeting and we do one split test. Just one. All right? And that's because everyone wants to test 100 things. But what it does is it forces us to be very focused on the one thing that we think is going to drive the highest result for us. Now you have to understand that this depends heavily on your traffic. Presumably somebody like Alex is driving, you know, tens of thousands if not hundreds of thousands of visitors to his landing pages across his portfolio every single month. He actually has sufficient traffic levels in order to do AB testing. Typically our minimum is at least 40 to 50,000 visitors per month. But let's say you only have 10,000 or 20,000 visitors per month or even lower than that. You're not going to be able to run traditional AB tests because you won't be able to reach statistical significance, which is kind of like a fancy way of saying is the test you did chance or not. So what we personally do for clients with lower traffic levels is we will make larger scale changes like completely revamping their landing page based on best practices on things that we've already AB tested with our higher traffic clients that get, you know, hundreds of thousands of visitors to millions of visitors. This is all in the absence of not having sufficient data to actually AB test.

**Для Напарника:** У маленького Напарника нет трафика для A/B: делать крупные обоснованные изменения и проверять на живых людях, а не на метриках.

# 🎬 Animating a Head Turn : The Important Exercise Everyone Forgets! — BaM Animation


### Планирование и тамбнейлы: идея движения, замах для отскока  ·  [02:36](https://youtu.be/3Mik2Gcrq-g?t=156)
**Мысль:** Планирование — основа качественного исполнения: сначала черновые рисунки, затем поза-замах (anticipation), добавляющая подскок, и преувеличенная реакция в нужном кадре.

> They are two things that are at odds with each other. But in animation and other art forms where there can be objective quality, planning is a fundamental component of good execution. So, we need to come up with an idea for the motion we want and how we're going to get there. Like, there are so many options for how she could turn her head. For example, in a very mechanical sense, she could just move her head from A to B without emotion or feeling. She could ease super slowly at first and then snap. Or we could have her sort of jump up and notice something and then kind of snap turn. I kind of like the last one. So, let's try to figure out how to convert this motion into drawings. I'm going to do an initial series of exploratory drawings where I'm just going to try to figure out how it's going to work. This is called thumbnailing, and it's how animators eloos plan the acting. We need to go from here to here. And I want her to jump up a little bit. So, I'll add a drawing in here and then add some space on the timeline before she goes to pose B. This new drawing here is going to be the frame where her eyes are kind of wide and it'll be just a little bit exaggerated. As you can kind of see, Brent is drawing on top of other drawings so that he can keep track of things. This method of drawing over top of barely visible layers is called potato skinning. Well, it's not actually called that, but if you do want to correct me in the comments, go for it so that we can get that engagement up. Okay, let's add two more drawings to this. The first one will be the anticipation pose where she actually moves down first before coming up so that we get sort of a bounce up motion.

**Для Напарника:** Реакция кота на находку: сначала лёгкое приседание, потом подъём; продумать идею движения до CSS-реализации.

**Мысль:** Сразу много изменений ломают исходный стиль картинки; одна правка за запрос сохраняет стиль.

> One mistake I'm seeing a lot of people make when they attempt this is they ask Chachi PT a bit too much in their request. Let me show you guys exactly what I mean by this. It's probably easier with an example. I have this other illustration here and this is another illustration that my fiance made. This is of our dog Luna. So again, this is handdrawn. So, I'm just going to drag it in here and say, can you modify it so that it's a gray cat and the background is actually a coffee shop and instead of a duck, it's a pizza and instead of the iced tea, it is a hot cup of coffee and I want the table to be blue instead of green. If you ask it to do all of these things at once, the problem is it actually can follow these instructions. But what I've noticed is it starts completely just not following the base style of the image you give it. And so that is the biggest problem that I've seen people do. And then when they see this, they obviously get frustrated and they're like, "ChachiPT doesn't work." The secret is to actually chunk this into individual requests and go very slowly so that Chachi PT has a good chance of actually just maintaining the original base style.

**Для Напарника:** Генерация иллюстраций для Напарника: по одной правке на запрос от эталонного кота.

Kind of just pick a name knowing full well that if I want in the future, I could easily change it. Even subscription monster, I have a suspicion that I might end up pivoting into something more than subscriptions. Like it might just be a more general business financial tool and I'm completely fine changing the name, changing the mascot if needed. I actually did that for some of my original products. A lot of people don't know this. Mogul, which is my personal CRM app that I have, was actually originally called Karen. And the reason I did that was it was named after the computer from Spongebob, which is the smart computer. And Mogul was supposed to be this very smart networking tool to help you manage all your contacts. Karen ended up not being a great name for an app. So I actually changed it to Mogul, which I think is a lot more fitting, but it was named Karen for probably about a year straight. Ellie actually had the name Kronos, which had to do with time because this is a time management tool. So it was named Kronos for probably like a month until I decided to change it, thankfully. The point is, I move really quickly with the names and I try not to let it get in the way and I'm very, very comfortable changing it if needed. And that's my advice to everyone. If you're thinking about coming up with a name, definitely try to come up with a good name at first, but don't spend too long on it. I think just pick something, know that it could be a placeholder, and it can totally be changed in the future.

**Для Напарника:** Процессный совет: не блокировать запуск из-за имени кота или бренда.



# 🎬 App Onboarding Best Practices: Why Users Quit Before They Start — Kris Greenwald


### Внутренняя и избыточная нагрузка: избыточная целиком на совести дизайнера  ·  [02:35](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=155)
**Мысль:** Внутренняя сложность задачи неустранима, а избыточная создаётся подачей, и почти любая проблема конверсии формы это избыточная нагрузка.

> Any task we perform carries two kinds of load. It's either intrinsic or extraneous. And this comes from cognitive load theory. Intrinsic load is the difficulty actually inside of the job. So, some of booking a table for a restaurant, they have to specify a date. You cannot design that away. But an extraneous load is everything else. So, difficulty created purely by how it is presented. And this one is entirely your fault. And today, we're going to fix it. Almost every conversion problem in a form is an extraneous load issue.

**Для Напарника:** При проверке экранов Напарника отделять сложность самой задачи человека от сложности интерфейса и убирать вторую.



# 🎬 Best Tools for Focus & ADHD | Dr. John Kruse & Dr. Andrew Huberman — Huberman Lab Clips


### Один список и триаж A/B/C: не вычёркивай шнурки вместо налогов  ·  [03:18](https://youtu.be/hnkkoR-rFIo?t=198)

> Having one Consolidated list because if it's everywhere then it's nowhere and two is the simplest triaging or organizing approach is having the things that are both urgent and important so they have to happen today those get in the a category the things that are important but aren't as urgent are the B category and all the other things are the C and one of The Temptations the people with ADHD have is I need to be productive I you know it's fun to go buy shoelaces and that's on my list so I'll go to buy shoelaces because then I can cross something off my list but I didn't move the car I didn't do my taxes I haven't done my homework you know all the important things remain undone so it's a system for getting done what's really needed to be done.

### Физическая сегрегация соблазна: соцсети только на отдельном старом телефоне  ·  [07:32](https://youtu.be/hnkkoR-rFIo?t=452)
**Мысль:** Усложнение доступа работает лучше силы воли; приложения-блокировки и физические боксы - следующие уровни.

> I've made it a point to put social media on a old phone so those apps are only on that phone I don't even know the number to that phone if I need to post something I aird drop it onto that phone and this has helped tremendously in segregating that activity and limiting it also means that if people send me something which would otherwise direct me to social media it's much more difficult for me to go look it up it's helped tremendously I just passed that on it's one of the things that's really allowed me to restrict my social media time.

**Для Напарника:** Подсказка перед Фокусом «убери телефон подальше»; без жёстких блокировок в самом приложении.



# 🎬 Body Doubling for ADHD: What the Research Actually Shows — FLOWN


### Дело не в усилии, а в среде и структуре: чёткий старт, общий контейнер, люди  ·  [09:16](https://youtu.be/pESS6GpxPq4?t=556)
**Мысль:** Условия вокруг внимания меняют день с фрагментарного на плавный.

> These findings confirm that it's not always about trying harder, it's about our environment and our structure. Body doubling works, at least in part, because it changes the conditions around our attention. There's a clear start point, a shared focus container, and other people working alongside you that create that feel-good focus feeling.

**Для Напарника:** Проектировать условия (чёткий старт, ограниченный контейнер сессии, присутствие), а не призывы «постарайся».



# 🎬 Body Doubling Will Change How You Work Forever — FLOWN


### Три уровня давления: тихая компания, средний (цель и итог), высокий (фасилитатор)  ·  [04:51](https://youtu.be/8F88hppZllg?t=291)
**Мысль:** Регулировать три параметра: взаимодействие, структуру, видимость обязательства; подбирать по сложности задачи и уровню энергии.

> Think of it as a dial from silent companionship on one end to fully structured sessions on the other. You're adjusting three things how much you interact with the other person, how much structure wraps around the session, and how visible your commitment is. So what does that look like in practice. Especially for virtual body doubling. Low pressure camera on. No talking, no set time limit, no check in. You're just there together. Think of it like working in a library. Nobody's watching you. But the quiet presence of others keeps you anchored. You less likely to drift off to your phone or disappear into a YouTube rabbit hole. These are good for admin, repetitive work, creative thinking, or maybe low energy days. You're adding just enough presence to prevent the drift. Medium pressure camera on a clear start time, you state what you're working on either out loud or in the chat. There's a light checking at the end. How did that go? Did you get it done? This is where it starts to feel noticeably different from working alone. You've made a small public commitment. Someone else knows what you're working on, and that's enough social energy to move forward without tipping into stress. This is good for your day to day work tasks you've been avoiding, anything that keeps carrying over on your to do list. And finally, high pressure camera on a professional facilitator sets the pace. You state a clear specific goal in the session is time boxed? Maybe 1 hour or 2 hours. At the end, you report back, did I finish what got in the way? This is the most powerful tier. It works brilliantly for tasks where you're truly stuck, where you need short bursts of activation, or for that project you've been circling around for a week. This is great for high resistant tasks. Deadline crunches, or breaking through task paralysis. Before you sit down to work, ask yourself two questions. How hard is this task and how much energy do I have today? Over time. You learn what type of body works best for you, so you can match the intensity to the task.

**Для Напарника:** Режимы Фокуса: «просто рядом», «скажу цель и отчитаюсь», «жёсткий таймбокс»; перед стартом два вопроса: насколько тяжело и сколько сил.



# 🎬 Bringing Simplicity to Complexity: How to Design at Scale | Jonah Jones | TEDxGlasgow — TEDx Talks


### Прорыв: искать, чем люди похожи, а не различаются (ориентиры вместо названий улиц)  ·  [05:15](https://youtu.be/cgKMuaW-Yw8?t=315)
**Мысль:** В Индии и Японии люди описывают путь ориентирами, а названия улиц - лишь один из видов ориентиров. Использование всех типов ориентиров решило задачу для всего мира.

> But the breakthrough came when we stopped thinking about all the ways in which people were different and started focusing on the ways in which everyone's the same. So, if you ask for directions in India today, people won't give you an address or a street name or any of this stuff. They'll say things like, "It's by the temple." Or "Go past the river." Or "It's next to the school." They use landmarks to help you get around. And the map reflects this. There may be no street names on this map, but you can see that there's lots of landmarks here that the local people use to get around. Now, in Japan, the situation's actually similar. Because of that really complex address system we talked about, people use landmarks instead of street names to get around. And again, you can see this on the map. No street names, loads and loads of local landmarks here. Now, it turns out that it doesn't matter where you go in the world, from Tokyo to Bangalore to Glasgow, if you ask someone on the street how to get around, they'll describe how to do it using landmarks as well as street names. And suddenly, you realize, if you look outside this building here, everything's a landmark. The restaurant in the bottom right is a landmark. The train station is a landmark. The big buildings are landmarks. The river is a landmark. And the street names, they're just another type of landmark. It turns out that Google Maps had been using landmarks for directions all along. It's just that they'd only been using one specific type of landmark. So, this insight, the Maps team went about upgrading the directions to use all the different types of landmarks that are available. Sometimes using street names when they're relevant, other times using something else. So, now in India, when you ask for directions, Google Maps gives you something like this. Continue past KMK Complex. Turn left at Gode Temple. Your destination is on the left opposite the big banyan tree. That's a great landmark, right? So, by focusing on the ways that people were similar, instead of focusing on the ways in which people were different, the Google Maps team were able to help people get around, no matter where they are in the world.

**Для Напарника:** Ориентиры острова как универсальные якоря памяти и прогресса: люди ориентируются по знакомым местам, а не по цифрам.

**Мысль:** Дизайнеры входят в систему через сценарии (регистрация, форма), а не через компоненты; приоритет гайдов оказался выше, чем предполагали.

**Для Напарника:** Документировать для ИИ-сеансов не только компоненты, но и сценарии (первый визит, финал сессии); CLAUDE.md уже идёт этим путём.


### Дизайн-система это продукт: знание не равно использованию  ·  [14:53](https://youtu.be/yPLY-FFf7OY?t=893)
**Мысль:** Нужно знать пользователей системы, оставлять место для итераций, учитывать разную зрелость пользователей; система полезна, только если реально используется.

> We've learned that we need to treat scapa as a living and breathing product and never make the mistake of thinking that one solution will fit everyone more or less like all products right there's no difference between this and a regular product so to make your design system a success you need to know your users and you need to understand what impacts their work the most their needs they will inform your roadmap and be the base for understanding the efforts needed to execute on it and you're not going to get it right the first time trust us you're not so make sure that you leave room for iteration and encourage your users to give feedback that's very important because without your users you don't get the right system and the second thought is that a d design system needs to be relevant to your users to make the most of this you need to understand not all users have the same level of maturity this is something that we learned the hard way as we thought that our designers would just pick this up and be happy with it turns out that it's not as simple some will need more support than others and planning out how to best support the rollout of your design system is equally as important as advocating for it and making people understand that it exists so knowledge is not the same as usage and the design system is only as useful if it as it's as it is and it's only useful if it's actually being used across your whole organization.

**Для Напарника:** Правила Напарника из CLAUDE.md ценны только когда соблюдаются: проверять их в ревью (контраст, toast, хаптика).

# 🎬 Closing the Game Loop in Gamification Design (Part 1 of 4) - Octalysis Prime Sneak Peak — Octalysis Prime by Yu-kai Chou


### Activity loop против game loop: последний шаг должен усиливать первый  ·  [02:37](https://youtu.be/lqoGeieNH7M?t=157)
**Мысль:** Цикл «делай действие снова» заканчивается и остаётся на милости пользователя; настоящий игровой цикл заставляет хотеть вернуться, потому что итог усиливает следующее действие.

> So the first thing I want to explain is the difference between an activity loop and a game loop right a lot of people are not aware of that so earlier if you have been in the community for long enough you'll know we've done we did a food design food hero design challenge where we go in and we design how to make kids eat healthily in China and different countries and so we work with a client and then we released it to the public and a lot of people have good designs and one of the things that we often see for people's designs like even if they're good they have something we called an activity loop most almost all systems have to a collectivity loop right and so we'll see here like oh the stay starts here you know we starts doing step one and then we do step two so you see teacher checks the food journals you get one power card but the blood right and then you do each of these steps and at the end it says eat at least one healthy food immersed in the food journal that's the last step and then goes back to the next day the beginning of the day now this is an activity loop because this is the loop you want people do over and over and over and over again right however it is not what I consider a game loop because the last step doesn't necessarily empower the first step and maybe my definition is different others out there I believe my definition is more useful to creating better designs so bear with me on that usually the last step is I'll complete the whole process right you know and sometimes like finish a module right and then of course to loop back people should start the module another module again right the thing is that the last step does not empower the first step it ends and you're hoping people come back and be the first step right you want people to do the activity but they're not creating some kind of game loop where we're the last that empowers the first step and the whole thing is interesting.

**Для Напарника:** Награда в конце сессии должна усиливать следующий старт: например, рост острова открывает новое место для следующего шага, а не просто закрывает цикл.

> When you do a gamification design you should be very sensitive of when does the experience end and when does you boost right when where are the boots where are the arrows the green arrows are really important in a game patient design I keep telling my clients this the one of the easiest ways to tell if a gamification design is good or not is to look at where they put their boosters or if they even have the Boosters most composed game can design so this is like what I said earlier no boosters right but if they have boosters that's a plus but where they put their blue boosters how do they design for their boosters you can tell so much about how good a game occasion designer is and but again something if it ends sometimes it's okay we work on a client where if you keep doing amazing things you get to interact with a celebrity right and that's the end of it and are an internet celebrity doesn't help you and pout and empower you to do things inside the game maybe it does right that'd be even cooler right if empowering the celebrity makes you even pom more popular in the game that'd be cool but that's the end and that's okay because that's the ultimate goal no one's gonna be upset that they end up interact with that celebrity and so it's okay if it ends but the key question is do you have the green arrows that boost desired actions.
