# 🎬 Core Game Loops — Timothy Cain


### Core loop: самые частые повторяющиеся действия большинства  ·  [01:17](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=77)
**Мысль:** Core loop — набор действий, которым большинство игроков занимается большую часть времени.

> The core game Loop is the set of actions in a game that most players will spend most of their time doing you can think of it as what are the most frequent and repeated actions that players take in your game.

**Для Напарника:** Чётко определить core loop Напарника (выбрать шаг, запустить Фокус, получить отклик) и вкладывать главное внимание в него.


### Как определить core loop: час игры, демо, стример  ·  [05:24](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=324)
**Мысль:** Достаточно часа игры или просмотра стримера, чтобы увидеть, что человек делает чаще всего; у игр без чёткого core loop человек не понимает, что происходит. Демо и vertical slice должны показывать core loop, а не просто красивую картинку.

> Don't have to play an entire game from start to finish to know what its core game Loop is you usually you can pick up a game play it for an hour and you can probably pretty accurately Define its core game Loop if it's a good game and this is what why some games fail I think is because they don't have a well defined core game Loop and you can play for an hour and you're like I still don't know what's going on alternatively you could just play a demo the whole point of a demo is to express the core game Loop by the way if you're if you're making a vertical slice you should also be thinking core game Loop because that's what you're trying to express not just here's a visual example of the game but I've got enough of the core game Loop in there that you understand what the game's going to be like when you play it's not enough vertical slices you just it's not enough for them just to be pretty they have to instantiate the core game Loop a lot of times I've noticed that if you just watch a streamer play the game that's enough for you to figure out the core game Loop they just watch them for an hour and you see what they're doing over and over and over again are they jumping are they sneaking around are they hacking terminals what seems to be the thing that they do the most that's the core game Loop.

**Для Напарника:** Проверка Напарника: за час наблюдения за новым пользователем должно быть очевидно, что он делает чаще всего, и это должно быть приятно.


### Core loop и дизайн-столпы — разные вещи  ·  [06:38](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=398)
**Мысль:** Столпы объясняют, как каждая фича служит замыслу; core loop — те из фич, которые игроки используют чаще всего. Core loop должен касаться всех столпов, остальные фичи могут их не затрагивать.

> Let me make a big distinguish here between core game loops and design pillars I have a whole video on design pillars and I'll link it below they are not the same thing the design pillars are there to explain how every feature that you've selected to be in the game serves those pillars but a core game Loop is you saying okay of all those features that we've put in the game that serve the design pillars these are the ones I expect most of the players to experience most of the time and there's difference there because you may have things that you put in like let's say you put in reactivity that's something I love putting in my game my games reactivity might be a design pillar which means when you put in features your you're going to always ask the question of how does the player what the player does when they interact with this feature come back to them how are they being told hey here's how good you're doing with this thing the core game Loop would be those features that the player does a lot you would expect all of them to have large amounts of reactivity because if this is what the player is doing most of the time it better be supporting that design pillar of reactivity because I've talked about you can occasionally have a feature that doesn't support all the design pillars like you may put something in that's if humor is one of your design pillars you may have something in the game that's not that funny as long as overall the game is funny but for your core game Loop it should probably touch on all your pillars because it's these features are what your the player is doing a lot it should it should re it should have a basis in those design pillars.

**Для Напарника:** Ввести столпы Напарника (тепло, без стыда, дофамин) и проверять, что core loop затрагивает каждый из них.


### Core loop задаёт приоритеты, расписание и оптимизацию  ·  [08:32](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=512)
**Мысль:** Он подсказывает, что делать в первую очередь, что можно выбросить при нехватке времени и где искать баги: частые пути важнее редких.

> Your core game Loop tells your entire team what to focus on because here are the features of this big design document here are the features that are the most important to get correct to get in early to get them correct it will help you it will help the production staff decide what to prioritize because when there are features that have to be done you're like well these are part of the core game Loop they have to be done you could have a fun it's like side quests if you have to drop a side quest because you run out of time that's okay because it's not part of the main story your features help you schedule in the same way this is a great feature it's fun but is it part of the core game Loop and if it's not it can go you may not want it to go but it can go similarly when you get to the end of the game this tells you where to spend a lot of your time optimizing because if core game Loop features like I said it's where most players spend most of their time you want that to be optimized you want a smooth UI experience you want a good frame rate you want to make sure that there are no bugs in that core game Loop because that's what most of your players are going to spend most of their time doing if there's a you know an occasional bug that occurs from some obscure thing that the player can maybe do like maybe if they craft one item at a particular table and give it to a particular NPC and it causes that NPC to go in a bad State you want to fix that but if you had to prioritize that bug against oh yeah if you ever shoot a gun at an NPC that has more than five hit points sometimes the game crashes fix that.

**Для Напарника:** Приоритизировать полировку и баги Дома и Фокуса (основные пути), а не редких экранов.


### Core loop как аргумент против лишних фич и монетизации  ·  [10:36](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=636)
**Мысль:** Когда издатель просит микротранзакции, можно ответить, что в core loop для них нет места; фичи вне core loop легко отложить.

> There's another reason for you to make sure the core game Loop is defined early because you want Publishers or whoever's giving you money to see that core game Loop it is a great way to provide context for answers to your questions when they have questions about it so if for example you have a publisher going well we really like microtransactions a great way to respond to that if you don't want them is like we don't see a role for microtransactions in this game's core game Loop and the reason that's a nice way of phrasing it is either they have to figure out how microtransactions would fit in that core game Loop which they're probably not going to be able to do or they're going to have to ask you to change the core game Loop which you your response would be that's the core game Loop so you want me to change the entire game so we're going to throw away this we're going to throw away these things and because if microtransactions are part of the core game loop we're going to redesign it is a nice defense against some of the things that Publishers will ask you to add but you because if it's not part of the core game Loop you're like I don't see a lot of players doing this and I certainly don't see a lot of players doing this a lot you can often get features suggested features either deprioritized or not considered altogether.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать core loop Напарника как фильтр против запросов вроде стриков, лидербордов, магазинов и тёмных паттернов.


### Чем больше core loop, тем больше расписание и команда  ·  [11:52](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=712)
**Мысль:** Большая вариативность режимов (боевой, диалог, стелс) множит работу; рекомендация держать core loop максимально сфокусированным.

> One last thing about core game Loop and I'm saying this from experience the bigger your core game Loop the longer it takes you to write it down the more features that are part of it the harder that game Loop will be to implement I say this from experience because when I make games that I say I want people to be able to play them all these different ways I want them to talk through it and fight through it and stealth through it and go through primarily with companion support that just added a lot of work and time to your schedule because if that's the core game Loop that you expect most of the players to be doing it's got to be good and of course like I've always said that then translates a big core game Loop will translate into a big schedule which will translate into a big team and a lot of money so it's in your best interest to try to get your core game loop as laser focused as possible but understand that some games will have big core game Loops it's just always in your best interest it's in your best interest it's in your team's best interest for knowing what they should work on and it's in the Publishers best interest for knowing what game they're buying and how you're going to respond to suggestions to change it's in everybody's best interest to get that core game Loop defined as early and as specifically as you can.

**Для Напарника:** Держать core loop Напарника узким: один основной путь вместо множества режимов.



# 🎬 Creating Our Duolingo Characters • Greg Hartman • Duocon 2020 — Duolingo


### Имя персонажа: критерии личности, произносимости и написания  ·  [06:37](https://youtu.be/m-3-D7S0piw?t=397)
**Мысль:** Имя должно отражать характер, легко произноситься на многих языках и писаться одинаково; примеры отклонённых имён (Jun, Pete).

> We figured this would only take like a week or two, until we realized naming is really hard, especially when spanning many languages. We knew we wanted everyone, no matter where in the world they were using Duolingo to refer to our characters by the same name. This proved more difficult than we originally thought. Finding a name that we, one, thought reflected the character's personality. Two, was a name that could be easily pronounced in many languages. And three was spelled similarly, if not identically across all courses. See, this last one is especially important because there are... so there wasn't any confusion around seeing a character's name in your native language versus the language you were learning, which could often appear on the same screen. An example of a name I really liked for this character, but did not fit the criteria was Jun. Jun would have been pronounced as Hoon in Spanish so that name was kind of out. Another example that I liked was Pete, but Pete, if not pronounced exactly right in French means fart. So we had to scrap that name too. So you take those examples and you multiply that by the nine characters we designed then multiply that by the number of languages we accounted for, and your spreadsheet quickly turns from this to...

**Для Напарника:** Имя кота выбирать по критерию простоты произношения и тёплого звучания в русском; проверить на нежелательные созвучия.



# 🎬 Design Systems at Scale // Episode 1: The Heartache of Design at Scale — Dan Mall


### Закон Галла: работающая сложная система вырастает из простой  ·  [00:02](https://youtu.be/-5h05poX9CA?t=2)
**Мысль:** Сложная система, созданная сразу сложной, ломается и не чинится; работающая вырастает из простой.

> I often think of gaul's law John Gall was a pediatrician of all things who said something to the effect that every complex system that works was invariably created from a simple system and that every complex system that was created from scratch as a complex system always breaks and fails and cannot be patched to work.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип разработки Напарника: наращивать остров и механики итерациями, начиная с минимального рабочего ядра.


### Дизайн-система — это курирование, а не изобретение  ·  [15:00](https://youtu.be/-5h05poX9CA?t=900)
**Мысль:** Лучшие системы состоят из скучных компонентов, которые уже строили тысячу раз; задача собрать лучшее, а не создать новое.

> The design system is not about invention it's about curation there's so many different Avenues to kick off a design system initiative that can almost become paralyzing right where do we start where do we put this stuff how do you actually convince your peers how do you convince your managers that a design system is worth it I think the most exciting Design Systems are boring and I don't mean that they create boring products I mean that they are composed of all of the boring components that we've all built a thousand times you have to speak the language of the person that you're talking to this is not the time to create something new but to pull together the best work from across the organization.

**Для Напарника:** Не плодить новые компоненты: сначала искать существующие в components/ и переиспользовать.



# 🎬 Design Systems at Scale // Episode 2:  Selling the Value of Your Design System — Dan Mall


### Показать боль наглядно: распечатанные сайты на стене убедили босса  ·  [02:31](https://youtu.be/XTGBbKjIP7Y?t=151)
**Мысль:** Менеджер распечатал сотни сайтов на досках и показал руководителю, сколько денег тратится зря, и вышел с бюджетом.

> Adventist Church to help them build a design system the web manager there Brent he did this great exercise that I love referencing he what he did was he printed out every website that they made on these little 3x3 in cards and every website that they made that year and he printed out they did a couple hundred and then he posted he pasted those onto mounting boards black mounting boards and then he brought them into his boss's office there's about six or seven mounting boards and he just showed them and he goes this is how much money we're wasting and what he was doing was really just kind of showing like visually literally showing the pain as like we could be doing this a lot more efficiently we're wasting a lot of money and this is why I need a budget to do a design system and he walked out of that room with a budget.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип для Мира: не рассказывать о прогрессе, а показывать его видимо (остров, гряда ориентиров), эффект Пик-Энд.


### Jobs to be done: никто не хочет дрель, все хотят дыру в стене  ·  [07:07](https://youtu.be/XTGBbKjIP7Y?t=427)
**Мысль:** Люди покупают не инструмент, а результат (картину на стене). Продавать надо не систему, а то, что она позволяет получить.

> The common stories about the let's say the jobs to be done framework is that is that when people are trying to buy a drill nobody wants a drill nobody wants a quarter inch drill they want a quarter inch hole right and you take that even further nobody wants a quarter inch hole they want a picture hung on the wall you know and I think that often times we forget and the industry forgets because Design Systems are becoming more popular and more prevalent we forget that no one needs a design system they need the thing the design system makes it'd be like walking in and trying to sell someone on the virtues of Sublime Text Editor and it's like well no one cares about Sublime Text Editor you know developers do but really people want what you can the code that you write and the thing that you make.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг и онбординг Напарника: говорить не про «таймер фокуса», а про результат: начать, довести шаг, почувствовать себя спокойнее.



# 🎬 Design Systems at Scale // Episode 6: Maintaining and Evolving Your Design System — Dan Mall


### Принципы нужны для выбора между двумя разумными путями  ·  [00:59](https://youtu.be/ejhk8tDO5fU?t=59)
**Мысль:** Принцип вроде «чисто и просто» бесполезен: никто не хочет грязно. Хороший принцип помогает на развилке, где разумный человек может пойти в обе стороны.

> The importance of design principles is to actually help people make a decision when there are two viable paths so design principle like clean and simple is probably not going to be very useful because no one wants messy and complicated as their as their byword instead we want to sort of have some principles that help to make decisions when you're at a fork in the road where a reasonable person could go in one direction or another so design principles become these things that are rallying cries in a sense or reminders of what is in our path and help us to make decisions when there are competing directions that we might go in.

**Для Напарника:** CLAUDE.md уже так работает (тёплый тон, диегетичность, медленность): формулировать принципы как правила выбора, а не лозунги.



# 🎬 Design Systems Showcase: TACKling Our Design System - Jon Kerwin, Thumbtack (Config) — Figma


### Начинать с основ и аудита: 821 вариант отступа сократили до 8  ·  [02:39](https://youtu.be/1wMnY4XzLG8?t=159)
**Мысль:** Команда начала с сетки, цвета, типографики, иконок и провела аудит; число значений отступа упало с 821 до 8, иконок с 530 до 150, и система сама пошла в рост.

> And of course it's to start with your foundations you want to start with your foundations for us this was grid color typography icons etc and you want to slowly work up through the complexity you don't want to go too fast you want to start slow just like you're building product and sometimes ripping up the floorboards can be really messy I'm so glad I get to use this slide it's one of my favorites she's so happy and for us it was like this we had 821 separate instances of space so developers were inputting manual space all over our product and you could see from page to page inconsistencies and spacing things would just kind of jump around through establishing a better system through thumbprint we were able to get that number down to eight four icons we went from 530 down to 150 to color and type so we would audit the system see what the mess was and think about a better system bringing in designers and developers and try to find a better way through the process and you'll find that this will snowball over time you'll set something out and developers will smell it they'll be like oh maybe I could use thumbprint for this maybe I could use thumbprint and save some time over here designers would say hey I think that's a little bit easier to use let me use that guideline in that pattern until other people about it so you'll find if you start with your foundation start small and communicate what they are you'll find that these will snowball over time.

**Для Напарника:** Провести аудит Tailwind-значений в Напарнике (отступы, размеры шрифта) и свести к короткой шкале токенов.



# 🎬 Design Theory: Shape Appeal — Sinix Design


### Мозг уже знает, что красиво: правила нужно впитать и довериться интуиции  ·  [07:18](https://youtu.be/P6yJO9gKSAI?t=438)
**Мысль:** Теория лишь раскладывает то, что мозг уже понимает; лучший результат даёт глубоко усвоенные принципы, которые выходят естественно, при нужной концентрации и энергии.

> Anyway why did I bring up intuition well as many things as I can just go on telling you about simplicity flow and obtuse angles the best aesthetic outcome will come from committing these ideas deep into your brain and just letting them come out naturally the best part about this subject as a whole you know shape appeal is that it's really just trying to deconstruct something that you already know your brain knows what looks good and what doesn't if you see a cool design you instantly know it you don't need to analyze every factor that makes your brain enjoy it that's my job you just have to acknowledge that your brain already understands aesthetics to a degree that is far more complex and nuanced than any lesson I can teach so while I'm digging through these abstract theories you can still create amazing shape appeal by simply letting your intuition guide your hand more how sharp should you make an angle how large should a shape be should it be curved or angular your brain knows what looks good you just have to find the right level of focus tempo and energy in order to let it come out well.

**Для Напарника:** Полировку делать глазами на реальных данных и устройстве: если «ощущается не так», верить ощущению и затем искать принцип (§9 CLAUDE.md).



# 🎬 Designing for AI: From Features to Systems. Rethinking UX in the Age of Agents — Belfast Design


### Решение хорошо ровно настолько, насколько точна проблема  ·  [02:02](https://youtu.be/qiR76B6iw8M?t=122)
**Мысль:** Мы одержимы решениями и мало думаем о корневой причине; решение — всего лишь идея, чтобы узнать больше о проблеме.

> Because when I hear people talk about UX design, they'll often describe things like would be cool if we had a feature that and then they go on and describe a solution. And a solution is only as good as the accuracy and the specificity, can't really say that, of the problem that it's solving. And we tend to only think about solutions. in fact, I would say we obsess over solutions. Everybody thinks that their idea is the best idea and no one's ever thought of that before. and we spend considerably less time thinking about the root cause of any problem. And solutions are good if they're treated as just ideas to learn more about the problem. And a solution can be very dangerous if it just if people just go with it and don't, you know, think of what the potential impact is of that thing.

**Для Напарника:** Перед новой механикой Напарника формулировать проблему человека с СДВГ, а не «добавим фичу».


### Промпт: описывай проблему, желаемый результат и измерение, а не решение  ·  [04:34](https://youtu.be/qiR76B6iw8M?t=274)
**Мысль:** GenAI работает лучше с контекстом; вместо готовых решений ему стоит дать проблему и критерии успеха.

> And basically you've described absolutely no context to something that is a genai tool. And genai works best when you give it context. so this will not provide a good solution. what will provide a better solution is if you describe the problem, the ideal outcomes and how to measure it. and try to avoid giving genai tools solutions because they are better at coming up with it.

**Для Напарника:** Промпты api/breakdown и api/companion строить из контекста человека (время суток, состояние, желаемый результат), а не жёстких инструкций.


### Зачем, когда, где, кто — контекст, без которого ИИ даёт «ровно что просили»  ·  [07:15](https://youtu.be/qiR76B6iw8M?t=435)
**Мысль:** Мы привыкли описывать как и что, но не зачем, когда, где и кто. Полный контекст — путь к полезному результату.

> Features and this is a big trap. because people tend to not move beyond thinking of things as features. and that's because we're addicted to explaining the how and the what. And not really the why, the when, the where, or the who. and by the way, the why is the problem. The when is how frequently that thing happens. So does it happen every day? Does it happen in the morning? Does it happen at night? does it happen only monthly? Whatever it is, where is obvious could mean many things not it could be physically where or it could be virtually in what environment and who is obviously the different the user. and all of this is context. So if you can describe that when you're using genai that is that is how you'll get the best outcome. and AI is more efficient with coming up with the how and the what when with the caveat being that when you provide it with the full context. and the thing is as designers we've spent most of our time providing ourselves with context. So this should be a skill that should naturally come to designers better than engineers or other groups that haven't had to consider the context as much as designers have and UX researchers as well. so when you describe the how and the what and not the why, when, where, or who, AI will give you exactly what you asked for, which isn't always what you wanted.

**Для Напарника:** Передавать в API время суток, длительность, прошлые сессии и имя, чтобы реплики кота были к месту.



# 🎬 Designing for Neurodiversity - Treyce M (Config 2022) — Figma


### Две стороны: доступ (access) и успех (success)  ·  [05:28](https://youtu.be/3aH--S3r9n4?t=328)
**Мысль:** Access — человек может войти и пользоваться; success — получает реальную ценность. Команды застревают на первом и забывают второе.

> Two sides of the same coin basically the first one the first thing that i think we need is what i'm calling access which is i think boiled down really sort of digital accessibility so this is providing users with sort of in essential engagement in your product right so they can navigate they can use your features they can log in you know they can do the sort of table stakes things that every website and every person using a website needs to do right and they can do those things without burden the other side of the coin is something i like to call success and i think if you boil this down you can sort of think of it as inclusive design or more on the inclusive design spectrum right and so this is that users can have really valuable engagement with your product right so they can not only navigate around but they can understand and achieve the value you're trying to bring them right they can have a meaningful experience and build strong proficiency with these products.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять Напарника не только на доступность (контраст, клавиатура), но и на то, достигает ли человек результата (запущенный шаг).


### Команды доступности застряли на комплаенсе, пора думать об успехе  ·  [16:35](https://youtu.be/3aH--S3r9n4?t=995)
**Мысль:** Соответствие стандартам — необходимый минимум, но не гарантия пользы; нужен следующий шаг к реальной ценности.

> Going back to those two concepts that i started with of access and success i think right now in our industry access is really trending which makes me so happy right i'm i'm thrilled that to see more designers engineers product people care about accessibility in a deep enough way that it is really become sort of an essential skill set or table sa table stakes functionality that every product needs to have it's really exciting but talking through to my friends and colleagues in the industry who are actively working in accessibility or who are accessibility designers their teams are really focused on compliance right now right which is honestly a wonderful thing i'm so grateful that we have laws and we have guidelines and legislation that require tools digital tools on the internet to reach a certain level of accessibility it's it's it's mind-boggling i wouldn't have like i would have never guessed that would have been the case years and years ago but these teams i think are really focused they're really focused on compliance for that business case right and what i would really like to see in the future is that they're able to balance this need for compliance and access but also be able to focus on success right so any team out there that's working on accessibility right now i really want i really hope this talk will empower your team to think outside of just compliance and start thinking.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист контрастов (6.63:1) — минимум; сверху добавлять проверку живыми сценариями и реалистичными данными.



# 🎬 Designing on expert mode — Dive Club 🤿


### Прототип, неотличимый от прода, это универсальный язык обратной связи  ·  [05:55](https://youtu.be/fJP4MKnx81s?t=355)
**Мысль:** Если интеракция ядро дизайна, проверять её нужно в фиделити продакшена: дать человеку выполнить реальную задачу и посмотреть, лёгко ли и сколько тапов.

> The quality of what you're designing is dictated by the interaction itself you can't wait too long in the design process to make something that feels real at that point I might turn to prototyping to put together a very low Fidelity prototype that has kind of a higher Fidelity implementation of the interaction just to test it out and see like okay that doesn't work or it needs to change in this way make it as kind of indistinguishable from what I might see in production if it was built over an engineering cycle and the goal with that being is you can just hand that to someone and say what do you think of this does it work maybe there's a little context setting of like what the goal is of this like what you're trying to get done but you hand it to someone and you say like change the properties on this linear issue and that is a real life user test in a way like can they do it do they find it easy does it take them way too many Taps to do when you have that prototype that may be just entirely spaghetti code that's never actually going to ship in the background but it creates this very high fidelity thing that's indistinguishable from production that's like the universal language of getting feedback and just like testing your idea for real.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять Фокус на реальных данных и в живом рендере (CLAUDE.md §6), а не по макету.


### Каждый слой абстракции даёт отговорку: чем ближе к реальному, тем больше ответственность  ·  [08:10](https://youtu.be/fJP4MKnx81s?t=490)
**Мысль:** Чтобы иметь убеждённость в решении, нужно трогать и чувствовать дизайн; слои абстракции позволяют сбросить ответственность.

> Designer is choosing between the best options of course you're going to rely on your gut and your intuition but you want to have conviction with whatever solution you're putting forward how can we get to that conviction if we don't even touch and feel our designs it's like a sculptor that spends all their time drawing a picture of the 3D sculpture and then spends the last 10% with their hands in the clay the more layers of abstraction that we add it increases our comfort zone because for every layer of abstraction is an excuse or a finger point to something else or someone else where I then don't have to take accountability the closer you get to the real thing also increases your level of accountability and responsibility.



# 🎬 Designing Through Uncertainty: Craft, AI, and Discomfort | Hugo Raymond | Layers 2025 — Friends of Figma, Portugal


### Крафт это не лоск, а каркас вокруг творческого риска  ·  [03:57](https://youtu.be/-uC_TbYYhK4?t=237)
**Мысль:** Нужно уметь пробовать идеи, которые могут не сработать; нехватка времени на неудачу главная скрытая цена.

> But here's the thing. Craft isn't only about polishing the final thing. It's about shaping the right thing. And to do that, we have to be willing to try ideas that might not work. Which brings us to the real hidden costs of many organizations. The lack of time for failure. Not because failure is bad, but because failure is how we explore. If we want meaningful innovation, craft isn't a luxury, it's the scaffolding we build around creative risk.


### Эксперимент с фотографами: количество приводит к качеству  ·  [06:11](https://youtu.be/-uC_TbYYhK4?t=371)
**Мысль:** Группу, оцениваемую по числу снимков, результат получился лучше, чем у группы, оцениваемой по одному идеальному: те много теоретизировали и получили один посредственный кадр.

> Gabriel Valdivia, who helps early stage companies, provided a helpful approach which he believes can benefit organizations of any scale. His core directive progress, not perfection, is the engine of innovation. Maybe not a huge surprise, but the insight came not from valuing quality over quantity, but understanding that quantity is often how we get to quality. He gave the example of an experiment conducted by Jerry Uelsmann, a photographer and teacher who split his students into two groups for an activity. You may have heard of this one before. Group A would be graded based on the total quality of the photos that they took, whilst Group B only had to produce a single photo and that alone would be based on quality. Group A, who were tasked with taking as many photos as possible. Naturally spent more time in the practice, which yielded far greater results than the team asked to focus on quality. They spent too much time theorizing and were left with a single mediocre shot.

**Для Напарника:** Близко к принципу Напарника: маленькие шаги и много коротких стартов важнее одного идеального; можно использовать в текстах про прощение несовершенства.


### Дешёвые одноразовые артефакты снижают эмоциональную привязанность  ·  [08:03](https://youtu.be/-uC_TbYYhK4?t=483)
**Мысль:** Когда идею легко выбросить, меньше привязанности и страха суждения; пустой холст превращается в площадку, где единственная ошибка это мало пробовать.

> So AI can lower the cost of risk with tools like Figma Make, generating high fidelity prototypes from designs or even text prompts in minutes. This lowers the cost of experimentation. If a wild idea flops, you haven't lost days or weeks of effort. The speed and disposability of AI generated artifacts makes it easier to fail forward. It can also reduce emotional investment. If AI can help you spin up and discard ideas rapidly, you're less likely to become attached to a single solution. This detachment makes it easier to take creative risks and embrace the learning that comes from failure. And finally, it can also help democratize feedback by increasing the surface area in which feedback that feedback is applied. AI helps to visualize and share examples in real time, making it easier to invite feedback from a broader group. It's also more participatory in the early stage, with lower barriers to entry for non designers to experiment. As I'm sure many of us have experienced with developers and product managers getting involved in that vibe coding aspect. This supports Valdivia’s call to share before you're ready and builds collective ownership, reducing the fear of judgment. In short, AI can act as a creative amplifier and the safety nets, making the messy, iterative process of early stage design less intimidating and more playful. It's a way to turn the fear the blank canvas into playground of possibility, where the only real mistake is not trying enough things fast enough to discover what works.

**Для Напарника:** Перенос на СДВГ-механику: низкая цена ошибки и право бросить без вины помогают начать задачу.


### Контекст для ИИ: шаблон, 2-3 образца, restate-first, гейтинг, self-check  ·  [16:37](https://youtu.be/-uC_TbYYhK4?t=997)
**Мысль:** Структурированный бриф (цель, аудитория, ограничения, не-цели, definition of done), хороший/плохой/идеальный примеры, пересказ брифа перед работой, белый список инструментов и проверочный рубрикатор снижают дрейф модели.

> And context is becoming progressively much, much more important in this. So think about it in using the word context for context, but context here. Structure. Use a template with fixed sections. If anybody's got experience of using markdown files, having that hierarchy or XML, which is another format for your goal, your audience, your constraints, your non goals, definition of done, sources which are allowed and which ones are blocked. Exemplars 2 to 3. This one is new to me, and I haven't had a chance to experiment with it that much. But this idea of actually providing the model with a bad example, a good example, and an ideal example. Models like to imitate patterns. So show the pattern that you want. And having those references can be helpful for the context of what it is you're trying to produce. A pinned brief. Place it in the system or the context. The first step is to restate the brief and ask clarifying questions. If it can restate, it can proceed so it can't. Sorry. If it can't restate, it can't proceed. So this is an opportunity for the model to sort of verify some of the reasoning that's happening. When it's reviewing the brief at each juncture. Constraints and tool gating. White list tools and sources add guardrails like never invent tokens. If token not found, ask. Require user confirmation for any side effect full action. This is a thing that, often feels like we're dealing with a large language model, like a naughty child. And we have to get it to sort of ask us for permission, rather than forgiveness, I think sometimes. Self-check. Before output, the model runs a short rubric “Did I meet...”, you know, this is almost like writing, test scripts. Did I meet, AA contrast? Did I keep the design system version three? Did I include empty or error loading? If any fail revise before handing off.

**Для Напарника:** Применимо к промптам /api/companion и /api/session-voice: фиксированный бриф тона (без стыда), образцы реплик и самопроверка перед выдачей.



# 🎬 Designing Voice user interfaces to boost customer experiences | Founder Fridays - June 2022 — Google for Developers


### Проектируй чат по реальным разговорам, а не по воображаемым  ·  [04:36](https://youtu.be/PT3lFf_wi7U?t=276)
**Мысль:** Люди из колл-центра и живых диалогов знают, о чём клиенты спрашивают и как формулируют. Это источник реальных реплик вместо придуманных.

> First off lean on real world conversations what do I mean by that if your business has a call center with human agents or agents who do online chat talk to them they are a wealth of information they're the ones who are actually interacting with your customers they know what kind of things they want to talk about and they know how they ask for them now if you don't have that think about where in the world those real life conversations are happening that are related to what you're trying to do for example if you're making an app that books restaurant reservations talk to the people at restaurants who answer the phone or maybe you have a health care app talk to the nurses the doctors the people who would be having those conversations with people.

**Для Напарника:** Реплики напарника писать от реальных фраз людей с СДВГ (из отзывов, интервью), а не от «правильных» формулировок.


### Sample dialogues: прочитай диалог вслух с партнёром  ·  [08:05](https://youtu.be/PT3lFf_wi7U?t=485)
**Мысль:** Дешёвые черновые диалоги, прочитанные вслух, вскрывают массу проблем ещё до кода.

> Do a lot of prototyping early and often you don't have to get fancy those things I did at the beginning with the tuna salad conversations those are called sample dialogues and that's how we begin any design experience we just start writing those and read them out loud hopefully you have a partner you can read with and it won't just be like I did reading to myself but you'd be amazed at how many problems you can find just by doing these simple sample dialogue readings and they're low-cost low Fidelity and you can work out a lot of things early.

**Для Напарника:** Любую новую цепочку реплик напарника сначала прогонять как сценарий вслух, проверяя тон и длину.


### Проектируй под то, как люди говорят на самом деле  ·  [09:10](https://youtu.be/PT3lFf_wi7U?t=550)
**Мысль:** Пользователи говорят неожиданно; принять это и итерировать — путь к успешному диалогу.

> If you only remember one thing from this whole talk this is the slide design for how people actually talk not how you want them to talk and what I mean by that is you may create what you think is a beautiful design and you put it out in the world and then you watch people interact with it and then you just start cringing because they're saying all these things that you're like but they shouldn't say that I never expected that but that's how it is people talk in a lot of different ways they're going to say things you don't expect and that's okay and if you can accept that and understand that you're going to iterate on your design and make it better then you're on your way to making really successful conversational experiences.

**Для Напарника:** Логировать (локально/с согласия) нераспознанные ответы в чек-инах и дописывать ветки реплик напарника.



# 🎬 Does Claude actually think like a designer? — Silu


### ИИ ускоряет, но вопрос «что для них награда» решает только ресёрч  ·  [14:10](https://youtu.be/_5EMOZGY9yg?t=850)
**Мысль:** Сколько ни итерируй в ИИ, не узнаешь, мотивирует ли именно эту аудиторию награда; рабочий прототип нужен как исследовательский артефакт.

> Here's where I'm going to stop and be honest about what AI can and can't solve. The reward system. I asked Claude to solve it and it gave me an answer, but I don't actually know if the answer is right. It doesn't feel right. And no matter how many iterations I do it here, I still won't know because it's now it's not about design, but now it's more about the core problem that we're trying to solve. It's more about the user research problem. Do users actually care about planting a tree? Does that motivate the specific person who's overwhelmed with a thesis deadline? I don't know and neither does Claude. So, my next step for this isn't more iterations in Claude, it's user research targeted at that exact user this app is built for. So, I need to understand what reward actually means to them before I design for it. And here's where the process comes full circle. So, I'll use Claude to build out a working prototype from what we have and use that as the research artifact and something I can actually put in front of the users and get real feedback and then come back again to design the right solution. And that's how I would use AI in real product process, not as the answer, but as the accelerator.

**Для Напарника:** Прототипы наград острова выносить на тест людям с СДВГ до полировки; ИИ — как ускоритель, а не ответ.



# 🎬 Executive dysfunction explained: Why ADHD makes everything so hard | Experts Answer — Understood


### Рабочая память как RAM: вынеси наружу и собери заранее  ·  [01:01](https://youtu.be/0XcppZUxxUo?t=61)
**Мысль:** Перегруженная память теряет вещи; лучший ответ - списки, и подготовка заранее, до последнего момента.

> ADHD impacts your working memory. That's the kind of memory you use for what you're doing right now. You wanna think about it like RAM in a computer. If it's overloaded or glitchy, things just disappear. Out of sight, out of mind is a very real thing for those with ADHD. So the fix for this is to externalize as much as you can. For example, if you're gonna go on a trip, I would strongly suggest that you create a packing list for yourself and write down everything that you're gonna need. And bonus points, pack ahead of time. Give yourself plenty of space in order to double-check, forget things, and remember them again and put them on the list. If you wait until the last moment, your working memory is gonna be overwhelmed with 10,000 different things, and you're way more likely to forget something like your passport.

**Для Напарника:** Заранее-список для частых ритуалов; предложение подготовить вечером то, что нужно утром.

Simple example for that is wearing a watch or always having a clock in view to help support your executive function challenge with time sense, right? ADDers tend to be time blind. So, always having a clock in view, always having a watch is one way to externalize time. Lists are ways to externalize our, you know, our executive function struggles with memory. A good task management system is another way to externalize your executive function struggles with mental organization of information and memory and all of that,.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник как внешняя память: очередь шагов, видимые часы, «что дальше».


### Без плана день диктует то, что попало в поле внимания  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/Cw2uijd2SOY?t=434)
**Мысль:** Планирование нелюбимо: одни перепланируют, другие избегают; но без него остаётся реактивность и тушение пожаров. Назвать можно как угодно.

> Most of my clients don't love the word planning. Most of us with ADD have sort of a love-hate relationship with planning. Some of us are over-planners, so we burn ourselves up, we use, you know, sort of use all of our energy up with making the perfect plan. Others of us find it really hard to sit down and force ourselves to plan. But, when you're determining what action to take when, that's a form of planning. So, if you can call it whatever you want, I actually call it setting myself up for the next day. I have clients that just call it the deciding, and that's okay, right? But, that form of planning is an important thing for us to do. If we're not planning in some way, again, no matter what you call it, if you're not planning in some way, you're letting whatever comes into your attention direct your action. So, you're reactive, you're your whole day is dictated by email, your whole day is dictated by whatever comes into your attention, or whatever sort of fires need to be put out in the moment. And that's not proactive, that's not moving anything forward, that's just basically treading water and keeping up. And most of us do a lot of that.

**Для Напарника:** Назвать планирование мягко («настроить завтра», «набросок на день»), не «план»; не требовать идеального.


### Не планируй в час упадка и прерываний; подгоняй систему под себя  ·  [10:09](https://youtu.be/Cw2uijd2SOY?t=609)
**Мысль:** Планирование стоит ставить в момент энергии и без помех; чужой способ редко подходит.

> You don't want to slate your planning, you don't want to slate your deciding part for a point in your day when your energy's really low or a point in your day where your focus is really low. You don't want to slate your planning for a time in the day when you're prone to interruptions, right? You want to You want to look at your life, look at how you do things most naturally, how you do things most effectively, what you're already doing now that's working well, and figure out how to make these things work for yourself. You can't try to plug yourself into someone else's way of doing things. It very rarely works.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-ин: «когда у тебя больше сил?» и подгонка напоминаний и Фокуса под ответ.

> If you strip away the design of the hero section, that leaves us with just three lines, a small label, a slogan, and a button. So, there's no line saying who's it for, what it does. It's not there, and buyers now have to scroll down to learn that. And most of them, they don't do that. A hero section that works have four things in order: a headline, who's it for and what they get, and proof, and the button. And if you see our redesign, it basically has all four of them right away. A clear heading, a clear indicator of what's happening. We're also telling them who's it for, and what they get. And now, we're also telling them who uses it, which is our proof. And a clear button which says what exactly is going to happen. So, write your hero section as a plain text before you design it. Don't worry about how it will look. Just see the text.

> Well the loop asks us to look at what problems the player is solving how often and how exactly we're going about reinforcing them a key insight with the game loop is the importance of looking out for signs and feedback does our loop adequately signal available goals and actions and does it provide adequate feedback to teach the player more about the game rules after they take an action once we know the problems that are being solved and we've identified our loops we can start considering the player's questions while they engage in those loops how much health do i have left how does that enemy die which enemies should i hit first is the player asking interesting questions are we providing tools to answer those questions are we asking too many questions at once looking at the game as loops as cycles of learning and problem solving we can evaluate play tests as learning exercises and sort out where the loop is breaking for players.

> The canvas helps me think through every aspect of the microcopy for every screen. For example, who's the target user? What's the purpose of the copy? What are the user's concerns and emotions at that moment? What is the user context? Do I have their full attention or do I need to highlight something to get their attention? What is the action I expect the users to take based on the microcopy? What should be the style and tone of the words that I choose? What is the success criteria? And my favorite part of the canvas – the copy experiments. Since I was new to the UX Writing process, I used to come up with like one version of the copy, and be done with it. But having this prompt to think through the copy experiments made me want to come up with different versions of the microcopy and then choose the one that best met my requirements. So that is Step 1 of my UX Writing process – step back, fill out the canvas, and think through the user experience for each screen.

# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal: The Full Summary — Yapping Books


### Инвестиция — самая медленная фаза и ту чаще всего пропускают  ·  [06:30](https://youtu.be/0A-Do6ePZyY?t=390)
**Мысль:** Инвестиция улучшает следующий опыт и заряжает следующий триггер, но её трудно подделать, поэтому на неё почти не тратят время.

> The fourth phase is the answer. An investment is something the user contributes that makes the product more valuable later. And that distinction is worth holding. The point is not that you spend something. The point is that what you spent improves the next experience. You follow people so tomorrow's feed is better. You rate a film so recommendations improve. You upload a file and now this is where that file lives. It can also set up the next trigger. You send a message so now you are waiting for a reply. And their reply is what brings you back. You post a photo, someone comments, and that is another one. The user is now helping build the thing that returns them to the product. Being honest about products I have worked on, we spent nearly all our time on the reward and almost none on the investment. The fourth phase is slower and much harder to fake than a reward is. I suspect that is exactly why it keeps getting left out.

**Для Напарника:** Вложения человека (имя, шаги, история острова) делают следующий визит лучше; проектировать их осознанно.

> The deeper idea is a loop with an order to it, and the order is the argument. If you are building something, the whole book reduces to four questions. What feeling brings the user here? What is the easiest useful thing they can do? What reward keeps it interesting enough to return for? And what can they invest that makes the next visit better than this one? The thing I would tell myself before reading this. Every time I treated retention as a messaging problem, I was working on the scaffolding. The question underneath was always which feeling we wanted to be the answer to.

# 🎬 How Claude Code’s lead designer builds with AI — Dive Club 🤿


### Скилл-прототип: N вариантов, пусть ИИ выберет и обоснует  ·  [02:59](https://youtu.be/hKeDfupbA4U?t=179)
**Мысль:** Дизайнер Claude Code делает скилл, который генерирует пять вариантов фичи в HTML, сам выбирает лучший и объясняет почему, а результат приходит как PR со скриншотом или записью.

> That's it. No design specs, no just like I want to add autocomplete, I want to see what it looks like. A few things I'm going to call out here that I think are important when you're prompting. The first is I built myself a slash prototype skill. I asked Claude to build this, you could build it in like 2 seconds. All it does is get Claude to generate n number of options, default to five, of a different implementation of a feature, generate an HTML file, preview it, and then iterate on it a few times and I can enter that n and I can enter what the feature is going to be. I did not write this. No one ever writes their skills by hand anymore. If anyone tells you they do, they're lying. Everyone just prompts them. and then a few things I will add in here is I always ask Claude to pick an option for me first. I think it's actually fun to see what Claude comes up with. So, historically, I used to be like, oh, like prototype and then I'll choose. Now I'm like, oh, prototype and then you should choose what you're going to do and then tell me why. And then I say another one is like feel free to research online. Now, if I were doing this in my production code base, I'd say like please look at Slack, Google Docs, any discussions, and like BigQuery, like look at all these sources and figure out which is the best one. But given that this is an open-source repo, online research is fine. And then, you know, implement the best option, verify, get the styles to match. really important one, put up a PR with a screenshot. Claude is actually really good at this, and I actually don't review the outputs anymore of Claude in the transcript. I'm typically reviewing a PR that Claude put up that has a recording of the feature that implemented.

**Для Напарника:** Для разработки Напарника: просить ИИ выдавать несколько вариантов экрана или микрокопирайта и выбирать с обоснованием, но финальное решение за владельцем продукта.


### ИИ пока плох в дизайне: человек остаётся в цикле ремесла  ·  [05:44](https://youtu.be/hKeDfupbA4U?t=344)
**Мысль:** Автор считает, что LLM ещё не умеют проектировать хорошо, поэтому решение о том, что попадает в продукт, должен принимать человек.

> So, the first is that Claude, tragically, and most LLMs are not good at design yet. So, what this means is that you should still very much be in the loop for the craft and the decision-making. It doesn't mean this is going to be holding true forever, but a lot of my workflows revolve around the idea that I still need to be the one making a decision about what actually goes into the product.

### Если все могут выпускать, не всё должно выпускаться  ·  [06:47](https://youtu.be/hKeDfupbA4U?t=407)
**Мысль:** Когда доступ к коду открыт всем, нужны системы масштабирования качества; автор отправляет сотни мелких полировочных правок и сводит их в один PR.

> And then the third one, which I don't think is controversial for this room, but has definitely been one that has been an uphill battle a lot of places, is just because everyone can ship doesn't mean not everything should ship. And that's a pretty important distinction that we need to start making as we're adding more features and as everyone has the ability to access code and like push to production, we need better systems that scale. And so, some ways that we're fighting this internally today is I actually use Claude in the web. Does everyone have this internally at their teams or companies? you might have it with your own internal infrastructure, but I have like Claude in the cloud or Claude in the web is a service that we offer and I use it as a way to send like hundreds of tiny little polish fixes all the time that I find in the app. They're not worth spinning up a new session for, they're not worth dedicated time. I just have all of these going and I would say, sometimes I get complaints from the engineers that there's too many of them. So, every now and then I'll ask Claude to squash them all into one PR for me. And sometimes they just got auto approved because they're tiny CSS changes and no one really needs to review them. So, I would say like this is a really great way to maintain crafting quality in your products and it's something that you can do on the side as Claude is working.

**Для Напарника:** Копить мелкие правки полировки и сдавать одним PR, а не дробить на множество; соответствует практике ветки и squash-merge проекта.


### Автоматизация на несколько шагов вперёд, даже если пока сыро  ·  [10:01](https://youtu.be/hKeDfupbA4U?t=601)
