> I have a Claude code routine, which is a scheduled task if you use Claude code work. And what this does is it scrapes all of our repositories for any front-end changes that anyone has implemented. It will check if a designer was involved in it by going through Slack, Google Meet transcripts, like Google Docs, like any kind of data that it has access to. If a designer was not involved in it, it will flag that it shipped without a designer or not. >> >> If it shipped without a designer, it will come up with an adversarial or it'll review the design and then it will typically come up with an adversarial design in a PR already drafted, DM it to the engineer who shipped that feature, and then ask them to work with the designer on that feature. I tested this a few times and I had to turn off the DMing cuz Claude was actually really bad at design, unfortunately. And I We did have to like it's And like that goes back to my first one, like Claude is not good at design yet, but I think this is the level of automation that you have to be thinking, like not just the first step, but like the next step, the next step, the next step after that. Like you want to be pushing it so far that it's like building the actual end product.



# 🎬 How Duolingo Built a $1B App Empire (And You Can Too) — Anything 


### CURR: удержание текущих пользователей весомее привлечения  ·  [04:27](https://youtu.be/i9S0pp97fEw?t=267)
**Мысль:** Метрика вероятности вернуться на следующей неделе у стабильно активных оказалась в 5 раз влиятельнее следующей по важности; фокус сместился на удержание.

> Around 2018, their growth team developed the CUR framework, current user retention rate. It measures how likely a consistently engaged user is to come back next week. When they ran sensitivity analysis on what drives daily active users, CUR had five times the impact of the second best metric. That insight changed their entire strategy. They stopped obsessing over acquiring new users and started obsessing over keeping the ones they had.

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника главная метрика: возвращается ли человек на следующей неделе, а не число установок.



# 🎬 How experienced designers drive alignment — Dive Club 🤿


### Не показывать сотню вариантов: объяснить рамку, которая вас вела  ·  [00:32](https://youtu.be/SxcpZq4od4I?t=32)
**Мысль:** Без объяснения компромиссов и критериев люди видят только визуальные различия и дают рассеянный отзыв; рамка создаёт архитектуру идей и позволяет согласовать на разных уровнях.

> Just display the prototype and the idea and show a thousand iterations because I think sometimes that can overwhelm people where you're like it could work like this, it could work like this, it could work like this, it could work like this. There's like a hundred options and people don't know when you came up with those options. They don't know what you had in your mind, what you were trading off, what framework was sort of guiding you as you put those different things together. And the differences are just going to be visual at first. The way they when they see the different things, they're going to be like, "Okay, well, that one the menu's on the top. That one's sits in the bottom. That one there is no menu." So, I think it's very important to establish and make very clear the framework that was guiding you when you did the thing so that it can sort of create an architecture around the ideas that you show that is very clear to people so that it's not like just getting a whole bunch of mocks thrown at you. You actually help them understand what is distinct about this idea. Why is it even being included in this in this combination? And where does it sit on this little map that I'm going to draw for you? You can get alignment at multiple levels. Maybe people don't like the specific concept, but they do agree with the underlying framework behind it. And that's why it's so important to communicate your thinking. If you're just sharing a like a whole bunch of work, like what do you guys think of this? And you don't really make it clear what you were really toiling over, you're going to get feedback all over the place.


### Найти главную переменную и показать идеи на спектре  ·  [01:48](https://youtu.be/SxcpZq4od4I?t=108)
**Мысль:** Одна переменная (мощность против абстракции, аппетит команды) определяет решения; на спектре заинтересованным легко сказать «где-то между вторым и третьим».

> Before you get lost in concepts, ask yourself, what is the main variable that will dictate what we design and ship for this project? If you're designing an AI product, maybe it's power versus abstraction, or maybe it's just how much appetite the team has to invest in this feature. Once I have that variable, my go-to strategy is to present my ideas on a spectrum. And this is what most of my in-flight projects look like. That way it's easy for stakeholders to say, you know, none of these concepts are quite it, but I think somewhere in between two and three feels right to me.


### Сгруппировать концепции в направления и спросить: «Направление верное?»  ·  [02:32](https://youtu.be/SxcpZq4od4I?t=152)
**Мысль:** Обсуждение на уровне направлений выбивает меньше споров о деталях; согласие с направлением полезный результат критики.

> Concepts, right? But what if you actually zoom 2x so that you actually summarize like these five different concept into two directions, you know? So then you can actually debate on a higher level like the directional level with your cross functional partners because they might have more opinions about that level versus the details. So when you can kind of visualize your thought process that way, you actually can guide the conversation. You know they may disagree with like you know option one A but they actually will bought in into this direction one but they just maybe there needs to be like a one D. You know that's just different. But like that's still a really good output from a crit, right? So like you get that some kind of alignment, but you know what are the next level of things that you need to work on. One of the questions I picked up from Maven that I still use all the time is asking does this feel directionally correct? And grouping concepts like this is a great way to get alignment on that top level direction before you get into the details of specific concepts.


### Показывать работу вместе со своим мнением и сомнениями; быстро делиться «неверным» направлением  ·  [06:01](https://youtu.be/SxcpZq4od4I?t=361)
**Мысль:** Мнение нужно не чтобы быть правым, а чтобы вызвать реакцию; быстрое «неправильное» направление заставляет других объяснить, что не так, и это открывает следующий шаг.

> Needs to be clear one, what is the help you need? Like what feedback are you looking for? But two, what's your own opinion? Like what do you think is the best solution and why? And what are some of the challenges you're still facing with it? Some of the doubts you still have. So people can tell you like, well actually I think you're overthinking this or no actually I think you're you're right to be doubting this. Here's another path you can take. Now why does having an opinion matter so much? Honestly, it's not really about being right. It's about forcing a reaction from stakeholders. It's why I keep referencing Michael Wanderer's clip about the ridiculously early hypothesis at Metalab. Something that I started like about 3 or 4 years ago and it's really caught on was doing a thing called a ridiculously early hypothesis. And what this meant was to try to come back after a week of kickoff with a really strong structured narrative driven point of view as to what we should be doing next and to present that to get a reaction to the client so that we don't give like some 4-week span of like product definition without having really put an idea forward. Often times the best way to move a project forward isn't figuring out what the right direction is. It's being quick to share the wrong direction because it forces other people to explain why they think something is incorrect. And that process of articulating those thoughts unlocks what you need as the designer to take the next step.


### Итерировать к истине: 10 сырых интерфейсов лучше недели чтения  ·  [07:27](https://youtu.be/SxcpZq4od4I?t=447)
**Мысль:** Делать вещи, пусть и плохие, быстрее приводит к правде, чем обсуждения; с опытом число итераций до хорошего падает (с четырёх до двух).

> Your way to truth. It's better to make 10 things without knowing how it works versus making one thing after spending one week reading 10 books and 15 documents and internalizing it. We don't want academic PhDs. We want just like doing. And I think creating interfaces no matter how shitty is a much better way to get to the truth than to ideulate and use words and like you know kind of talk to people like 10 meetings is not worth it. And if you are having 10 meetings take a picture to each of them. Take 10 pictures. Show all 10 of them. Let the work speak for itself. That starts to become faster over time where like it used to take me like four designs to get to good. Now it takes two. And it's the same idea of like how can I like short circuit the iteration process because it's like I've been there before.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает практику проекта: показывать реальный рендер на реалистичных данных, а не обсуждать на словах (CLAUDE.md §6).



# 🎬 How I Fixed a Client's Onboarding and Won the Contract — Bear Liu


### 19 шагов онбординга: что исправили — шаги, тон «про нас», нет aha-момента  ·  [00:11](https://youtu.be/S7eGDOkd_Pg?t=11)
**Мысль:** Клиент заставлял проходить опросы, знакомство с функциями и сбор данных до входа в приложение. Исправления: сократить шаги, сменить тон с «о продукте» на «о пользователе» и добавить aha-момент, где человек ощущает ценность.

> I still hadn't been able to get into using the app, just because his app has 19 onboarding steps, including asking people to fill out a survey, introducing some new features, or just collecting some inputs from users. So there are so many different steps before people are actually able to land on the app and use the app. So I mapped out all the things that could be improved on these 19 steps and proposed something, and long story short, I got this client, I got this contract, and I also mapped out a new onboarding experience for this product and got it designed and implemented, and now it's ready for launch. But there are still some things I feel could be improved on this onboarding flow, or on the larger scale, how to improve people's onboarding experience. And there are things I fixed — for example, so many steps; now it's getting much shorter. And the tone is all about us, it's all about the product, not about the users, and this is something we need to shift. And also, now it's all in the app, but actually the larger onboarding experience happens starting from people browsing your landing page, or even earlier — before the landing page — just when people start to check any relevant content from social media. And also another major thing I fixed is that in this onboarding flow, there's no aha moment, which means that people can't feel the value of the product.

**Для Напарника:** Эталон «чего не делать» для онбординга Напарника: минимум шагов до первой сессии, тексты про человека, а не про функции.



# 🎬 How I Make Apps FEEL 10x Better (5 Design Secrets) — Chris Raroque


### Сложную анимацию не заказывай одним промптом: разбей на подшаги  ·  [02:01](https://youtu.be/8mMH6Pq8qnE?t=121)
**Мысль:** Если описать каждый под-эффект отдельно (стрелка превращается в галочку, фон расширяется, текст гаснет), ИИ исполняет это безупречно, а общий запрос даёт банальный результат.

> Don't try to oneshot this and say, "Hey, can you go animate this for me?" Because it's not going to come up with anything this cool and complex. For this one, I had to break down all the sub animations that I wanted to happen. Can you make the arrow rotate into the check mark? Can you have that background expand from the microphone? Can you add some of this fade in fade out for the text? So, I broke down all the components I wanted to happen in the interaction and it just executed it for me flawlessly. Apple has a really good built-in API for animations and Claude Code and a lot of AI coding tools are very well trained on it. So, you can actually just tell it to do this stuff for you in plain English and it has a really high chance of doing it. But again, the trick is to not oneshot it and to instead break it down.

**Для Напарника:** При постановке задач ИИ на хореографию (RevealIsland) перечислять последовательность подшагов с таймингами.


### Вкус растёт от постоянного воздействия хорошего дизайна  ·  [09:01](https://youtu.be/8mMH6Pq8qnE?t=541)
**Мысль:** Подписка на дизайнеров и закладки того, что нравится, формируют вкус постепенно, не за один раз.

> Up with these good designs? How do I just elevate the feeling of my app in general? and it is to expose yourself to really good design constantly because it's not a one-time thing. I really do think that it is a process to get better and elevate your taste. And I have two specific places that I go to do this. Number one is Twitter. There's a ton of really good talented designers on Twitter. So, I recommend just go finding them, following a bunch of designers, and then you'll just see a bunch of their stuff on your feed and bookmark everything that you like.

**Для Напарника:** Вести папку референсов (зрелые дорогие сайты, как разрешает §10 CLAUDE.md) для каждой полировки экрана.



# 🎬 How I make UI color palettes — UX Tools


### Для палитры используй HSB, а не hex или RGB  ·  [02:36](https://youtu.be/yYwEnLYT55c?t=156)
**Мысль:** HSB даёт понятный контроль над оттенком, насыщенностью и яркостью, а значит, палитру проще строить систематически.

> Most design programs will default to using hex values for colors but when making a color palette you should use HSB instead of hex or RGB the H in HSB stands for Hue which is the color it's a number from 1 to 365 which makes sense because it goes all the way around the color wheel s stands for saturation which is how intense the color is on a scale from 0 to 100 and b stands for brightness which means how much black or white you're adding to the color again on a scale from zero to a hundred HSB will give us much finer control over our colors in a more understandable way.

**Для Напарника:** Палитру ориентиров и неба в oklch строить так же систематически: менять один параметр за раз.


### Шкала из 9 оттенков 100–900 по диагонали палитры  ·  [04:21](https://youtu.be/yYwEnLYT55c?t=261)
**Мысль:** Берётся базовый цвет как 500, самый тёмный и самый светлый края, затем серединные точки по дуге от верхнего левого до нижнего правого угла пикера. Тёмный оттенок идёт на текст, светлый на тонированный фон.

> Our colors are going to be used in all kinds of different places throughout the UI so we're going to need more than just a dark medium and light we'll probably want five to ten Shades so here's the process I like to follow layout nine blocks nine is a good number because it's easy to divide up as you'll see in a minute label them 100 to 900 and we use hundreds like this in part two allow us to easily add half steps later if we want but the color you've already chosen in the middle spot at 500 so let's start with our brand purple now we're going to start picking Shades but here is the trick for finding them imagine a line that starts at the top left corner of the Color Picker and goes to the bottom right corner passing through your base color the secret is to pick your Shades along this Arc the first Shades we're going to be picking are the lightest and darkest Shades out at the ends if you need a starting point try a dark shade around 90 to 100 saturation and 20 to 30 brightness and a light shade around 5 to 10 saturation and 95 to 100 brightness but also think about where it's going to be used the darkest shade of a color is usually used for text and your lightest shade will probably be used for a tinted background an alert component is a good example that combines both of these use cases so it can be a great place to pick these colors now we have two gaps let's pick the colors right in the middle of the gaps at 300 and 700 our lightest shade is here on the Color Picker and our 500 shade is here so let's pick this middle point between them on the arc for 300 and we'll do the same for 700.

**Для Напарника:** Построить для акцента Напарника шкалу 100–900 и фиксировать её токенами в дизайн-системе, а не подбирать оттенки на глаз.


### Проверка шкал прищуриванием: одинаковый диапазон светлоты  ·  [06:29](https://youtu.be/yYwEnLYT55c?t=389)
**Мысль:** Если шкалы разных цветов не совпадают по диапазону света, при замене 200 жёлтого на 200 синего интерфейс выглядит несвязным.

> The fifth and final step is to test them out to see if we need to make any tweaks something I like to do is put all the scales in a row and then squint my eyes or blur the layers to make sure each scale has the same range going from light to dark you can see in this palette that we're making that the line is a little squiggly it's not really even and that means that when we swap out colors in the UI like for example switching out a background from yellow 200 to a blue 200 it won't look cohesive.

**Для Напарника:** Прищуриванием или блюром проверять, что оттенки неба по часам суток остаются в одном коридоре светлоты.



# 🎬 How Ramp and Plaid create a culture of craft — Dive Club 🤿


### Много неформальных петель обратной связи, а не один еженедельный крит  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/36iCkzRbC0Y?t=22)
**Мысль:** Формальный крит раздувается подготовкой; в Ramp завели небольшие группы доверия («truth seeking pods») по 3–4 человека, где просят честный отзыв: добрый, но не вежливый.

> The best designs come from design teams not designers and it's all about feedback cloops you create within the team the problem is too many teams have a weekly crit and that's it so what happens is the formality of crit increases over time and this is something that Diego Zach the VP of design at ramp saw happen as the company scaled people were spending a lot of time preparing for it and making this like big presentations and hey I wanted to look good and I'm making all these Transitions and figma and all this stuff and actually like and the work became 20% of it now I've never been one to make particularly pretty decks but I've been in this situation multiple times and having a single weekly crit puts a ton of pressure on designers to make the most out of that time as a result you're probably only talking about the highlevel really big strategy decisions so here's how Diego handled that at ramp great if you want to be formal here's a place where you're really formal and then let's create all these other avenues for you to get feedback the least formal one the safest environment that you have is we've created these truth seeking pods which is three or four designers that are across the entire platform so they're not working the same things they get together two or three times a week and every time that they get together a different person presents their work through the lens of a decision that they've made so the goal is to have a very small group of high trust people that are going to help you deconstruct a decision a framework that got you somewhere and you set the expectation that you want real feedback you want people to be kind not nice.


### Работа с открытой дверью: поздняя демонстрация превращает отзыв в нападение  ·  [02:20](https://youtu.be/36iCkzRbC0Y?t=140)
**Мысль:** Чем глубже в работе, тем сильнее эмоциональная привязанность и тем больнее обратная связь, плюс времени менять уже нет; делиться нужно на этапе наброска на салфетке.

> That's a huge part of my philosophy as a design leader is the designers need to always have again the grass door up like you need to let people see what you're working on comment on it and really give you that sort of ongoing feedback that was so crucial problema in very many different design teams struggle with where designers wait a little too long before they share their work and so one when you too deep into the work you sort of have an emotional attachment to the solutions and so the feedback you get feels like an attack on you not on the work and people get like start of defend their solution their ideas rather than listening to feedback and it's also often a little too late really to kind of shift like you wen't the designer is likely running out of time and so they're showing the final thing like well you know there's only so much you're going to be able to change before you need to go and build this and so making sure designers sh share the work at the beginning of the idea like hey like here's a real concept I have I made a sketch on a napkin like let's talk about my sketch on a napkin.

**Для Напарника:** Созвучно тону продукта без стыда: показывать и просить помощи рано (первый шаг, черновик), пока нет привязанности и страха оценки.


### Polish reviews: постоянная панель сеньоров перед передачей в разработку  ·  [06:16](https://youtu.be/36iCkzRbC0Y?t=376)
**Мысль:** Одни и те же четыре человека за 24–48 часов проверяют поля, консистентность, глагол вместо существительного в кнопке; глаз замыливается, и со временем замечаний становится в разы меньше, потому что дизайнеры учатся.

> Something we call polish reviews we've created this panel of I think currently four I Thief like really senior ic's on the team who really understand this design system have really good eye for like the little things the little like margins are slightly off or like they're like consistency with this other experien we have a Content designer was like looking at all the words like is there a typo is there a word that a button that doesn't use a verb but use a you know noun and we don't do that so like really paying attention to those details and so that panel which is always the same so there's a consistency you see everything will be receiving that figma file at the very last moment before it gets chicked up to like building like before the engineer start working on it they'll review it one last time they have 24 48 hours I think it kind of depends on the project and they'll just nitpick it's like really final moment of like fixing things look it doesn't matter how senior you are if you're staring at the same set of pixels before long you're going to start to miss things but the value isn't just proofreading or increasing the quality of the product it's also leveling up the abilities of the entire org we kind of did a really retro on it recently with the team and the panel I asked the panel ahe how is it different now than it was a year ago when you're reviewing those designs and they're saying that you know they used to have 20 30 comments on the file Al together a year ago and now it's five six seven and it's really just the designers have all the feedback they got now they incorp into their work from you know the early stages and so it just makes better designers as well.

**Для Напарника:** Перед сдачей проверять отступы, консистентность и тексты кнопок (глаголы) по чек-листу, как polish review; методология проверки из CLAUDE.md §6.



# 🎬 How the best designers tackle complex design challenges — Dive Club 🤿


### Выписать все вопросы и найти ключевые «поворотные»  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/kMWdmnQtJu0?t=22)
**Мысль:** Для сложной проблемы перечисляют все вопросы, большие и маленькие, и ищут те, ответ на которые снимает остальные.

> Approach like a big open-ended problem is I sort of like just like write down all the questions that we have and all the questions that we need to solve and I actually remember doing this with fig Jam we had this messy page in our figma file that just listed like all of the different questions that we had and that we knew we would have to solve before launching this spatial writing has pretty quickly become my favorite way to think especially when dealing with really complex design challenges the meteor the problem the more likely I am to start my design process in fig Jam it's big questions like what is the relationship between figma and fig jam and small questions like how do you manipulate text like really being exhaustive there your goal is to figure out which questions do you have to answer that unlock answers to other questions as well I will go through and sort of look at the questions and say like what are the questions here that really feel like once we crack them all the answers to all the questions other questions will sort of be easy to answer and very obvious these Tipping Point questions are often referred to as igen questions and they're a key way to frame problems more effectively.


### Показывать варианты на спектре: сначала крайности, потом середина  ·  [02:19](https://youtu.be/kMWdmnQtJu0?t=139)
**Мысль:** Вместо шести разрозненных макетов показывают два-три направления на шкале (например, насколько выразительным быть: от утилитарного до чрезмерного); крайности помогают увидеть, что даёт оптимизация одного свойства.

> Now here's the mistake I see designers make too often let's say you're exploring one of these Tipping Point questions and you narrow in on like six different concepts that you think show a lot of potential now the last thing that you want to do is present these as six separate Concepts to the team here's Alex Cornell explaining why if you're just sharing a like a whole bunch of work like what do you guys think of this and you don't really make it clear what you were really toiling over you're going to get feedback all over the place so I think it's very important to establish and make very clear the framework that was guiding you when you did the thing so that it can sort of create an architecture around the ideas that you show that is very clear to people so that it's not like just getting a whole bunch of mocks thrown at you actually help them understand what is distinct about this idea why is it even being included in this combination and where does it sit on this little map that I'm going to draw for you that's why I'm such a big fan of presenting my work on a spectrum if we do a good job of identifying those Tipping Point questions then you can anchor all of your Explorations along that Spectrum now all of a sudden instead of looking at six isolated mockups you're presenting two or three highlevel directions that your team can take and the key is to paint the edges first and that's exactly what Jenny did while working on fig Jam like there's just two extremes so for example how fun and expressive should fig Jam be and you know there could be one side that's like super not expressive very utilitarian and one that's like over the top and I usually think with most design questions it's actually never the polar opposite it's always like somewhere in between the fine line is like figuring out where it is in that middle but I think just pushing the extremes lets you see like hey if we just optimize for one certain thing what does this actually design Direction look like when I'm presenting my ideas at crit or maybe just making a simple loom video to drop it in slack I'll often start my presentation with a simple visual representation of my Spectrum now this helps people understand my mental models before we dive into specific Concepts.

**Для Напарника:** При выборе тона и плотности эффектов (насколько живым делать Мир) двигаться по шкале «спокойно ↔ ярко», а не выбирать единичный вариант.


### Приходить с мнением: чёткая позиция вынуждает реакцию  ·  [05:08](https://youtu.be/kMWdmnQtJu0?t=308)
**Мысль:** В Instagram не давали переложить решение на других: если нет мнения, просят вернуться, когда оно появится. Ясная позиция заставляет людей реагировать.

> The importance of having an opinion when presenting your work has definitely been the theme over the last 80 or so episodes Rich Arnold even said it was one of the main lessons that he learned after he left Vine and moved over to Instagram I think I had this habit of just sort of coming in with like a menu of solutions and being like what do you think yeah you want to pick one and at Instagram in general at Facebook like they're very good at not letting you take that decision off of your plate and put it on to them so if you come in and you don't have an opinion on what's right they'll just tell you to come back when you think you know what the right thing to do is and you have to come back and present why this is the right thing to do and the reason is when you have a clear point of view it forces people to react.

**Для Напарника:** Согласуется с принципом «Default Effect»: продукт сам предлагает вариант (25 минут, рекомендуем), а не выдаёт меню.


### «Это может выглядеть примерно так» и высокая точность как фонарь  ·  [09:35](https://youtu.be/kMWdmnQtJu0?t=575)
**Мысль:** Оговорка о возможности снимает придирки к пикселям; при этом реалистичные прототипы дают лучший отзыв, потому что вещь выглядит и ощущается настоящей.

> Have always been good at telling an inspiring story showing what's possible but not showing what will be only showing what could be that's why when I'm exploring early Concepts the phrase that I use the most is it could look something like this that tiny little disclaimer is a powerful communication tool and a great way to avoid visual nitpicking now I know what you're thinking wouldn't it be more obvious if the designs were low Fidelity and possibly but again you get better feedback when something looks and feels real when you're exploring a cave High Fidelity prototypes serve as the brightest flashlights.



# 🎬 How to ALWAYS Stay Motivated (if you have ADHD) — ADHDVision


### Конкретная подотчётность: кто, когда, что, на сколько  ·  [11:29](https://youtu.be/LqouZ6in6yY?t=689)
**Мысль:** Расплывчатое «поработаю сегодня» слабо; чёткие время, активность и длительность с другими создают социальное обязательство и идентичность «я из тех, кто приходит».

> Make accountability specific. This is the one most people miss. Let's say you're looking for a job in the age of AI. Telling someone I'm going to work on this today doesn't do much even if you're sitting in that cafe. Motivation may fade because your ADHD brain doesn't just run on intention, it runs on identity. We follow through on what people like us are doing. When you're working with random people at random times, there is no signal for who you are in that moment. But the moment you say I'll be on a call at 2:00 p.m. with other people who are also submitting job applications and I'll be on that call until 2:50 p.m., something fundamental happens. You've just made the time and activity you're working on specific. Specific accountability has four elements your brain can actually process. Who, when, what, and for how long.

**Для Напарника:** Старт сессии как маленькое обещание напарнику: что и на сколько минут; кот «ждёт к 15:00».



# 🎬 How to be great at systems thinking — Dive Club 🤿


### Антипаттерн: идеальное локальное решение с «делайтом» сверху  ·  [00:58](https://youtu.be/MC5jAxh0ymA?t=58)
**Мысль:** Дизайнеры по умолчанию берут одну проблему, строят идеальную модель внутри пузыря и добавляют красоту сверху, не думая о связях.

> Mode for a lot of designers when they work on problems is they want to kind of okay let's focus on this one problem and then make a really good solutions for that and then we add all the delight and all the Crazy Craft on top of it they focus on this thing they come up with like a ideal sing Single model that does everything within that bubble.


### Решение каждой проблемы отдельной фичей усложняет систему  ·  [01:29](https://youtu.be/MC5jAxh0ymA?t=89)
**Мысль:** Продукт можно видеть как набор блоков-примитивов; точечные фичи сверху множат сложность.

> It was mostly system thinking building the blocks when you're building a product that serves so many different groups of users so many different use cases you can't think in one way or the other way you need to come up with good systems to tie up all this different people's needs people's mental models of how their tools work different variations of doing say project management but then it's like still kind of built with the same ideas Primitives building block you can think of most digital products as a collection of blocks but if you solve each product problem in isolation then you'll end up creating a new feature or in this case new block for each solution you can't build single purpose features in notion and try to slap it on top of it cuz it will make the systems more complex.

**Для Напарника:** Новые механики Напарника (реплики, чек-ин, ориентиры) строить из общих примитивов (пузырь, награда, ориентир), а не добавлять отдельные виджеты.


### Успех: минимум уникальных блоков, которые делают максимум  ·  [02:26](https://youtu.be/MC5jAxh0ymA?t=146)
**Мысль:** Лучшие дизайнеры решают корневые проблемы и предвидят будущие нужды, добиваясь системы с наименьшим числом частей.

> Even if you're not designing a general purpose tool like notion the best designers dig deep to understand root level issues instead of solving each new problem as it sprouts that's what allows systems thinkers to see the entire set of blocks as well as anticipate future product needs from that vantage point success isn't just solving all the problems it's figuring out the broader product puzzle that using the least number of unique blocks ideally the system has the fewest parts that does the most things you want.

**Для Напарника:** Перед добавлением нового экрана проверять, нельзя ли решить задачу существующим примитивом (чат, награда, остров).


### Локальное решение против примитива (пример с артефактами Claude)  ·  [03:11](https://youtu.be/MC5jAxh0ymA?t=191)
**Мысль:** Кнопка «свернуть» решает локальную проблему, а новая абстракция вроде артефактов растёт и решает будущие задачи; дизайнер должен увидеть и отстоять примитив.

> Best features are the ones that solve multiple problems at once that are simple abstractions that can grow into ones that solve future problems and so part of shaping strategy I think is proposing a solution that is not just let's take artifacts as an example because we just talked about it there is a user problem which is that it's hard to write documents with Claude because it takes up your entire screen you can't actually see claude's commentary Etc you could find 10 different solutions that would solve that problem some of those Solutions are the seeds of A New Concept a new abstraction a new thing that you can build on and I think it's designer jobs to see that and advocate for it so you know to paint stupid ends of the spectrum you could just have a collapse button that takes long documents and hides them in the chat that would be like a local solution to a problem but it is not a of compounding abstract concept that you can build off a primitive in the product.

**Для Напарника:** Остров как примитив прогресса может нести будущие механики (редкие находки, ориентиры), вместо отдельных счётчиков и значков.


### Проектировать настройки как интероперабельные компоненты  ·  [05:11](https://youtu.be/MC5jAxh0ymA?t=311)
**Мысль:** Автор проектировал экран настроек, думая, что те же действия потом появятся в чек-листах онбординга и модалках, поэтому сделал переиспользуемую систему.

> Zooming out to consider the full system is how you preserve Simplicity as your fature surface area grows an example for my time at Maven is when I was working on settings now I could have designed that set of screens in isolation but in the back of my head I knew that we would probably want to surface some of these actions in future onboarding checklists and maybe even in modals on different feature pages and so I thought through this interoperable component system that would allow us to easily showcase each configuration in ways that I hadn't even thought of yet.


### Продавать идею через то, что она открывает для бизнеса  ·  [08:21](https://youtu.be/MC5jAxh0ymA?t=501)
**Мысль:** Редизайн два года не проходил, пока автор не показал, что он открывает маркетинговые возможности; чем больше поверхностей затрагивает идея, тем больше людей заинтересовано.

> But you know what's really effective when you can lead with the ROI of a feature and show how it unlocks new opportunities in the product for instance I wanted to redesign the syllabus editor at Maven for like two whole years and I failed multiple times to get buying my breakthrough though was showing how it could unlock new types of marketing opportunities for instructors like maybe allowing students to preview lessons on the landing page now not only is the team getting more bang for their Buck but the more surface area that your idea touches the more people have the potential to be excited about it because all of a sudden it's easier for them to see how it impacts their slice of the product.



# 🎬 How to Build a Friend: AI Companionship and Its Game Design Potential | AI and Games Conference — AI and Games Conference


### Цель — тренировка эмоциональных навыков; непрерывность, осознание привязанности, якоря реальности  ·  [14:13](https://youtu.be/FiGUKTILfQQ?t=853)
**Мысль:** Не создавать зависимость, а давать площадку для эмпатии; прозрачность обновлений, внимание к привязанности, связь с реальным миром, проектирование под уязвимого пользователя.

> I believe that the goal should not be to create dependence or to replace human connection, but to use AI as training grounds for emotional engagement and empathy. And users who see AI this way actually tend to have a more positive experience. Like they are not ashamed of being connected to AI, they feel like they it helped them to be to feel better in their social life, for example, and they're openly sharing this type of stuff. And at Wargaming, we currently also try and explore AI to explore AI as a collaborator, but not a replacement both in our tools and further down the road probably player experiences, too. So, there are some practical things that can be used to create safe and responsible AI experiences. It all boils down to basically caring about your user, but there are some practical things that can be used. The first thing is concerned in continuity. I believe that we owe users to give them transparency and notice about model updates and maybe even given the give them a choice to use the model that they prefer. We had this in replica when people chatted with a model that we thought that was long outdated, but they had some attachment to it. They loved chatting with it. Why not? the next thing is attachment awareness is understanding that this attachment will happen and maybe towards healthy habits away from dependency. next thing is reality anchors. It's connected with the previous one and it's connecting your AI conversation to some real world stuff. For example, if it's a companion, it can bring up some ideas of what person wants to do in their outer world outside world. And this way helping to stay in touch with reality. And the last thing is ethics and enforcement. I believe that it should not just be ethics. We have to actively design and enforce these protocols. And the idea is that we should think that anyone can use our AI system. For example, a person who does not understand very well how AI works. Or the person can who can be in crisis in some vulnerable state of mind. And we have to think how they will interact with the system and what will be the flow with which we reach our goal.

**Для Напарника:** Кот приводит разговор к реальным шагам вне экрана; прозрачность изменений; продумать кризисные формулировки.



# 🎬 How to Build Meaningful Animations | Louis Ansa Art Director at makemepulse — awwwards.


### Простая центральная идея — основа сильной системы анимаций  ·  [08:59](https://youtu.be/Npu68jiGG94?t=539)
**Мысль:** Чем проще концепт, тем легче строить на нём систему; автор взял идею «тройного наложения» и применил к заголовкам, изображениям, переходам.

> I need to find a concept and I thought maybe the best thing is to find a simple concept which can embed everything you think about and the simpler your concept is the easier it's going to be for you to build them upon it a strong system.

**Для Напарника:** Один концепт Напарника («присутствие рядом», рост острова) применять ко всем анимациям, а не набор разрозненных эффектов.


### Чистая раскладка, анимация только при взаимодействии  ·  [11:32](https://youtu.be/Npu68jiGG94?t=692)
**Мысль:** Автор признал, что трендовый эффект не принадлежит проекту; вывод: макет чистый, анимация включается взаимодействием и добавляет слой информации.

> Fell for a trap and on the rollover I used this very trendy WebGL displacement animation and it's not I tried to build the animation system but this is a flaw and it doesn't belong here but it was cool so I just put it in so what did I learn from it is that building my portfolio my walk was speaking for myself and I didn't need to do some very complex layout and animation that the layout should be clean and it's only when you interact with elements that you should trigger animation and add a new layer of information otherwise should be very simple and have fun.

**Для Напарника:** Контингентность по CLAUDE.md: заметное движение только как ответ на событие, а не по таймеру.


### Собери собственные правила анимации, как типографические  ·  [15:02](https://youtu.be/Npu68jiGG94?t=902)
**Мысль:** Набор простых правил движения применяется последовательно, как правила типографики, там, где они уместны.

> Thing you should remember is that you should come up with your own rules and use them like in your toolkit and use them similarly to typographic rules with systems and Yaqui and use them whenever they feel like they should belong to in your project.

**Для Напарника:** Зафиксировать в CLAUDE.md короткий набор правил движения (длительности 7–23с для фона, easing, stagger).



# 🎬 How to Choose the Best Colors for UI/UX Design | Color Theory, Contrast & Accessibility Tips — Chandini Creates


### Не менять оттенок целиком - подкрутить lightness в HSL до прохождения контраста  ·  [04:20](https://youtu.be/SdWDrFqkax0?t=260)
**Мысль:** Если оттенок не проходит стандарт, достаточно снизить светлоту, сохранив цвет.

> So what you can do is instead of searching for a different shade or all together you can also try to tweak the hsl values here so I just reduced the lightness and you can see that now it passes dou large and dou normal as well as AA a large.

**Для Напарника:** Тот же приём подгонки токенов под контраст на фоне Напарника, не меняя характер цвета.


### Тональная палитра вместо случайных оттенков для hover и фонов  ·  [07:39](https://youtu.be/SdWDrFqkax0?t=459)
**Мысль:** Если для каждого состояния подбирать оттенок на глаз, дизайн теряет цельность; нужна шкала 50-900.

> Us take an example let's say you're making a button you know that when you make a button you also need a hover state right now for hover State let's say you want the button background to be a little darker so what you do is you would want to select a darker shade now let's say you're just going to pick a darker shade randomly like this now what happens is over time for different elements when you want different shades sometimes you might want a lighter shade for background for your toast messages or maybe for your input Fields so at times like that if you start picking colors randomly Your Design will not feel cohesive so what you need to do is set up a tonal palette.

**Для Напарника:** Завести шкалу токенов для кнопок и состояний Фокуса вместо ручных значений.



# 🎬 How to do Basic Animation from a beginner to a beginner — Bluebiscuits


### В анимации цель — движение, а не идеальный кадр  ·  [00:57](https://youtu.be/Al_k9hmd4ws?t=57)
**Мысль:** Анимация это другой способ видеть искусство: простые формы, без идеальных кадров, главное — чтобы двигалось.

> The first thing you have to do is let go of what you know about drawing like illustrations. Animation is a whole other way of seeing art. Try not to follow the rules you'd use for an illustration. Don't think about making every single frame look perfect or adding maximum exaggeration and shedding. It's literally about making it as easy as possible with the simplest shapes. Movement is the goal, not the masterpiece.

**Для Напарника:** При анимации кота и острова начинать с простых форм и ритма; не полировать статичные кадры, пока движение не ощущается живым.


### Планируй по ключевым кадрам: старт и конец, потом промежуточные  ·  [02:47](https://youtu.be/Al_k9hmd4ws?t=167)
**Мысль:** Сначала фиксируешь начальную и конечную позу, затем добавляешь промежуточные кадры, которые делают движение плавным. Можно взять лишь один кадр рядом со стартом и один рядом с концом.

> The simplest way to plan is by focusing on the start frame and the end frame. This is the main movement you want your character or object to be in. Get those two perfect and then you add the in-betweens to smooth everything out. This makes it much easier to plan out. My animations will look something like this. start and end frame planned out and then I go in and add the inbetweens. The in-betweens are the frames that allow the animation to actually move. So, it will look a bit choppy and then it won't. I'm pretty lazy, so I usually just do one frame like slightly off the start and then closer to the end frame. I don't really do the ones in the middle cuz I think this works just fine.

**Для Напарника:** Анимации кота (проснулся, подпрыгнул) задавать двумя позами, а плавность добавлять easing-ом в CSS/Framer Motion, а не набором промежуточных кадров.


### Учись, разбирая чужую анимацию покадрово и считая кадры  ·  [05:21](https://youtu.be/Al_k9hmd4ws?t=321)
**Мысль:** Замедление работ мастеров и подсчёт использованных кадров быстро объясняет, как строить тайминг.

> What really helped me grasp when to use it is to go frame by frame on animations I like and then count the frames that this animator has used. It's completely unlock something in my brain and really helps me like visualize what I'm supposed to be doing. I slowed down Minbits animations on YouTube. They're really cool and she uses like really fastpaced animations. I highly recommend trying this. If you're using YouTube, you can use the comma and the dot key to go frame by frame on a video. H and then you can count. I know counting isn't that interesting, but it's very important.

**Для Напарника:** Перед созданием анимации кота замедлять эталонные анимации (нужный характер) и считать мс фаз, чтобы переносить в тайминги.


### Самое полезное: замедлять и изучать чужие работы и просто начать  ·  [09:01](https://youtu.be/Al_k9hmd4ws?t=541)
**Мысль:** Изучение работ других плюс практика сняли страх перед анимацией; после старта оказалось не так страшно.

> Think slowing down and studying other people's work has helped the most along with just practicing because once I got started, I realized it wasn't as scary as I thought it was.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип запуска применим и к пользователю: страшно только до первого шага; формулировать первый шаг очень маленьким.



# 🎬 How to Get Stuff Done with ADHD Part 1: Task Initiation | Neurodivergent Magic — The Neurocuriosity Club


### Шаг 1: выгрузи хаос на бумагу за 3 минуты, и только потом приоритизируй  ·  [02:22](https://youtu.be/WQuYHPg3Tjo?t=142)
**Мысль:** Выписанное можно двигать глазами и руками, а не держать в голове; приоритизация во время выгрузки только путает и вызывает самооценку.

> Is getting the jumble out of your head. Here's what I want you to do. Set a timer for three minutes, and then I want you to pull up a pad of paper, pull up your laptop, whatever, and just start writing. I want you to try to stay focused on the task that you're trying to accomplish and just write whatever thoughts come to mind around that topic. But if your thoughts wander, let them. The idea is to get the jumble out of your brain and onto the paper. It doesn't really matter what's in the jumble, you just need to write it down so that you can manipulate it with your eyes and your hands, and you can focus on it in a very literal way as opposed to trying to deal with it all in your head. Then and only then can you start to prioritize.

**Для Напарника:** Режим «выгрузить мысли» в чате: таймер на 3 минуты и сортировка потом, а не одновременно.


### Один шаг, не весь план; начинай там, где реально начнёшь  ·  [03:01](https://youtu.be/WQuYHPg3Tjo?t=181)
**Мысль:** Полный план рушится при первой неожиданности; лучшее место старта не самое логичное, а то, где ты действительно начнёшь.

> This is something so many ADHDers struggle with. Prioritization is one of the eight executive functions that we tend to struggle with and many of us try to prioritize while we're brain dumping. Did you notice yourself doing it when you did the free write? You were trying to organize what needed done first and then second and then third, and that just made it more confusing and you started judging yourself and struggling really hard just to write something down. Yeah, try the free write again, and this time do not try to prioritize. Just write down what your thoughts are. Once you've done that, then you can start to prioritize. So how do you start to prioritize? Personally, I really like the idea of just one foot in front of the other, so you start with one step. Don't plan out the whole task because odds are something's going to go awry in the middle, and then your whole list is going to be pointless and you're going to feel like you failed even though you didn't. Instead, just pick what needs done first and PS little bonus tip, the best place to start for you is the best place to start for you. It doesn't necessarily mean the most logical place to start, the most efficient place to start or the place other people might start. It's the place that you will actually start.

**Для Напарника:** Breakdown предлагает один первый шаг и разрешает выбрать «лёгкий вход» даже если это не самое логичное.



# 🎬 How To Make Change Work | BJ Fogg | Tiny Habits — Dr. Maika Steinborn


### Focus Map: выбирай поведения с высоким влиянием и высокой выполнимостью («золотые»)  ·  [03:55](https://youtu.be/54Nxzhwja7E?t=235)
**Мысль:** Оценка вклада и реализуемости (смогу ли в самый трудный день, хочу ли, а не должен) отсеивает идеальные, но нереальные привычки.

> Now you want to take a closer look at the swarm of bees that you've collected and examine each one for its impact and feasibility and then you'll Place each behavior on this map this Focus map as BJ fog calls it pack means to ask how much will this Behavior actually contribute to achieving my outcome and feasibility is the question of can I get myself to do it given my resources my ability can I do it and do I want to do it is there more of a motivation and a want-to behind it than a should can you see yourself doing this even on the hardest day of the week and the habits that are most promising the ones you want to select are those in the top right corner with the highest impact and feasibility and BJ fog calls them the golden Behavior.

**Для Напарника:** При выборе первого шага предлагать «самый выполнимый и самый полезный»: вопрос «смог(ла) бы ты это сделать в самый тяжёлый день?»



# 🎬 How to Make OCs People Care About — Ullaiin


### Цикл: личность, затем предыстория по теме, затем уточнение личности  ·  [09:23](https://youtu.be/nITc8FP0zyY?t=563)
**Мысль:** Сначала базовая личность, затем предыстория вокруг общей темы, затем уточнение личности через неё; цикл повторяется. Это даёт согласованность и подсказки для идей.

> That's why I think backstory and personality are so intertwined. And if you're writing an OC, you should try to build them both together. A lot of the time, I'll start with a character's base personality, figuring out generally how they act, so I have an idea of who they are in the present before looking to the past. After that point is when I think about backstory, usually considering a general theme I want to go for. For example, the original concept for my character Jun was a Yakuza turned babysitter, and I wanted his personality to kind of be like a stereotypical delinquent. From that concept, I figured I wanted his backstory and general character to revolve around themes of toxic masculinity and discrimination. Since the Yakuza is focused on hierarchy and is male focused, whereas caring for children is often seen as women's work, I could use this as a basis for those themes. I also consider the history of Koreans in the Yakuza and how discrimination against Koreans in Japan pushed many of them into joining the Yakuza due to lack of opportunities. From this, I was able to write his backstory. That being how poor circumstances living in Japan as a Korean pushed him to join the Yakuza and how he developed his toxic traits from both the culture of the Yakuza and the need to appear strong in order to avoid discrimination or being looked down on. You can already see how his backstory feeds into his character, defining the way he acts. And having a base idea of what themes I want to touch on, all aspects of his backstory and personality can feed into that and stay consistent. Using that backstory as a basis, I was then able to refine his personality more. Deciding to make him someone who often lies about his achievements and tries to appear cool at all times, which makes sense due to his backstory of needing to be tough in order to avoid ridicule, which was reinforced both by his upbringing and prior profession. Having that system of making a base personality, deciding a backstory, and then refining the personality using the backstory has been a great system for me to be able to figure out what a character's backstory should be and how to keep their character consistent.

**Для Напарника:** Описать для кота центральную тему (присутствие без надзора) и выводить из неё реплики, привычки и жесты.



# 🎬 How to Make Your Vibecoded Mobile App Feel Human | Product Delight — Build Native Mobile apps with AI


### Иерархия потребностей: приятность на вершине и дешёвые нижние слои  ·  [01:28](https://youtu.be/Hvnk76SKmtc?t=88)
**Мысль:** Функциональность, надёжность и юзабилити теперь дёшевы, поэтому отличать продукт будет четвёртый слой — приятность. Раньше его считали вишенкой на торте.

> I want to share with you a framework that actually is almost 20 years old. So nothing very new about it. It's called the hierarchy of users need. Maybe you've heard about it. It's developed and designed by Aaron Walter. Aaron Walter used to be the head of design at the Mailchimp. And he actually came with this framework saying that for every product to hit in a good way market and the user, it has to go through four layers. So, the first layer is functionality, meaning that the product has to function. It has to work, right? I mean, that's the basic. it has to be reliable, meaning that it has to work all the time for all conditions for all condition and infrastructure. And then, the third layer is about usability. The product has to be easy to use. And that's extremely important, and it's really important to design things in a way that they are easy to use. But what's even more important from this framework is that Aaron said that the top of pyramid is pleasurability. So, the product has to be pleasurable to be used. And a lot of people has been wondering, what does it actually mean to build something pleasurable? And also, the other problem is that it sounded like this comes like the luxury part. It's that cherry on the top. It's the icing on the cake. Who actually cares? And more importantly, that for couple of years ago, building the first three layers has been extremely expensive. Like you need to hire an entire dev team. You need to raise funds. You need to get an office. And so, getting to the fourth layer sounded like a dream, and it was like abandoned for many years. The problem that we're seeing, which is by the way a positive problem that we're living, is that the access to the first three layers is becoming cheaper. It's becoming easier. We can build product in like hackathon days and hours. And so, what will distinguish, what will differentiate your mobile app or your product, is going to be that pleasurability.

**Для Напарника:** Эмоциональная отделка Напарника (кот, остров, тон) — не украшение, а главное конкурентное преимущество.

### Чувство времени подводит: сначала узнай, сколько реально занимают дела  ·  [03:49](https://youtu.be/siHBuHdOhCY?t=229)
**Мысль:** Использование старой оценки времени ведёт к вечной перегрузке плана; первым делом надо измерить настоящую длительность.

> One of the things that I think is true for a lot of us with ADHD is our sense of time is pretty lousy. Right? You're like: Oh yeah, that'll take me ten minutes. And that task is never taking you ten minutes. EVER!!! Right? JESSICA: Ever! BOTH: Ever! Ever! ERIC: And so if you start just jumping into weekly planning, but you're using your old sense of time You know, the part that tells you those fifteen minute tasks will only take you five minutes And the thing that you think will take you half-an-hour actually takes a good part of a day You're going to always feel overwhelmed because you're going to always have too much on your plate. So, the first step -- if you're trying to build something big So whether it's a Youtube channel, or trying to structure your home, or trying to get through college Whatever it is. The first thing you have to know is how long do things actually take you.

**Для Напарника:** Сохранять реальную длительность сессий и со временем предлагать подходящую («обычно тебе хватает 25 минут»).


### Таймер вверх (count-up) вместо обратного отсчёта, чтобы знать реальное время  ·  [04:39](https://youtu.be/siHBuHdOhCY?t=279)
**Мысль:** Обратный отсчёт доходит до нуля и ничего не сообщает о реальной длительности; прямой отсчёт измеряет её.

> So what you want to do is take out your standard to-do list, have your task written down, and then look at the task and say: How long do I think this task is going to take me? Say it's writing an email. It's going to take you five minutes. So what you do then is set a timer that actually goes up. So, not a count-down timer. Because if you set a count-down timer, and it gets to zero, and you're still working on that email, How're you gonna know how long it actually took you? So use a count-up timer, stop the timer when you're done with the task, and then write down how long it actually took.

**Для Напарника:** Для калибровки времени в Напарнике: после сессии показывать факт и прежнюю оценку, без оценки «хорошо/плохо».

# 🎬 How to pitch your designs like a pro — Dive Club 🤿


### Строить рассказ вокруг одного пикового момента, а не 20 идей  ·  [01:06](https://youtu.be/w3CtCzW9G8s?t=66)
**Мысль:** Руководитель запомнит одну-две идеи из 20-минутной презентации, поэтому нужно осознанно выбрать пик и проектировать вокруг него, иначе слушатель выберет сам, и не обязательно главное.

> The biggest mistake that you can make is starting to craft your presentation by pulling from all of the various visual artifacts that you've made over the last few weeks of exploration they'll sort of use these visuals as like their Q card they'll see it on screen they'll respond to it they'll react to it and then they'll repeat and they'll go to the next slide they'll look at what's on the slide they'll decide what they're going to say so you get a lot of content but it's not cohesive for someone who hasn't been living and breathing this project it gets really hard to follow that's why Chris follows a different approach when crafting a presentation he starts with words not visuals because the reality is that product leaders will probably only remember one I mean maybe two different ideas from your 20 minute presentation so the key is to build your story around a peak moment that emphasizes the exact idea that you want people to cling to if you can figure out what your Peak moment is in your presentation and then do that in pick that intentionally and then design around that's much more effective otherwise what happens is you throw 20 different ideas at me and then I decide which one I want to hang on to that might not be the most important one to you.

> Beyond AI as an assistant, I believe that AI should be a tool for thought. AI should challenge, not obey. And I believe that right at this moment, we are at a critical juncture where the world of work is poised to be transformed by generative AI, and we must act now to shape and drive that transformation towards humanistic values. Of these two diverging roads, we must take the one less traveled. Beyond getting the job done, a tool for thought helps us better understand the job. Beyond getting it done faster, it helps us get it done better. Beyond getting us to the right answers, a tool for thought helps us ask the right questions. Beyond automating known processes, it helps us explore the unknown.
