# 🎬 How To Think Like A Game Designer — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Не копируй механику, не поняв, почему она работает  ·  [00:00](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=0)
**Мысль:** Автосейв по чекпоинтам работал в Call of Duty, но убил страх в Alien: Isolation: игроки без опаски бродили, зная, что потеряют пару минут.

> Okay, so during the development of Alien: Isolation, the game had a pretty basic save system. It would automatically cache your data whenever you tripped invisible checkpoints, which were dotted throughout each level. It was added because it was easy to implement, simple to understand - and, in truth, because that's how saving worked in pretty much every game. Design director Gary Napper said "like any development team, we all play a lot of games and each have our favourites. Often our decisions and choices are coloured by the games we play". But... as development went along, the team started to question if this save system was actually the right choice. You see, because players knew that if they died they'd only lose a couple minutes of progress, they would just waltz around the space station without a care in the world. Not ideal... in a horror game. *Alien scream* So while these checkpoints might have worked in Call of Duty and Bioshock - Napper says it was "not the right approach when making a game that is designed from its core to terrify and put people on edge". Because here's the thing. It's totally fine to take influence from other games. Many designers borrow mechanics from their favourite titles - often evolving, combining, and remixing them into something new. But: it’s essential to understand why those mechanics work in one game, before copying and pasting them into yours.

**Для Напарника:** Перед заимствованием механики (стрик, сгорающий прогресс) проверять, какое поведение и чувство она даст в контексте СДВГ-продукта.


### MDA: механики, динамика, эстетика (чувства)  ·  [01:33](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=93)
**Мысль:** Механика (правила) порождает динамику (действия игрока) и эстетику (эмоции). Меняя число (запас патронов), каскадом меняешь поведение и чувства.

> Alright, so... way back in 2004, , three game designers got together and wrote an academic paper, featuring a framework that would prove to be perfect for analysing the mechanics in games. It's called MDA, and it breaks games down into three, neat sections: mechanics, dynamics, and aesthetics. Mechanics describes the actual workings of the game: like the rules, the systems, what the buttons do, and all of the individual stats and numbers. For example, one mechanic could be the maximum amount of ammo the player can hold - and we could set that number really high. Then, dynamics is how the player acts and behaves, in response to those mechanics. So because the player has tonnes of ammo, they may storm into battle, and shoot wildly in the general direction of every enemy they spot. And, finally, aesthetics - not to be confused with the graphics or the art style. Here, aesthetics is how the player feels when acting in that way. Their emotional response. So this wild shooting could make you feel reckless, powerful, unstoppable - basically, like this. *Machine gun fire* To put it another way - mechanics happen in the code, dynamics happen in the player's actions, and aesthetics happen in the player's feelings. Now, unfortunately, game designers can't just go inside the player's head and directly alter their actions or feelings. But they can change the code - and because MDA describes a casual relationship, these changes will cascade down and alter the dynamics and aesthetics. For example, if we tweak that knob the other way, and massively limit the player's ammo stash, that's definitely going to change the player's behaviour. Now, they will act cautiously before heading into battle, or might not engage enemies at all. Every shot fired will be done with careful consideration, and more time will be spent hunting for ammo in the environment. And this will lead to completely different aesthetics: like disempowerment, fear, and caution. So, this MDA framework takes mechanics - and then puts them into a wider context, which asks 'how do they make the player act, and how do they make the player feel?'.

**Для Напарника:** Анализировать каждую механику Напарника по цепочке: правило, что делает человек, что он при этом чувствует (облегчение, гордость, давление).


### Называй чувства точнее, чем «было весело»  ·  [04:37](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=277)
**Мысль:** Трудность анализа в формулировке подсознательных ощущений: нужны сильные эмоции (мощный, любопытный, напряжённый, растерянный), а не «понравилось».

> If there's one hard part of this analysis process, it can be putting words to the subconscious feelings that arise when playing games. We're looking for way more than just "this game was fun", - we're looking for strong, emotive feelings like, this game made me feel powerful, creative, sneaky, tense, intimidated, curious, deceitful, cooperative, flustered.

**Для Напарника:** В интервью с пользователями просить описывать чувство конкретными словами, а не оценкой.


### Одна фраза-видение как маяк для решений  ·  [05:52](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=352)
**Мысль:** Subnautica: «thrill of the unknown», RE Village: «struggle to survive». Короткая формулировка позволяет оценивать и перебалансировать механики.

> That's why it's useful to truly understand your game's vision - the overarching feeling or experience you want to give the player. Because if you know you want to make the player feel, say, scared, then you know you'll want to pick mechanics that induce feelings of fear, dread, isolation, and disempowerment. That vision could be a single statement: Subnautica was based around the phrase "thrill of the unknown", and every mechanic had to suit this idea. Resident Evil Village was built under the banner of "struggle to survive" - and so when playtesters hated the game's messy combat, the designers could use that phrase as, like, a lodestar to rebalance and rethink the game's mechanics. The vision could also be a fantasy that you're trying to deliver. Perhaps you want the player to feel like Batman, or an assassin, or a world leader. It could be a feeling - like how Flower tried to make you feel relaxed. Or it could be a specific experience - when making FTL, the devs said their starting point was "we wanted to recreate the atmosphere of commanding a starship".

**Для Напарника:** Закрепить для Напарника фразу вроде «тёплое присутствие рядом, без вины» и сверять с ней каждую новую фичу.


### Сменили сейв — сменилась эмоция  ·  [10:58](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=658)
**Мысль:** Ручные сейвы с задержкой сделали сохранение напряжённым и усилили ужас. Одна механика полностью переписала поведение и чувства игроков.

> So, going back to Alien: Isolation - the team eventually tried a different save system. Something closer to older games. Now the game features a small number of save points that you have to operate manually. And - to make things worse - it takes a while for the save point to turn on, and for your game to actually save... giving the alien a chance to sneak up and kill you. And this new mechanic changed everything - completely altering the player's behaviours and emotions. "We were afraid," says Napper. "If we didn’t make it to the save point and successfully save, we would lose our progress. Saving became tense. Looking for a save became tense. The simple act of saving had become supportive to the game's driving factors of terror and isolation".

**Для Напарника:** Пример силы малой механики: сохранение и завершение сессии в Напарнике должны ощущаться как облегчение, а не как риск потери.



# 🎬 How to Work with Non-Designers | Proven UX Design Process — Stuart Studios


### Четыре категории риска (Каган): usability, feasibility, business, value  ·  [03:28](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=208)
**Мысль:** Дизайнер обычно зациклен на usability-риске, а команда думает о четырёх рисках сразу, и они пересекаются; общий язык рисков выравнивает команду.

> Number one is rally around the same risks so when you're designing a building product every decision that you make is going to carry some amount of risk and in marty kagan's book inspired he explicitly calls out four major risk categories and i've been using his framework for years now and anecdotally it has been so helpful for my day-to-day work not only actual designing piece of it but in influencing and communicating and collaborating all right so take a look here are the four risk categories i'm not going to go into real detail here i highly recommend that you check out the book inspired by marty kagan he does a great job of explaining these and going into detail and breaking everything down but for the purpose of this video and just also the way i communicate this in meetings and in presentations i'm going to do it that way just to make it easy so let's go bottom up usability risk this is about if the user can actually figure out what you designed or built pretty straightforward right this is kind of in the wheelhouse of designers now feasibility risk is more about capabilities can the builders engineers actually make it code it build it business risk can the business work with it i know that's a little bit vague but typically it deals with like legal financial you know maybe there's some regulatory or compliance thing going on and the most important probably is going to be value risk so even if everything else checks out and you design it and build it and put it out into the world will the users actually like really buy it or will they actually use it if you make it for them and it's helpful to know that these are all related it's they're not mutually exclusive so there's going to be overlaps also if you change one or you adjust anything it may impact some other risk category.

**Для Напарника:** Для решений по Напарнику взвешивать не только удобство, но и ценностный риск: будет ли человек реально возвращаться к острову и коту.


### Говори на языке рисков других ролей  ·  [05:07](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=307)
**Мысль:** Практика: учить язык бизнес-, ценностного и технического риска, понять какой риск важен какой роли, и свести команду к общему описанию смеси рисков.

> So the problem i want to point out here is that early on i had this issue and i see junior designers having this issue and it's that we focus too much on the usability risk now i'm not saying don't focus on it because it's important it's part of the equation but you can imagine other roles prioritizing and really thinking more about the other types of risks now this creates an issue when you're trying to get on the same page about what the problem is and how you're going to solve it so here's what i would suggest and this is something that i've practiced over the years and i am still working on so number one speak to the other types of risks practice it try to see if you can understand what a business risk is for your company what is a value risk for your product or what is a feasibility risk for the engineers that you work with and see if you can start practicing speaking to it intelligently and speaking in a language that they will understand if you talk design jargon you know for feasibility risk it probably won't be as effective and if you start talking design stuff to business stakeholders in the way you may talk to other designers that's probably not going to be effective either so you want to start practicing like speaking in a way that other people will understand and that leads me to tip number two try to figure out what types of risk is most important to what type of role so let's just take a product manager for example because they probably care about a lot of the risk that you care about however given a certain situation you know stakeholder situation or the budget or timeline certain other types of risk might be prioritized over usability now you need to be able to speak to that and also be able to speak to how your usability risk is an important factor or an overlap to everything else going on now that leads me to tip number three there's not going to be just one risk that the team cares about it's going to be a mix of risks and a different priority depending on the project it's your job to try to figure out what that is and be able to get the whole team to speak about the risk in the same way that's just i found it to be the best way to get the team aligned on the same page about what the problem is and how you're going to try to solve it and even then it's still difficult but i found that it just makes it way easier and it's pretty effective.


### Мелкие UX-компромиссы копятся; покажи будущее состояние  ·  [07:28](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=448)
**Мысль:** Мелкие решения (не тот цвет, кнопка чуть в стороне) складываются в долг; в контексте долгосрочного видения их эффект виден, а решения сегодня готовят завтрашний продукт.

> Align on a future vision and designers i think this one may sting a little bit but hear me out no one intentionally sets out to make a product of terrible ux it's it happens because there's real pressure to meet business outcomes and in those situations usability things can typically get overlooked or it's very easy to overlook them especially with the iterative nature of design and building these days it's even for designers i think it's hard to see what the big deal is of using an off-brand color or just rearranging things to squeeze stuff in or just placing a button slightly somewhere else just to get the job done but you and i know that you know those things compound over time and the effects of it the negative effects of it'll show in the medium to long term but the here and now it may not make a major difference and it may seem completely fine look so it's easier to see the impact of the smaller ux decisions if you place it in context of a more holistic and longer term vision and you can use your design skills to kind of draw that up and help people see what you're talking about because typically non-designer roles they probably have a hard time imagining what you're seeing as designers it may be easier for us and more natural to be able to piece things together and imagine what things could be but that's not true for a lot of other roles so this is where you can really be proactive and help drive alignment and strategy for the product and project that you're working on the other thing i'll mention is that then the usability risks become way more apparent when you're able to see the small little decisions and the impact of it in a future state so oftentimes do showing people what a decision may become is extremely powerful this is also going to be helpful for you as a designer because this is sort of playing chess with the future state of the product or feature that you may be working on so what i mean by this is the decision that you would make here and now just to solve the problem right now will likely be very different from a decision you would make to set yourself up to have the product become something else in the future so by doing this exercise and having that holistic vision the north star of where you're trying to go it's way easier to figure out what types of decisions you need to make sure that you have so that you set yourself up well.

**Для Напарника:** Держать north star Напарника (остров растёт, кот рядом) и сверять мелкие правки с ним, как уже делают жёсткие константы визуального языка.


### Принципы важнее пикселей: держись за ядро, гибок в деталях  ·  [09:53](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=593)
**Мысль:** В кросс-функциональной работе спорить о деталях бесполезно; нужно жёстко удерживать базовые концепции и ментальные модели, а цвет меню или типографику уступить (пример с закусочной и суши).

> Way more but hear me out because i really think this one's going to be helpful so amongst designers it's really helpful to talk about pixels it's rather productive and fun to do so iconography you know typography rounded corners placement of things is someone going to be confused about it how do they feel about it will they know how to use it if i design it that sort of thing and that's all important but in a cross-functional setting it's probably not going to be effective if you harp too hard on those things it's because when it comes to actually solving a problem for you know the product the feature for the business it's going to be way broader than just the usability risk so keep the pixels in mind for sure but when you're working cross-functionally you want to be flexible with the solution anchor hard on the principles like core concepts mental models that you really cannot mess up because if you do mess up on those things it changes the whole purpose of the solution because that's going to really determine the outcome of you know whatever solution you're working on so let's use a non-digital example to illustrate what i'm talking about here so if you're creating a menu for a local diner you might think of adding classic american breakfast items and staple lunch sandwiches but you probably wouldn't consider adding in a section for sushi just because your cook used to work at a sushi restaurant or because you know that sushi probably could charge more and make more money from because it just doesn't fit with the whole idea of what a diner is no way but maybe you can agree to disagree on the color of the menu or the typography and how it folds and the material that you use because that doesn't take away from the main purpose of what you're trying to do.

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника ядро: присутствие, тёплый тон, без стыда; всё остальное можно обсуждать.


### Мысли системами: пакет решений важнее одной детали  ·  [11:36](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=696)
**Мысль:** Влияние продукта определяется совокупностью решений, а не отдельным экраном; пример IKEA: система ящиков вместо одного. Три вопроса: связанные проблемы, срок жизни фичи, повторное использование.

> Precedence it's very natural for us to really just focus on the here and now and the problem that we're given and as makers and doers as craft people i know we want to sweat the details for every single problem that comes our way but i would argue that for an evolving digital product the impact on the product is really defined by the collection or the package of solutions that you design versus it being defined by any single solution that you've created so this is a nudge for us to maybe let go of our grip on you know perfecting every single detail for every single problem but thinking a little bigger and more systematically and thinking about for any project that we're working on to consider all the related problems and see if we could come up with a solution that addresses some or all of the overlaps let's run through an example to better illustrate this so let's take ikea for example so you could have a designer just create one single storage bin right and it probably would be a very good storage bin it would get the job done however compare that to having a whole system of storage thinking about it as a whole collection of different types of bins and then designing a bin for that system and you can see the benefits of it immediately so first for you as a designer you're creating the system so you're creating like a cohesive user experience where all these different parts can now work well together the customer is gonna benefit because they're gonna probably have something more cohesive and uniform for their home and the business will probably benefit because instead of selling one storage bin you can sell a whole system of them hope that makes sense now before we move on i know this is still probably a bit nebulous so i'll just run through a couple of questions that i ask myself as i'm trying to think this way and train myself to think this way so whenever i'm working on anything i'll ask what are the related problems and how does my design impact them or you know how long will this feature be around after i design it no what are the chances that you know i may need to actually remove it and what are the chances that another part of the product is gonna need or benefit from the same exact thing that i'm making right now.

**Для Напарника:** Новые элементы (ориентиры острова, реплики, чипы) проектировать как часть системы, а не одноразово.


### Всегда показывай несколько вариантов: три, два полярных и средний  ·  [13:45](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=825)
**Мысль:** Контролируешь только мыслительный процесс; набор вариантов с плюсами и минусами вовлекает команду. Больше трёх перегружает: два противоположных и один посередине.

> Present more than one possibility there is no way that you're going to control other people's perception of your work or that you're going to have absolute control over the precise solution that gets implemented what you do have complete control over though is your thought process and how you articulate that to other people and you want to anchor hard into what you can control so over time i've gotten in the habit of always presenting a set of solutions then walking the teams through the pros and cons of each option as well as the essence of how each option is going to solve the particular problem and anecdotally this has just always led to better design and product outcomes although it definitely takes more work and you kind of have to let go of a lot of things that you probably hold near and dear and just trust that the team is going to make better decisions this way and it's just better for brainstorming it invites people into your design process people are more engaged it's just better for cross-functional collaboration which is really at the core of good problem solving to help you get started below are some questions that you can ask yourself as you try to come up with a set of solutions so let's start with the top one you know what if we integrated with another tool instead of like building and designing something of our own what would that look like you know what would be the easiest for us to build versus what would be the fastest thing for us to build what would be the simplest experience for the user like the absolute best and what would set us up better for a future vision of the product and typically like three options is usually good for some reason anything beyond three i notice that people you know get overwhelmed and often what you wanna do is have two like polar opposites and then one that kind of sits in between what i mean by that is like two very different options and then one that kind of cuts the difference between the two.

**Для Напарника:** Для собственных дизайн-ревью Напарника: подавать три варианта (самый простой, самый быстрый, самый перспективный).


### Практика вариантов поднимает дизайн-планку всей команды  ·  [15:35](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=935)
**Мысль:** Если долго так работать с одной командой, все учатся думать о дизайне и понимать, что значит лучше.

> And this is a major benefit if you can do this for an extended period of time with the same team then the design standard of everyone on that team is going to be elevated and it's going to make collaboration easier it's going to make the product better and it's because through this everyone is practicing how to think about design and understanding what better design is and it's just a deeply influential like way to do things.



# 🎬 How your brain's executive function works -- and how to improve it | Sabine Doebel — TED


### Меняй контекст: компания, убери телефон, запланируй награду  ·  [07:56](https://youtu.be/qAC-5hTK-4c?t=476)
**Мысль:** Окружить себя людьми с той же целью, убрать отвлекающее до начала и заранее запланировать награду.

> So what does this mean for you and for your kids? Well, let's say that you want to learn Spanish. You could try changing your context and surrounding yourself with other people who also want to learn, and even better if these are people that you really like. That way you'll be more motivated to use executive function. Or let's say that you want to help your child do better on her math homework. You could teach her strategies to use executive function in that particular context, like putting her phone away before she starts studying or planning to reward herself after studying for an hour.

**Для Напарника:** Ритуал входа в Фокус: «убери телефон-отвлекалки» одной строкой и обещание награды после сессии.

# 🎬 I Studied 1,460 Onboarding Flows. Here's What I Found. — Mobbin


### Лучшие онбординги не ощущаются онбордингом: быстро к ценности или не мешать  ·  [08:53](https://youtu.be/Qsq-Sj_rojU?t=533)
**Мысль:** Общее у лучших: быстро приводят к ценности, добавляют удовольствие, дают персонализацию или просто не мешают; для некоторых продуктов лучший онбординг это его отсутствие.

> I don't think there's one right or wrong way to design an onboarding flow that's best in class. The ones that stuck with me didn't feel like onboarding. What I saw in common in these apps is that they brought users to value quickly. Sometimes it's adding delight to a very long onboarding flow. Sometimes it's letting users personalize their app experience for themselves. And sometimes it's getting out of the way. So, do we even need onboarding? Mobbin is a place to find design inspiration. The product speaks for itself. Same with AI chat apps. The first prompt is where users find value. For products like these, maybe the best experience is just to let users get in fast and not have an onboarding experience that gets in their way.

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника: дать начать сразу (Фокус с одним полем), а всё остальное предлагать позже, по одному.



# 🎬 I used AI to design my app's mascot (this is how) — Adam Lyttle


### Маскот должен иметь функцию в продукте, а не «приклеенную» роль  ·  [02:17](https://youtu.be/zmfzCi_fpJc?t=137)
**Мысль:** Идея пришла из вопроса, как маскот взаимодействует с пользователем: полосы на лбу как чёрные клавиши, белый кот как белые клавиши, спящий кот просыпается при ошибке.

> Then I had to think how this mascot would actually interact with the app itself so it doesn't just look like it's being plonked in there. If the identity of the app is going to be rebranded and based entirely around this mascot, I needed to serve an actual purpose for it so it actually fits in. So I started brainstorming some ideas. It's a piano app after all. So the mascot should have some simple element of music iconography. My original sketches revolved around this David Bowie inspired cat. But I just I wasn't connecting with it. I had to go back to the drawing board. I had to ask myself, how does this mascot interact with the user? How does this mascot make sense in the app? And that's when I had a bit of an aha moment. Instead of lightning bolt, why not make three stripes on the cat's forehead symbolic of the three black notes? From there, everything else just flowed really nicely. Instead of a gray cat, it's a white cat representing the white keys. And I felt like, hey, this is kind of a cool dude. He needs a little bow tie. And this is the first mascot that I've ever designed. I don't want to design something that's hard to animate. So, what's the easiest pose to animate? Well, if he's asleep the whole time, it's going to be really easy to animate. Then, when you hit a wrong note, the cat wakes up and you lose a life. This grounds the mascot into the app itself, and now it kind of makes sense.

**Для Напарника:** Роль кота в Напарнике (присутствие у костра, реакция на сессию) задавать как функцию продукта; без наказаний вроде потери жизни.


### Выбери образ, отражающий суть продукта, и свой уровень навыков  ·  [03:47](https://youtu.be/zmfzCi_fpJc?t=227)
**Мысль:** Метафора продукта (помодоро: томат) и интеграция в приложение; не берите сложный стиль на первую попытку.

> So, to come up with a mascot, you need to think about what type of animal or object best represents your app. For example, a pomodoro timer could have a tomato. A relationship app could be flowers or a love heart. You get the picture. And then you really need to think about how the mascot interacts or feels part of the app and not just plonked in there. You should also consider your own skill level at this stage, too. Don't go animating a 3D Pixar inspired mascot on your first attempt.

**Для Напарника:** Кот у костра как метафора присутствия; держать стиль простым и анимируемым.


### Шаблон маскота + собственный набросок позы + ИИ только полирует  ·  [05:03](https://youtu.be/zmfzCi_fpJc?t=303)
**Мысль:** ИИ хорош в полировке своих идей и посредственных шаблонных идеях; эскиз позы и шаблон сохраняют пропорции.

> This acts as my template and I can ask Chat TPT to manipulate my mascot into any pose I want while keeping the design and proportions consistent. First, I do a rough sketch of a pose and then I use the prompt I want this character doing XYZ in this pose. I provide chat GPT with a mascot template and my sketch. A so cute. It's important to do some of your own creative thinking here. You can't really outsource the design elements to chat GPT. It's really only good at two things. Polishing the ideas you've already come up with or coming up with its own bland, boring ideas. If you sketch your idea first, helps to prompt Chat TPT to provide a better mascot and pose. Plus, it saves you a bit of back and forth trying to get the mascot absolutely perfect.

**Для Напарника:** При создании поз/выражений кота давать ИИ эталонный шаблон и грубый эскиз; идеи придумывает человек.

The content design team was seeing the company and our scale and the speed of which we were moving was just like far exceeding their ability to do you know reviews and you know make sure all the words were checked and that we were only you know following the right thing. Chris within the content design team partnered with engine and PM to work on a tool that could then be integrated in Slack in GitHub in the CLI so that when people are building experiences we are along the way helping them make sure that they're making the right choices and following the right styles and that the content that's going to be in our product is a content that we're proud of and really fits the end all.

# 🎬 Making big ideas happen with design — Dive Club 🤿


### Сделать просьбу как можно меньше: одобрить исследование, а не весь проект  ·  [02:16](https://youtu.be/tWePWqWXff8?t=136)
**Мысль:** Вместо согласования всей затеи просят ресурсы на оценку риска; небольшой шаг сохраняет импульс идеи.

> Mitigating risk upfront instead of greenlighting this whole project can we Greenlight some investigation into seeing how risky is this what would we need to know to feel more confident moving forward and if it is risky how would we mitigate it and then how costly would those mitigations be now that is what it looks like to make your ask as small as possible maybe the outcome is a more focused research project or a new type of data collection or maybe even a day or two prototyping with an engineer it really doesn't matter what matters is that you've effectively maintained the momentum of your idea.

**Для Напарника:** Параллель с дроблением задачи на микрошаги в /api/breakdown: первый шаг должен быть таким маленьким, чтобы его не страшно было начать.


### Заранее назвать возможные провалы: снижает блокировку первыми плохими цифрами  ·  [04:18](https://youtu.be/tWePWqWXff8?t=258)
**Мысль:** Установка ожиданий, что метрики могут быть отрицательными, с гипотезами и планом, строит доверие и не даёт отказаться от проекта из-за первых цифр.

> One way to help teams feel more comfortable with your vision is to proactively share potential failure points upfront I think the other thing that really helped was also setting expectations going into this that these numbers may be negative and if they're negative here are some like six hypotheses why and a road map for what we will do to make sure that those things aren't actually contributing to this being a negative test that really helped say yep negative metrics happen that's okay we have a plan but we're going to keep moving forward with mitigating those negative metrics not back away from the entire project by identif potential issues early you're not just preparing for setbacks you're actively building trust plus thinking deeply about why something might not work forces you to consider every possible angle if people are already aware of potential negative outcomes you'll be less likely to be blocked by that first set of numbers that comes in.


### Инкрементальные A/B отрицательны, пока не открыты все «тумблеры» сразу  ·  [07:23](https://youtu.be/tWePWqWXff8?t=443)

> Good ideas die because we're committed to incremental progress but expect incremental value Val in return so we abandoned ship at the first sight of negative numbers thinking that we're on the wrong track sometimes the numbers going down is well we should keep moving forward we're not there yet we haven't unlocked all the Tumblers to open the vault to get to the gold inside so what we should do is keep unlocking tumblers not just back away so we had done these like AB tests and we kind of like tried to like incrementally stair step our way to a better solution everything's negative so I think eventually we're like screw it and we just like made things the way we thought they needed to be and then launched like one big AB test to say like if we redesign the whole experience from the ground up and we got rid of like all the debt and we did all the things instead of trying to piece meal our way towards like bad experience bad experience bad experience magic experience we just try to go to magic experience.



# 🎬 Master Spacing in UI Design 💪 — Jesse Showalter


### Выбери систему отступов и держись её: база 8px для всего  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/cf95Z7Ngg8k?t=22)
**Мысль:** Один базовый шаг используется для отступов, скруглений и типографики, что ускоряет решения и снижает число вопросов.

> You need to choose adopt and adhere to some sort of spacing system of your choice I prefer an pixel grid system because it gives me a small Base number I can multiply off of that divide things by it and I use that base number for everything for spacing for rounding my corners for sizing my typography I just use it for everything you don't have to use the one I use but you do have to use something that at least allows you to make decisions quickly and ask less questions.

**Для Напарника:** Принять 4/8px как базу и для отступов, и для радиусов, и для высот кнопок в Напарнике.


### Пример шкалы: 24 обёртка, 32 секции, 24 заголовок-контент, 16 карточка, 8 кнопки, 6 элементы  ·  [07:03](https://youtu.be/cf95Z7Ngg8k?t=423)
**Мысль:** Заголовок ближе к своим карточкам, чем тег к заголовку, и это показывает степень связи. Спускаясь внутрь, отступ только уменьшается.

> Have a before and after of the same design here's what we did we applied a wrapper to our design that was that first outer wrapper container and inside of that wrapper we've given it 24 pixels of padding a lot of times people will use 30 that's fine too that's nice and spacious and Airy I like 24 because it aderes to this 8 pixel grid that I'm such a fan of everything is flush to the edges of this wrapper and it's 24 pixels away then I established 32 pixels of spacing in between my sections you can see this is a section and it's about 32 pixels away and you have another section of content and it's also 32 pixels away we've jumped up a little bit 4 to 32 but that's okay our next step down is going to be 24 pixels between the titles and the content so now instead of having 32 pixels of spacing here we now have 24 pixels what we're saying is that these are more closely related these are more grouped and have a connection to one another than maybe this line or these elements are to this so my title directly relates here it needs to be closer to the cards than this career tag is to the title itself so we're going from the outside in next we're going to do 16 pixels of spacing inside of the cards so we have our actual cards here and this section is about 16 pixels away you can see how we move from 24 16 we're just decreasing the spacing as we tighten things up.

**Для Напарника:** Взять эту шкалу как стартовую для токенов отступов Напарника и проверять её на реальном экране.



# 🎬 My App Design Process: What Took Me 5 Years to Learn in 10 Minutes — Chris Raroque


### Тестируй UX на реальном устройстве и с реальными данными  ·  [01:36](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=96)
**Мысль:** Фигма показывает UI, но UX проверяется только на устройстве; красивые фейковые данные и картинки скрывают проблемы, которые вылезают на реальных данных.

> The reason I want to test it on a real device is I want to test the ux or the user experience figma is great to be able to see the UI and see how it looks but I found if you really want to test the ux you need to do it on a real device so you can actually simulate what it's going to be like in the real world for users and I try really hard to test it with real data because sometimes fake data makes everything look really good there are so many designs that I've seen that look amazing because they have really good high quality imag or specific texts that just fit the screen and look really good but then in production with real data and lower quality images it just doesn't look as good so I try to test it with my own data or as real data as possible just so I can catch some ux problems that the really good fake data was hiding.

**Для Напарника:** Это ровно §5.2 CLAUDE.md: проверять Дом/Фокус/Мир на реалистичных данных (9 стартов, а не 1) на реальном вьюпорте.


### Отбрасывать красивое, если оно не служит функции  ·  [04:18](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=258)
**Мысль:** Карточка с глубиной не добавляла смысла (глубина должна выделять); группировка по типу ломала порядок релевантности. Скучный классический список дал лучший UX.

> The first prototype was this concept where the results are inside of a card and the card looked really cool once I physically saw it here mocked up I realized it kind of added some unnecessary depth to the app usually depth is added to highlight or Elevate something for example if we added depth to the search bar it would make that more prominent in the UI but in this case the depth being added to the search results didn't really add anything in my opinion so I know we got this inspiration from this mockup from Airbnb but it makes a lot more sense for them because they have this map in the background so they kind of want to show this and let things float over it but since we just have a white screen in the back and we don't really have anything behind it felt a little bit unnecessary so we decided to not go with that option so another concept was grouping the results by type so if the matches are from the task title they'd be grouped here if they're from the notes they'll be grouped here that made a lot of sense and from a UI perspective it really did feel like this would make the design a little bit more clean but one of the issues with this when we thought about it a little more is the way that my backend and type sense which is the service I'm using for search they return the results and it's ranked by relevancy so the order actually matters if we group things I realize it would actually impact that order let me show you guys an example in the ungroup version it could appear as the second result however in the grouped version it appears as result number four so because there would be an actual impact on functionality tried not to go with the grouping option either and the last concept that we tried was a more traditional search list that you see in a bunch of different other apps it is a little bit more boring compared to the first two but I felt like it had the best ux out of all the three options.

**Для Напарника:** Перед добавлением глубины/свечения/группировки спрашивать, что это выделяет; привязка к принципу из §10 CLAUDE.md.


### Если не добавляет опыта, вырезать; заменить значимым элементом  ·  [06:40](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=400)
**Мысль:** Иконки типа совпадения игнорировались, их убрали ради снижения когнитивной нагрузки; чекбоксы выполнено/нет вернули воздух и неожиданно улучшили сканирование. Лишние индикаторы тоже вырезаны.

> The next thing I noticed was the icons on the left side which indicated what type of match the result hads for example this one showed there's a match on the note there's a match on the subtask it was really good in theory but when I was using it didn't seem that helpful I was kind of just ignoring the icons every time I ran a search so we decided to cut it because my philosophy is if something isn't adding to the experience it's best to just remove it so it reduces cognitive load on the users but then removing it the design felt super text heavy and I really loved how in these mockups there are icons on the left because it does break up the text a little bit and make it a little bit more breathable so we did try to brainstorm something to replace those icons luckily we realized we forgot to add the complete and incomplete indicators on the task like the literal checkbox that shows that a task was complete maybe we can just put those there and when we tried that I actually feel like the ux improved a little bit because it made searching for incomplete task a little bit easier it's just easier to scan and see oh these ones are incomplete so that was an unintended side effect that came out of that where I feel like the ux actually improved for some reason we just didn't think about doing this in the original design so that was a pretty cool thing that happened so on that note of looking for things to cut and simplifying the UI I was trying to look for more things that weren't that necessary for me or I was ignoring and one of the things I noticed is there's a couple of indicators on the task that I didn't feel like were relevant during the search process one example is this indicator on the task shows how much time was spent on the task this is really relevant on certain screens like the task list screen as I was searching and I was trying to put myself in the user shoes I felt like that task indicator wasn't that relevant to the search so for now we cut all the indicators except for label subtask and Note and I feel like that would cover most of what people are going to be looking for when conducting a search if people ask to bring back the indicators I'll reconsider it but for now we cut it just to keep things simple.

**Для Напарника:** Аудит Дома и Фокуса: убрать декор, который пользователь игнорирует; вместо него использовать смысловые элементы (статус шага).



# 🎬 Netflix Product Designer | Navin Iyengar | Design Like a Scientist — awwwards.


### Работа как серия экспериментов освобождает мышление: смелые идеи в малом масштабе  ·  [04:04](https://youtu.be/XRd6Ddn4ZSY?t=244)
**Мысль:** Идей больше, чем можно сделать, поэтому пробуют понемногу многих и вкладываются в те, что показывают эффект.

> Well we run experiments - and we found that this idea of thinking of our work as if it were a series of Experiments really frees up our thinking it allows us to try more innovative ideas and crazy ideas and maybe even stupid ideas but in a small scale contained way first to really understand what the effects are of what we're doing before we invest in building that at scale for 125 million Netflix customers Because we're trying to solve for something that you may be - at whatever app or website you're working on Which is that we wit we have way more Ideas than we could ever actually design and build and ship and so what? Experimentation allows us to do is try just a little bit of a lot of ideas to see where we should invest further.

**Для Напарника:** Новые механики Напарника пробовать в минимальном виде на себе и нескольких людях, не выкатывая всё сразу.


### И подтверждённая, и опровергнутая гипотеза это выигрыш  ·  [05:33](https://youtu.be/XRd6Ddn4ZSY?t=333)
**Мысль:** Опровержение экономит время на ненужной разработке, подтверждение открывает плодотворное направление.

> It's worth noting that both of those outcomes true or false are actually pretty positive Because if you especially in a product development context if you disprove your hypothesis Then you have just saved yourself. A lot of time. You might have designed and built and shipped something That actually doesn't have a meaningful impact on your customers satisfaction and if you prove your hypothesis true also great because you've just opened up a fruitful new area of Exploration that can improve your customers experience.

**Для Напарника:** Формулировать правки Дома/Фокуса как проверяемые гипотезы и считать «не сработало» полезным результатом.


### Наблюдай, что люди делают, а не что говорят  ·  [11:54](https://youtu.be/XRd6Ddn4ZSY?t=714)
**Мысль:** Сказанное слабо коррелирует с реальным поведением, поэтому A/B-тесты и наблюдение надёжнее опросов.

> About human behavior and the top line of that is really that what we've learned is that you need to observe what people Do not what they say. And that's what a be testing in its unbiased way of observing people really allows us to do this There's this quirk of human behavior that people say things but that doesn't really correlate very well with what they will actually do in real life.

**Для Напарника:** Опираться на реальные скриншоты и поведение пользователей (как в CLAUDE.md §8), а не только на пожелания.


### Даже в качественных сессиях сверяй слова с реальным поведением  ·  [16:11](https://youtu.be/XRd6Ddn4ZSY?t=971)
**Мысль:** Человек может одобрить опыт словами, а действовать иначе; объективное наблюдение выявляет расхождение.

> And so I challenge you to think about the same thing What behavior can you observe if you can if you can a be test sure do that because that is the most unbiased way to observe people but even what we saw in our qualitative sessions, is that when we ask someone whether it's the right experience they may say one thing but if you actually observe how they use it and Try to be as objective as you can you'll see that there might actually be a discrepancy between those two things.

**Для Напарника:** При тестах на людях смотреть, куда реально нажимают и где замирают, а не только что отвечают.



# 🎬 Neuroinclusive Design Deep Dive: From Burnout Loops to Sustainable Systems (June ‘25 Edition) — Pythoness Programmer


### Режим фокуса включается автоматически вместе с таймером  ·  [05:40](https://youtu.be/nblgz9lRcrM?t=340)
**Мысль:** Интеграция таймера задач со статусом убирает ручное переключение и сигнализирует остальным без усилий.

> Absolutely. Another game changer mentioned was integrating a task management tool like Sunsama with Slack. This setup lets a user automatically enter focus mode in Slack, maybe with a visual cue like a emoji next to their name whenever a timer is running in their task manager. >> Oh, clever. So, it signals focus without manual effort. >> Exactly. It's seamless. Reduces the cognitive load of constantly switching tools or updating your status. And it gently signals to others, hey, I'm deep in work right now.

**Для Напарника:** Запуск сессии Фокуса меняет фон/состояние кота автоматически, без отдельных кнопок.


### Системы должны работать в плохой день, а не только в хорошую  ·  [09:42](https://youtu.be/nblgz9lRcrM?t=582)
**Мысль:** Урок после сбоя: системы проверяются, когда ты устал и перегружен; доступность должна быть по умолчанию, а не пунктом в списке дел.

> That's a critical point. And a key revelation here is that accessibility has to be a continuous practice. One anecdote shared described an air conditioning breakdown during a heat wave that forced a complete work stoppage. >> Oh wow. Not ideal. >> Not at all. But it led to this powerful moment of clarity. Your systems need to work not just when you're feeling great and full of energy, but especially when you're tired, overwhelmed, or just, not at your best. Accessibility can't be just one more thing on the to-do list. It needs to be baked into your default state.

**Для Напарника:** Минимальный путь в Напарнике (один тап «начать») должен работать и в самый плохой день.


### Доступность по умолчанию: шаблоны, автопроверки, мягкая деградация  ·  [11:09](https://youtu.be/nblgz9lRcrM?t=669)
**Мысль:** Сделать правильный путь самым лёгким: автопроверки, готовые доступные компоненты, взаимные напоминания, система работает на базовом уровне даже в плохой день.

> The solution really lies in building accessibility into your foundational systems, making it the path of least resistance. This means leveraging things like automated checks, setting up tools that flag potential issues proactively before they even become problems, >> like llinters for accessibility >> kind of. Yeah. Then there's template based design, creating reusable components, documents, whatever that are accessible by default, so you don't have to reinvent the wheel every single time. Also community accountability, building systems or norms where members can gently remind each other about accessibility needs, not policing, but supporting >> a culture of accessibility. >> Exactly. And finally, this idea of a graceful degradation, designing systems that still function maybe at a basic level even when users or the creators themselves aren't at their best, ensuring a minimum level of usability even under suboptimal conditions.

**Для Напарника:** Хранить a11y-проверки (контраст) в дизайн-системе; сохранять работоспособность при сбое ИИ.

> What we need is psychologically smarter communication. And AI can help with that, especially when you ask it to critique before you create. That's a key distinction. So let me say it again. Critique before you create. Most people use AI by saying, write me an email. Sometimes that works. More often it produces something grammatical and bland. A better approach is to say, here's my draft. Analyze it from the customer's point of view. Where will they feel confused, skeptical, pressured, bored, or annoyed? That question changes everything.

**Для Напарника:** Прогонять реплики кота через критику «где здесь стыд или давление?» перед выпуском.

# 🎬 Pode, a Rose Painted Adventure — GDC Festival of Gaming


### Модульные ассеты: общий набор плюс уникальные детали на уровень  ·  [09:16](https://youtu.be/lv3YqqWs0qU?t=556)
**Мысль:** Небольшой набор универсальных элементов плюс специфичные для уровня растения, текстуры и цветовая схема создают впечатление гораздо большего разнообразия, чем есть на самом деле.

> The most important thing we did was to create modular world building and decorative assets we made a small set of generic assets for the entire game including walls floors filler plants and some decorative elements and from these we created multiple combinations some of them generic and some of them level specific then we had a small set of level specific plant assets to give each level its distinct look mushrooms and pine cones for the pine woods level and cloudberries and cotton grass for the birch woods and so on and so we did the same with textures we had some generic textures with a few variations and level specific textures with different colors and patterns and this approach makes each level feel new in spite of the reuse of assets unique floral and crystal combinations give each level the feel of its own shape and texture the combination of generic assets and level specific assets give the impression of a much bigger variety than there actually is.

**Для Напарника:** Для спрайтов острова (island-sprites): общий словарь форм, различающийся палитрой и парой уникальных деталей на каждый ориентир.


### Урок малой команды: мало ассетов, безжалостный отсев, визуал служит истории  ·  [15:37](https://youtu.be/lv3YqqWs0qU?t=937)
**Мысль:** Мало переиспользуемых ассетов, постоянно резать всё, что не нужно для цели, строить игру мачете. Визуал - инструмент рассказа: растущий мир отражает растущую дружбу.

> From this is you need to work smart streamline your pipeline because with a small team there is absolutely no time for idleness make few and reusable assets you really just need enough variation not to hinder the creative process or make the game feel repetitive you need to be constantly cutting everything not vital to your core goals i mean the phrase kill your darlings is cliche for a reason right you simply can't create quality content if you don't have the time or resources to do so you have to be ruthless build your game with a machete focus on what's most important and perfect that you have to make sure not to lose track of your goals visual design is really just a tool to help tell a story and for us it's a growing evolving world telling the story of a growing friendship and evolving emotions our increasingly detailed and beautiful world mirrors how empathy selflessness and cooperation makes the world more beautiful.

**Для Напарника:** Остров и кот как визуальная метафора растущих отношений и присутствия, а не декор; при ограниченных ресурсах ресурсы на главную сцену.
