# 🎬 Should Designers Listen to Negative Feedback? — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Не слушать громкое меньшинство  ·  [05:28](https://youtu.be/P05ONfLOqmY?t=328)
**Мысль:** Недовольные пишут чаще равнодушных и часто хотят сложнее, что отпугнёт тихое большинство. Нужно учитывать, сколько людей и каких говорят это.

> Don't listen to a vocal minority Insomniac Games brand officer Ryan Schneider says "just because you see it online and you see it in the forums, that doesn't necessarily represent the majority viewpoint", meaning criticism might be coming from a tiny subset of your players. In fact, he reckons the game Resistance 2 suffered because his studio listened too intently to a small group of noisy players. Whenever you receive criticism you have to consider how many people are saying it, and what sort of person they are. So, when it comes to something like DOOM's Marauder, people who are fed up and frustrated are much more likely to speak up than those who don't really have an opinion either way - skewing the perception of how many people hate it. On other games, devs tend to find that the most vocal critics want games to be more difficult and complex - which would likely alienate the more casual fanbase, who don't interact with forums and other sources of feedback.

**Для Напарника:** При разборе отзывов о Напарнике взвешивать их тихим большинством (данные использования), а не только громкими жалобами.


### Искать проблему за предложением пользователя  ·  [07:42](https://youtu.be/P05ONfLOqmY?t=462)
**Мысль:** Пользователь предлагает «убери X», но это редко верное решение; нужно докопаться до реальной причины. Пример Nioh: вместо удаления износа оружия механику перевернули в «знакомство с оружием».

> Two: Identify problems, not solutions A lot of player feedback comes in the form of a proposed change. You know: nerf this! Buff that! Remove him! But in truth, those suggestions are rarely the correct answer, and would often create new knock-on effects that the player can't be expected to foresee. So as the game designer, the goal is to drill down deeper into what the actual problem is, and from there you can find potential solutions that will actually work. Super Meat Boy maker Edmund McMillen says "If people don't like how your game controls, this could mean one of hundreds of things, from how things move in the game to what buttons it uses. When responding to feedback, ask specific questions and try to find the root of the problem". So, when Team Ninja released an alpha build of Nioh, players told the studio to remove the annoying item durability mechanic. But that was in there for a reason: to stop players using the same weapon over and over again. So instead of removing it outright, the developers flipped the mechanic on its head to create item familiarity. Now, when you use a weapon a lot you'll be able to transfer its best stats into a new one - which still encourages players to swap items on a regular basis. The game's director, Fumihiko Yasuda, said that player feedback shouldn't be used to find solutions, but rather generate questions that the developers can ask themselves.

**Для Напарника:** Когда пользователь просит убрать шаг или напоминание, выяснить, какую эмоцию или проблему он испытывает, и решать её, а не выполнять просьбу буквально.


### Обратная связь шлифует углы, но не убивает характер  ·  [10:02](https://youtu.be/P05ONfLOqmY?t=602)
**Мысль:** Если реагировать на каждую жалобу, продукт сходится к серой середине. Пример: Nemesis в RE3 обрезали из-за жалоб на Mr. X, и игра стала слабее.

> Three: Don't let changes lead to a boring game Sean Davies, technical director at Rare, says "if all you do is respond to feedback, then you end up converging on a fairly middle of the road and you don't surprise people". And I think that's largely true: feedback should be used to sand off the roughest edges of a game - but shouldn't lead to the removal of everything that makes the game interesting. As an example of this, DUSK programmer Dillon Rogers had a good Twitter thread about the invincible baddies of Raccoon City. So, the Resident Evil 2 remake features Mr. X: an unstoppable, jackboot bastard who relentlessly chases you through the Racoon City police department, regardless of the doors, and walls, in his way. He was a contentious feature at the game's launch, leading to complaints about him being too stressful. This year's Resident Evil 3 remake has its own big ugly bugger in the Nemesis, but unlike Mr. X he very rarely chases you. Instead, he's largely limited to boss fights and set pieces. We don't know for sure, but Rogers argues that it's "very possible that Capcom listened to people complain about Mr. X's roaming sequences, and decided to limit Nemesis to only a few short sections". And the result was a far worse game, with almost every critic noting how Nemesis is disappointingly neutered compared to last year's Mr. X, as well as the Nemesis in the PlayStation original. "Devs need to be careful not to let their own fans ruin their games,".

**Для Напарника:** Сохранять отличительные черты Напарника (кот, остров, тёплый тон), даже если часть пользователей просит «как у всех».


### Объяснённое решение вызывает больше доверия  ·  [12:22](https://youtu.be/P05ONfLOqmY?t=742)
**Мысль:** Когда авторы прозрачно объясняют, почему механика устроена так, игроки охотнее принимают спорное. Бета и ранний доступ сигналят «это ещё меняется».

> Create a conversation between developers and players Chris Avellone, who let players help guide the development of Pillars of Eternity during its Kickstarter campaign, said "once you explain why a certain feature is a certain way and make a very structured argument for it, we find that generally that level of frankness causes a lot of buy-in". Developers often find that players are more forgiving of a contentious game mechanic if they understand the reasons and motivations behind its inclusion. And so it's not uncommon for games makers to open their doors and communicate freely with players - from Warframe's weekly Twitch streams to game designers diving into the community forums. Also, things like beta branches and early access builds allow developers to experiment with new game mechanics while the game is still under development. This signals that the game is still in flux, and so players generally give the developers the benefit of the doubt￼.

**Для Напарника:** Пометить новые фичи Напарника как «пробуем» и коротко объяснять голосом кота, зачем они.


### «Помогите сделать игру, которую мы задумали»  ·  [14:51](https://youtu.be/P05ONfLOqmY?t=891)
**Мысль:** Переформулировка роли сообщества: не «помогите сделать лучшую версию», а «помогите достичь нашего замысла». Корпсы сделали опциональными, нанят комьюнити-менеджер.

> The ultimate solution for Red Hook was to, one, make corpses an optional feature - and, two, hire a community manager, John Lindvay, who helped the developer redefine the relationship between the people who make the game, and the people who play it. He says he shifted the dynamic from "help us make the best version of the game", to "help us make the game we're aiming for". That's a subtle shift in language, but he says it helps frame the relationship as developers leading the charge with a creative vision - and players helping them to achieve it.

**Для Напарника:** В запросах отзывов формулировать рамку как помощь замыслу продукта, а не голосование за любые фичи.



# 🎬 Simple 2D Idle Animation for your Character | Handdrawn | Beginner 2D Game Art — Nonsensical 2D


### Начни с двух крайних поз, чтобы ограничить амплитуду  ·  [09:08](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=548)
**Мысль:** Две ключевые позы задают диапазон движения заранее, анимация живёт между ними.

> The reason I start with the head is that it helps me figure out how much my character is going to move overall and it also helps me contain the animation so now I know that my animation is going to exist between these two frames and the jump is not going to be bigger or smaller than this.

**Для Напарника:** Для CSS-дыхания кота зафиксировать два состояния (scale/translate) и анимировать только между ними.


### Минимум кадров и ping-pong вместо loop для idle  ·  [10:46](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=646)
**Мысль:** Добавлять кадры только до исчезновения рваности; ping-pong для idle вдвое сокращает работу, но не подходит для бега.

> I don't generally care too much about whether I have 4 or 24 frames I tend to just add the least amount of frames I feel are necessary to get the animation the way I want it to be what this means is I'll slow it down to the type of speed I wanted to move and then I'll check if it's to jacket and if it's too Jagged then I'll add more frames when I'm shaking my animation I have it set to ping pong the animation so in most animation software you have it to try the ping pong or Loop what Ping Pong does is that it makes the animation play back and forth rather than repeat itself so the ping pong goes back and forth whereas the loop goes whoop and starts over Roop so we'll go to my last frame and then cycle all the way back to the start this kind of makes sense for an idle animation and makes it so that we only have to animate half as much.

**Для Напарника:** В CSS дыхание кота делать animation-direction: alternate с easing, а не два отдельных кейфрейма вверх и вниз.


### Дай работе отлежаться: дистанция показывает реальные проблемы  ·  [17:06](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=1026)
**Мысль:** Время вдали от проекта даёт перспективу, что действительно является проблемой; перфекционизм мешает закончить.

> But the thing is I'm never fully happy with any of darts I do but after work done it then give it into a try I let it sit and then I come back to it in the future because time away from the project will give me perspective on whether it is an issue or not as an example when I made my staging scene I tried focusing on the core idea of where you might place your main background assets to maximize Paradox but then I completely overlooked working on the background so it looked really flat and empty but eventually this bugged me a lot so before I started my video on environment animations I went back into the scene and fixed it and we can see if I compared it to the difference it made and that tends to be the process I work through I don't like the idea of trying to perfect things because you would simply never get things done.

**Для Напарника:** Процессный совет: полировку экранов делать итерациями с паузой, а не доводить до идеала за раз.



# 🎬 Sir Jony Ive's 2021 CCA Commencement Honorary Doctorate Address — California College of the Arts - CCA


### Идеи хрупки: не начинать с критики проблем, а удерживать саму идею  ·  [10:04](https://youtu.be/gr3GKcwG9s8?t=604)
**Мысль:** Новая идея частична и недоказана; если сразу сосредоточиться на известных проблемах, теряется вера в решение. Критика без конструктивной идеи разрушительна.

> I obsess trying to deeply understand the general nature of ideas. Understanding their nature, I tend to have more ideas and do a better job caring for, protecting, and developing them. Ideas, by definition, are always fragile. If they were resolved, they wouldn't be ideas. They would be products that were ready to ship. I've come to learn that you have to make an extraordinary effort not to focus on the problems which are implicated with any new idea. These problems are known. They're quantifiable and understood. But you have to focus on the actual idea, which is partial, tentative, and unproven. If you don't actively suspend your disbelief, if you don't believe there is a solution to the problems, of course you will lose faith idea. That is why criticism and focusing on the problems can be so damaging, particularly in the absence of a constructive idea. Remember, opinions are not ideas. Opinions are not as important as ideas.

**Для Напарника:** При обсуждении новых механик Напарника сначала формулировать идею и её смысл, и только потом разбирать риски.


### Любопытство это решение, требующее намерения и дисциплины  ·  [11:25](https://youtu.be/gr3GKcwG9s8?t=685)
**Мысль:** В группах нас тянет к измеримому и известному; желание учиться важнее, чем быть правым, а радость любопытства смягчает страх нового.

> Perhaps the most important thing I can share with you today is about curiosity. Being truly open, inquisitive, and curious has become the very basis for all that I do and how I think. Having a genuine relish for being surprised and for learning is fundamental to creating. Many of us have a natural or innate predisposition to be curious. Though I have learned that after a traditional education or working in an environment with many people, it has to be a decision. It requires intent and discipline. In interactions with larger groups, many of us gravitate towards the tangible and the measurable. It is more comfortable, far easier, and more socially acceptable to talk about what is known. Of course, being curious fuels our appetite to learn. And wanting to learn is far more important than being right.



# 🎬 So you want to make an Incremental Game? — Aarimous


### Простота: ценность понятна с трейлера, возвратов 5%  ·  [01:00](https://youtu.be/S-JsFaJhs88?t=60)
**Мысль:** Через трейлер сразу ясно, нравится ли игра; люди без интереса не покупают и не оставляют негативных отзывов. Результат: возвраты 5%, конверсия вишлиста 30%.

> And the first thing I'm going to say is keep it simple, stupid. The fun thing about A Game About Feeding a Black Hole is that you can come to this page, you can start watching this trailer, and you very quickly know, you hover over things, you break them, you feed the black hole, you get some upgrades, bada bing bada boom. Do you like this sort of game or not? You know so quickly if this is something for you or not. Plenty of people have told me no, they don't like it, and that's great. And they didn't buy it, they didn't leave a negative review, and that is shown through our lifetime return rate is only 5%, which is really, really good. And I think our conversion rate sitting at 30% wish list conversion rate. So, the value proposition there is there. It's understood easily at the at the core of it.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг Напарника должен за секунды показывать суть (кот, остров, фокус), чтобы нужные люди оставались, а ненужные уходили спокойно.


### Копируй удачные игры и пропускай через свой опыт  ·  [02:08](https://youtu.be/S-JsFaJhs88?t=128)
**Мысль:** Смотри на рынок, играй в успешные игры, составь список того, что не нравится, и сделай свою версию; искусство — это то, что ты вдохновился и пропустил через свой опыт.

> Go look at the market. Go find games that are successful. Don't go copy games that have low reviews and try to be this hero where you're going to you're going to take the idea and zhuzh it up and throw the Hail Mary and have the game be successful. If you're trying to be a successful developer right now and you're looking at the incremental space as a place to be able to make small scope games, go look and see what players in that space are interested in doing. Play the games yourself. Find the ones you're interested in doing. I did that with Keep On Mining. I had a blast playing this game. I played it, I came up with my list of things that I didn't like about it, and then I slapped together the game that I wanted to make. I love space things. I liked this more minimalistic theme. I liked the movement and kind of I started making decisions that aligned with my sensibilities as a gamer. So, although we all feel this weird feeling of why you're just copying the game, you're not doing anything new that's all of art, in my opinion. Art is taking the things you're inspired by and putting them through your own unique experiences as a human on this planet. The last 32 years of my life are very different than however many years you've been on this planet, and that's going to be able to tell if I make decisions that align with my experiences, the game that I create at the end of the day is going to feel unique and different and hit home with different players.

**Для Напарника:** Изучать уже работающие продукты для СДВГ и переосмысливать через собственный тон и ценности, а не изобретать с нуля.


### Фокус на одном: активные мгновенные апгрейды, а не пассивные  ·  [03:46](https://youtu.be/S-JsFaJhs88?t=226)
**Мысль:** Пассивные эффекты делали игру «мутной»; сосредоточились на активных эффектах с мгновенным откликом.

> A Game About Feeding a Black Hole is fundamentally a very active play style where you're moving your cursor around, hovering over stuff and breaking things. You're getting upgrades, but all of those upgrades we ended up finding out had to be these active instant effects. So, electric shocking the screen or supernovas exploding in areas or laser beams or moons making all these things better. We tried out passive effects, but that made the game feel really muddy. So, we kind of really focused in on the one thing we're doing really well and then tried to deliver on that experience.

**Для Напарника:** Выбрать для Напарника один главный опыт и удалять улучшения, которые размывают отклик.


### Найди момент скуки через пробу и вопрос «хотел ли ты остановиться?»  ·  [14:04](https://youtu.be/S-JsFaJhs88?t=844)
**Мысль:** Собственная проверка («съесть собственную еду») теряет чувствительность, поэтому нужны плейтестеры; ключевой вопрос: был ли момент, когда хотелось бросить. Это точка, куда нужна новизна.

> And basically, what we tried to do for the rest of this tree is figure out when do we need to re-engage the player? When does it kind of start to feel like, "Okay, I've done this loop for enough." And you can do that by yourself as a player, play your own game, dog food it, eat the game. Does it taste good still? At what point does it not keep tasting good to you? And when do you need to add something new? Eventually, you'll lose your ability to tell if that if that novelty is there or not, and that's where play testers come in huge. Ideally, you can watch them in person play your game and just kind of ask them, "Hey, a really good question. Was there any point that you wanted to stop playing the game?" A lot of the times I'll be playing incremental games and play testing them, and I want to stop pretty quickly, which just means the main mechanic doesn't align with me. There's just some friction or some cumbersomeness or some weird stuff about it that I didn't like, or I'll get 15 minutes in and kind of get bored by it. And that is where, my friend, novelty comes into play.

**Для Напарника:** В интервью с пользователями Напарника спрашивать, в какой момент хотелось закрыть приложение, и добавлять новизну именно туда.


### Нарушение контракта с игроком и знание, когда закончить  ·  [16:37](https://youtu.be/S-JsFaJhs88?t=997)
**Мысль:** Если исчерпаны механики, можно кардинально сменить правила либо считать игру законченной. Короткая игра на 1-2 часа по цене 4-5 долларов нормальна; главное, чтобы первые 5 минут были чистыми.

> And maybe if the game gets if it gets long enough, you maybe have to do some more drastic novelty or what Thorn and I call breaking of the contract with the player. For A Game About Feeding a Black Hole, at about an hour or 2 hours in, we've kind of explored all the mechanics we really could with kind of what we had there. We probably would have had to do something where we broke the contract with the player and maybe now introduce a mega click where they can click and hold and go and you get this crazy big clicking moment or something to break the player the contract with the player in a different way that would let us kind of do something different. I'm not saying do that for your game. I think if you get to the point where you've explored all the mechanics and you really don't you're like, "I just I don't know what else to add, Aramis." I think at that point you can call that game done. Wrap it up, package it up. If the game's an hour or 2 long, that's perfectly fine. Price it at 4 to 5 bucks, discount it at 30% when it launches. Be in the next fest, get your stuff done on the itch page so you get the validation. Make sure those first 5 minutes are nice and tight and clean.

**Для Напарника:** Не раздувать Напарника лишними режимами, когда идеи исчерпаны; лучше шлифовать первые минуты.



# 🎬 Storytelling masterclass for designers — Dive Club 🤿


### Идея должна быть краткой, запоминающейся и передаваемой, как мем  ·  [01:39](https://youtu.be/LZEh-QTim2Q?t=99)
**Мысль:** Вторая встреча решает судьбу идеи; комикс из трёх панелей про граф контрибуций GitHub дал историю, которую пересказывали и вставляли в презентации.

> That's why the key to effective storytelling is making sure your ideas are easily shared kind of like a meme now I know when you hear the word meme you probably think of distracted boyfriend or Futurama Fry or maybe the Drake grid but if we abstract what's really happening a meme is simply a method of distilling an idea into a format that is one concise two memorable and three sharable that's why I think we can learn a lot from this story from Kathy zang's time at GitHub I remember when I was like creating designs around the GitHub user profile it was just like me trying to explain the problems around the profile that I was observing first comic I made was this story that one of my peers who worked at getup told me where she wears her get up hoodie and she was on an airplane had her laptop open and she was like looking at her get a profile and somebody passed her on the plane and she said that they asked her oh do you work at GitHub and she was like yes and then the person was like oh but your graph it's like not very green like there's not enough commits on your contribution graph and then she just felt so gutted so my comic just like told that story in three panels I just anchored the whole like okay here's like the problem with the contribution graph and the problem it creates in the community and why people are like so polarized on it's an example of how to like condense that story into a format that maybe has the chance to meme itself people will screenshot it and include it in other presentations when they're talking about a thing that is anchoring to the same problem or same user story and then that thing just like proliferates.

**Для Напарника:** Формулировки микрокопирайта и сообщений Напарника должны быть короткими и запоминающимися: фраза вроде «это не минус, это ноль» шарится и запоминается.


### Давать названия концептам: «Less but better», «Sea Peek»  ·  [03:16](https://youtu.be/LZEh-QTim2Q?t=196)
**Мысль:** Короткое имя превращает прототип в общий словарь команды: идея разошлась и была выпущена. Названные сущности упрощают асинхронное обсуждение.

> Memes don't have to be images either I mean deer Ram's design philosophy can be summed up in three words as a meme less but better and that's why the language that we use to present our Design Concepts is so important I think sometimes it's helpful to name your Explorations so that it's easy for people to grasp like oh this is the drawer concept and that is the sheet that's y wng who was a design L at Twitter Airbnb and now is the head of design at Maven and I remember specifically when Yen was leading a project to redesign the maven marketplace now she used data to shine a light on some potential opportunities one of the problems she wanted to solve was making it easier for students to get the gist of a course without having to navigate to what we would call the CLP how we refer to course landing pages on Maven so at our weekly demos she showed off a pretty nifty popup like this now people liked the Prototype but she did something really genius while presenting it she gave the concept A name she called it the C Peak it was perfect because her prototype was a way for students to get a quick look or dare I say a peak into a course then it's easy for them to also talk about them sure enough the idea behind seal Peak caught like wildfire because now in only three syllables anyone on the team could reference her prototype and all of the surrounding conversation data points everything from that meeting and as a result the concept of C Peak quickly gained popularity and the feature was shipped shortly after but it's not just y I talked with Alex Cornell about his time designing the linear mobile app and he shared the same tactic you're now referring to these things as named entities and you maybe sometimes it's even worth giving them a name you know this is the no menu option this is the gestural option or whatever then it gives people a vocabulary that they can use in their asynchronous message back.

**Для Напарника:** Давать имена механикам Напарника (Фокус, Дом, Мир, ориентир), чтобы единообразно говорить о них в продукте и в команде.


### Побуждающий инцидент: сначала мир, потом конфликт, потом решение  ·  [05:19](https://youtu.be/LZEh-QTim2Q?t=319)
**Мысль:** Запоминается история с конфликтом в первых 20%, а не список фактов и фич.

> To stick with people then you need to nail the inciting incident the inciting incident is the single most crucial part of any story that serves one function which is to arrest your attention and the inciting incident is the moment that happens almost always within the first 20% of a film or a story that turns the Hero's World on its head it's this major opposing Force the first major piece of conflict that the hero faces and then has to take action to try and overcome to address this major upheaval now if you think about that and you care about the design and presence of the inciting incident everything else you need comes out of it so the sighting instant only matters if you create the world beforehand so if you establish the world of the story and then you need to articulate your way to a resolution stories are memorable facts and features not so much.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг: начинать с узнаваемой боли (не могу начать) до показа механик.


### Микро-конфликты: эмоциональный пик, где пользователю тревожно  ·  [07:09](https://youtu.be/LZEh-QTim2Q?t=429)
**Мысль:** Момент тревоги или перегруза в потоке можно сделать завязкой истории: сначала мир пользователя, затем конфликт, затем решение.

> Inciting incidents exist in the micro too maybe there's an unexpected change in the data or maybe you identify an emotional high point where users feel anxious or overwhelmed in a given flow now you can use that as a way to tell a story that makes your designs more memorable first you create the user World beforehand then you really highlight that moment of conflict and then you can propose Your solution so going back to our Q3 planning meeting internalizing this moment of conflict is what empowers your product lead to effectively tell your story if you arm your person with the major piece of conflict and the way to overcome it they are more able to rearticulate that idea onwards.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать момент, где человеку с СДВГ тревожно перед стартом, как отправную точку для онбординга и текстов.


### Равновесие боли и инсайта: инсайт убеждает, когда виден путь к нему  ·  [08:06](https://youtu.be/LZEh-QTim2Q?t=486)
**Мысль:** Голый вывод «нужна фича» слаб; когда ясна боль, которая к нему привела, команда действует и лучше усваивает инсайт.

> More compelling when the pain that led to them is also just as clear let's say I'm a researcher at dualingo just did some research on this math product that's about to come out and I have insights you know like we need to include this feature one option would be to just tldr it just share that Insight hello Executives we need to put this feature for math learning in the product that's the short version you want the bottom line that's the bottom line anyone from dualingo CEO their executive team down to every single I see on every single product te working on this math product will feel a lot more compelled to act on it and will internalize the Insight a lot better when the pain point that led to it is just as clear how did we get to this Insight how did we get here the brain craves that.


### Знай аудиторию и её уровень: топ-менеджер остановил после двух минут контекста  ·  [10:49](https://youtu.be/LZEh-QTim2Q?t=649)
**Мысль:** Подробная настройка контекста для старшего руководителя сразу была оборвана просьбой показать результаты.

> My number one tactic or recommendation for improving your storytelling or trying to get your ideas bought in easier is knowing your audience knowing what their goals are what they really care about what their level of altitude is in the company I remember back in my time at Uber I was trying to tell a story to a senior product executive at the company and I went like to Ground Zero I was like okay all the context setting and like it took less than 2 minutes before he stopped me and he was like I get it like let's see the outcomes.


### Кубик Рубика: у каждого своя сторона идеи  ·  [11:32](https://youtu.be/LZEh-QTim2Q?t=692)
**Мысль:** Разные люди видят разные стороны; нужно выбирать, какую сторону показать, чтобы все поняли, что речь об одной идее.

> Some people really cared about metrics and qualitative anecdotes from users some people really cared about the perfect pixel view of that potential product and the most helpful insight that I received from my head of design Max showning was this analogy he gave me which is people are very different they all see different sides of a Rubik's Cube you have to learn which side to show them so that they realize that when you're talking about an idea you're actually talking about the same thing.



# 🎬 The 6 Most Common User Onboarding Mistakes 1,000 SaaS Companies Made — ProductLed


### Схема событий: первые три для aha, затем 5–10 для внедрения функций  ·  [06:05](https://youtu.be/1XtjFTHrAVQ?t=365)
**Мысль:** Сначала определить первые 2–3 события, которые приводят к aha-моменту; после активации задать 5–10 событий, по которым показывать следующие функции, чтобы не оставлять человека без направления.

> So how to take advantage of custom events in user onboarding how can you think about it right the way you can think about is by thinking about the first initial aha how the users in the first three events they want to do when they come inside the app how do you want to show them the key feature activation and then once you have done this you can show them feature a feature b feature c feature d which is a product adoption side which means that they have understood the value of the app and now it's time to keep helping them discover new features so you can have around five to ten events after user activation event to know what the user should be doing and based on those in-app custom events that user does you show them the next thing so you don't leave them hanging.

**Для Напарника:** Лестница: первая сессия → первая награда на острове → первый ориентир → вечерний чек-ин. Каждая ступень открывается по событию, а не по таймеру.



# 🎬 The 80% of UI Design - Typography — Sajid


### Разбор списка: размер и вес заголовка, приглушённый ник, кнопка в карточке, проверка в исходном размере  ·  [07:00](https://youtu.be/9-oefwZ6Z74?t=420)
**Мысль:** Крупный жирный заголовок, более тёмный и мелкий ник и лёгкая кнопка создают иерархию. Нужно проверять результат в реальном размере, не только при увеличении.

> Firstly there is no hierarchy everything just looks like a blob of text secondly this button needs to stand out let's zoom in some size and weight to emphasize the title then a darker color and smaller size to deemphasize the username a lighter color to highlight the button but the button doesn't look quite right here let's move it to the right and wrap everything inside a card it's looking much better now the button still looks a bit bulky though let's reduce the font size to match the username it looks good but remember we're zoomed in we need to check if it works at the original size the username might be hard to read it could use some weight and so could the button.

**Для Напарника:** Всегда сверять типографику на реальном вьюпорте 390px, а не только в увеличении.


### Размер относителен соседям; динамические числа тоже проектируй  ·  [10:32](https://youtu.be/9-oefwZ6Z74?t=632)
**Мысль:** Элементы конкурируют за внимание, и жирный крупный шрифт бывает слишком резким, тогда снижают вес. Нужно учитывать динамические значения, не только визуальную иерархию.

> Font size is a relative unit meaning it depends on its neighboring elements right now both are competing for attention let's try a bigger font size and see if that works big bold fonts can often be too harsh on the eyes so let's bring the weight down and see if that works it looks good but this is a dynamic number so we need to account for that as well don't just design for visual hierarchy design for functionality too.

**Для Напарника:** Таймер сессии должен корректно выглядеть и при двузначных, и при однозначных минутах, не прыгая шириной.



# 🎬 The ABC's of High-Converting User Onboarding Experiences — ProductLed


### Силы прогресса (Jobs to be Done): боль и притяжение против привычки и тревоги  ·  [00:24](https://youtu.be/tMea1lPSCTc?t=24)
**Мысль:** Две силы толкают к изменению (боль текущего и притяжение нового), две блокируют (привычка и тревога перед новым); онбординг должен усиливать первые и снижать вторые.

> To build a high converting onboarding experience, one useful concept is the progress making forces from the Jobs that we've done framework. According to this framework, there are four main forces that influence users as they go from the Current Situation to a New Behavior. There are two forces that direct users to adopt a new behavior. That's a portion of the current situation whether it's painful for them or the pull of the new solution, whether it's innovative or something new. And there are also two forces that block change. This is the habit of the present and the anxiety of the new solution,.

**Для Напарника:** У человека с СДВГ главная блокирующая сила это тревога начала; интерфейс и тон кота должны снимать её, а не добавлять давления.



# 🎬 The ADHD Executive Function Map Nobody Gave You (+8 AI Prompts) — Kat Tan | Technically ADHD


### Когнитивная импульсивность: паркуй идею вместо того, чтобы хвататься  ·  [06:28](https://youtu.be/A5XOw_jsNss?t=388)
**Мысль:** Согласие или запуск нового проекта до завершения прежнего; помогает пауза и вопросы про приоритет, цену отсрочки.

> ADHD impulsivity isn't just about interrupting or acting recklessly. It's more often about cognitive impulsivity. Saying yes before considering your bandwidth or starting a new project before finishing the last one. Between more intense emotions, the constant dopamine chasing, and everything feeling urgent, it's easy to make snap decisions before considering consequences. Advice for those with ADHD, it's okay to take a pause, even for five extra seconds. Train your awareness that your capacity is limited and not everything has to happen right now. And the last AI prompt, evaluate whether I should act on this idea now. Describe it. My current top priority is X. Answer three questions. One, does it align with or pull me away from X? Two, what would I have to pause or drop to pursue it? Three, is there a real cost to deciding in two weeks instead of today? And end with one recommendation, pursue now, park for later, or drop it.

**Для Напарника:** Кнопка «припарковать идею» в чате, чтобы не отвлекаться из сессии, без ощущения потери.



# 🎬 The Best AI Coding Tools for Designers — Dive Club 🤿


### Квадранты инструментов: размер задачи против того, что уже есть в проде  ·  [00:10](https://youtu.be/J08endIde9E?t=10)
**Мысль:** Автор выбирает инструмент по двум осям: размер работы и доля уже существующего материала; для неоформленных идей холст и текст лучше кода.

> A mental model that I find very useful. So I'm going to share it with you today. These are the quadrants that exist inside of my brain. And the x-axis is the size of whatever I'm working on. Is it a quick P3 bug fix or are we going 0ero to one on some really big meaty ambiguous idea? And the yaxis is how much of the material that I'm going to work with already exists in production. Is this feature work that is starting from a blank slate or you know maybe we're just iterating on something that is already shipped. There's a lot of hype right now on Twitter about how the canvas is dead and everything has to exist in code. And honestly, I think it's kind of ridiculous. And yeah, not to overuse the double diamond metaphor, but I think there is merit to thinking of our work that way because a lot of times it's very unshaped. We have no idea what problem we're even solving or what way to approach it. And if that's the case, man, I love making a mess on a canvas. And I actually use a lot of writing. Like this is often what the initial stages of my design looks like. And you can see how ridiculous this is, right? Like I'm just not ready to start playing in code. I just want to get the general shape of an idea.


### Ловушка: пересобирать прод внутри прототипного инструмента  ·  [05:12](https://youtu.be/J08endIde9E?t=312)
**Мысль:** Дни, потраченные на доводку пикселей и интеракций вне дизайн-системы и без связи с продакшн-кодом, оказались пустой тратой; прототип должен быть маленьким и сфокусированным.

> You're creating incredibly small focused prototypes. And the trap that you fall into is trying to rebuild production inside of an AI prototyping tool that doesn't sit on top of your production codebase. And I've totally been guilty of this. 6 months ago, I was spending days prototyping fully functional components inside of Lovable like this one. And they turned out kind of great actually. Like I was really happy with how this worked. There's no way that I have been able to do something like this inside of Figma because there's a bunch of different animations and anytime you're working inside of a textbased environment, like you kind of got to get into production code. So, all of this made a lot of sense to me, but I'm also self-aware enough to admit that I spent way too long sweating the details on all of these pixels and interactions and doing it in a way that wasn't built on top of my design system at all.

**Для Напарника:** Экспериментальные анимации острова и кота делать сразу в кодовой базе Next.js на токенах проекта, а не в отдельных прототипах.


### Параллельные агенты спасают состояние потока  ·  [07:13](https://youtu.be/J08endIde9E?t=433)
**Мысль:** Ожидание ответа каждые две минуты ломает поток; несколько изолированных рабочих пространств убирают стоп-старт.

> And I freaking hate having to wait for Claude to reply every 2 minutes. So, the tool that has pretty much consumed the majority of my AI coding as of late is Conductor. It's an easy way to run multiple clawed codes in parallel, and each one is their own isolated workspace. So, I can work on multiple features at once, which kind of solves that flow state problem where I'm stopping and starting every single 2 minutes. Obviously, this is great for speed, but what I love again is that it helps me stay in a flow state because I don't have all of these little stop, start, stop, start where I'm waiting for Claude to process my request.

**Для Напарника:** Параллель с Фокусом: любые паузы ожидания (загрузка реплики напарника, дробление задачи) разрушают состояние потока, поэтому их нужно скрывать или сокращать.


### Планирование с ИИ-CTO: фазы, параллельные ветки, оценка отчётов  ·  [16:21](https://youtu.be/J08endIde9E?t=981)
**Мысль:** Сначала долгий диалог, где ИИ задаёт вопросы и фиксирует контекст; затем план по фазам с учётом параллельной работы и цикл отчёт-оценка-следующий промпт.

> CTO and help me create some of these initial project docs. The most important one is this development plan here. And I specifically asked Claude to break it up into phases and to think strategically about which work can happen in parallel so that I can use separate conductor work trees as well as separate conductor agents within an individual PR as effectively as possible. So you can see like this is phase one, the core data layer. And what I'm doing is I'm creating a new chat for each one of these phases. And then I actually have Claude write the prompts for conductor for me. So if I scroll all the way up here, these are separate conductor workree chats and then it's simultaneously writing the prompt for each one of them. I literally don't even read these. I kid you not. I just grab them and paste it into Conductor because I've already done all of the work up front in the planning phase. What I then do is tell Conductor that it has to report back to the CTO. And I take that report, I paste it back into Claude, and I ask it to evaluate the output, make sure everything looks good, and then it will create the prompt for the next step.



# 🎬 The Best Landing Page Structure for 2026 — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Скелет лендинга: первый экран, боль/доказательство по уровню осведомлённости, 5–8 выгод, как работает, FAQ, закрывающий блок  ·  [04:13](https://youtu.be/vwhR2M0SQtI?t=253)
**Мысль:** Порядок зависит от осведомлённости: кто знает решение — доказательство раньше боли, кто нет или предложение сложное — сначала боль. Каждая выгода с картинкой или видео; в конце «закрывающий» блок.

> This right here is what we typically follow for any given project. And this is basically the blueprint of how to create a high converting landing page. I'll put a link in the description for this below. So, make sure you grab that. So, at a high level, right, it all starts at the very top with a very action-packed above the fold where we have a benefit- driven headline, benefit driven sub headline. We typically have two to three different forms of social proof. So, really building a ton of trust and credibility up front. We try to reduce fear and uncertainty around buying which we call FUDs. And then we have an image or video or sometimes a form that typically helps to reinforce your core value proposition and articulate what it is you do. All of this is just the above the fold. Following that section, we have what we call the painoint section, which is basically where you're trying to explain what the old way is and kind of meet people where they are right now and then also tie in your own unique benefit, right? and what makes you actually unique. We'll either have this painoint section or we'll have the social proof section, right? It typically depends on the awareness level of your audience. So, if they tend to be more solution aware, like they know exactly what they're looking for, then we will actually put the social proof section before the pain proof section. But if they tend to be more unaware, right, or the offer is more complex, then we always typically lead with the painoint section first, followed by social proof afterwards. Then as we scroll down, right, these are what we call the value prop sections, right? So this is basically going to take up a huge portion of the page and is really kind of the meat of the page. And we typically have, you know, anywhere from five to eight value props for any given company. For each of these different value props, right, we have an image or video that helps to complement what we're actually talking about. We typically have a how it works section. So just showing people the process from step one to step three or step four and just showing them how easy it actually is. We have an optional about us section to meet the team and then we have more social proof FAQs right towards the end of the page and then what I call the closer section which is basically trying to seal the deal and give them that one final push to get them over the edge to convert if they're still not convinced.

**Для Напарника:** Структура лендинга Напарника: hero, узнавание боли, 5–6 выгод (кот, остров, мягкий старт), как работает, FAQ, финальный CTA.



# 🎬 The BEST Productivity Method Ever for ADHD | Interstitial Journaling — Novie by the Sea


### Почему системы не держатся: слишком строгие душат, слишком свободные забываются  ·  [01:06](https://youtu.be/UFidZJhxz84?t=66)
**Мысль:** Планирование будущего не работает для мозга без чувства будущего; нужен инструмент, работающий в моменте и дающий ясность сразу.

> So, I've tried so many productivity methods over the years. All the planners, all the habit trackers, Pomodoros, time blocking, Notion templates, all of it. And I swear some of these are built by people who have perfect time awareness, perfect executive function, and perfect lives. Because for me, nothing ever stuck. If the system is too strict, I suffocate. I instantly feel like I'm in a box, and then I rebel and stop doing it after 2 days. But if a system is too loose, then I forget about it completely. And if it requires planning things in the future, then it's even worse. My brain has absolutely no understanding of future. If you ask me what I'm doing in 3 hours, I don't know. I barely know what I'm doing in 3 minutes. So, I needed something that works in the moment. Something that doesn't require planning or predicting or scheduling or mapping out an entire day. Something I can pick up in the middle of the mess, and it gives me clarity immediately.

**Для Напарника:** Дом и Фокус должны работать «здесь и сейчас», без планирования дня вперёд; план на сегодня не обязателен.


### Интерстициальный журнал: записывай, что делаешь сейчас и что дальше  ·  [02:11](https://youtu.be/UFidZJhxz84?t=131)
**Мысль:** Короткие записи между задачами дают понимание, где ты, что только что сделал и что следующее.

> So, what even is interstitial journaling? The name sounds fancy, but it's literally just this. You write down what you're doing as you're doing it in between tasks. That's it. You don't plan, you don't write it to do list, and you don't try to guess the next 10 things. You simply document what you're doing right now, in the moment, and the next tiny thing you're going to do. It can look like this. Little entries, short sentences, just you narrating your day as you move through it. It feels stupidly simple, but it works for an ADHD brain. You always know where you are. You always know what you just did. You always know what you're going into next. No guessing and no what was I doing confusion.

**Для Напарника:** Лента чата в Доме как журнал: напарник коротко фиксирует «что сделали» и «что дальше».

### Три группы цвета достаточно: нейтральные, бренд, семантические  ·  [00:12](https://youtu.be/vvPklRN0Tco?t=12)
**Мысль:** Сложность цвета в том, чтобы вовремя остановиться. Нейтральные для фона, текста и границ, основной для главных действий, семантические для состояний.

> The tricky bit is knowing when to stop playing and just pick something that works. Here's what actually works. Neutral colors for background, text, borders, and a lot of other elements. the brand or primary color for main actions and to add a bit of character. And finally, maybe some semantic colors to communicate different states. These are all the colors you'll ever need to design apps and UIs.

**Для Напарника:** Ограничить палитру Напарника тремя группами, а новый цвет добавлять только при реальной смысловой необходимости.


### Светлая тема: вычесть светлоту из 100 и подправить глазами; имена токенов нейтральны  ·  [02:37](https://youtu.be/vvPklRN0Tco?t=157)
**Мысль:** Инверсия даёт стартовую точку, а глаз корректирует: свет обычно сверху, поэтому верхний элемент самый светлый. Токены лучше называть так, чтобы имя работало для обеих тем.

> Start by subtracting the lightness value by 100. That'll give you a good starting point. Then we'll use our eyes and some common sense to adjust the values manually. For example, why does the top element have the darkest color? Light usually comes from the top, so it should be the lightest and the base color should be the darkest. That's why I name my background colors as BG dark or BG light. So BG dark will be the darkest and BG light will be the lightest shade, whether it's dark or light mode. But we can't do that with text colors. So use names that make sense for both cases.

**Для Напарника:** Токены дизайн-системы называть по роли, не по цвету, на случай светлой темы.

# 🎬 The INSANE Gap Between Average and World-Class UX/UI Design — uxpeak


### Требования нестрогие: фильтр помогает человеку выяснить, чего он хочет  ·  [05:25](https://youtu.be/U7jTCgIWS4w?t=325)
**Мысль:** Люди готовы смягчить бюджет или число комнат, если это даёт лучшие варианты; задача фильтра не в скрытии несовпадающего, а в помощи сузить огромный набор, пока человек ещё определяется.

> Sounds pretty simple, but this is where filters get interesting because those requirements usually aren't as fixed as they sound. Maybe you want three bedrooms, but if that leaves you with almost nothing, you'd probably consider two. Maybe you have a budget in mind. But if another 50,000 suddenly gives you much better options, you might be willing to stretch it. So the job of a filter isn't just to ask the user for a bunch of values and hide everything that doesn't match. It has to help them narrow down a huge set of options while they're still figuring out what they actually want. And this problem is everywhere. Shopping, flights, jobs, food delivery. You start with hundreds of thousands of options and somehow the interface has to help you get down to the few that are actually worth your time. That's what makes filters surprisingly difficult to design.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип выбора для Напарника: помогать выбрать задачу и длительность через рекомендуемый дефолт и подсказки, а не заставлять сразу назвать точное значение.

# 🎬 The Psychology of High Converting Landing Pages — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Три шага: первый экран, правдоподобные отзывы, убрать лишнее  ·  [06:37](https://youtu.be/qGJG7nLPd8w?t=397)
**Мысль:** Убрать навигацию и оставить одну CTA, объединить поля формы, заменить текст иконками и задать иерархию.

> Step one, create a killer above the fold. Think of that one hard-hitting benefit or outcome that your target audience dreams of wanting to achieve, and communicate that in just your headline and your image. This is going to create a powerful halo effect like no other. Step two is creating ultra-believable social proof. All of the social proof you have, I want you to think of how can I make the social proof way more believable and trustworthy? Can you add images or video? Can you add a story of what the person has experienced? The more specific you can be, the better the results. Step number three is to make things simple. Go through your entire landing page and simply just remove anything that is not absolutely critical for conversions. On your navigation, right? Remove all of your items and keep your focus on just one CTA button. On your forms, combine first name and last name into just full name so you can get rid of an extra field. Instead of text, utilize images and icons and things that are more scannable wherever possible. Finally, have a clear visual hierarchy using things like font size, weight, boldness, and color to prioritize the most important elements and deprioritize the less important elements.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист для лендинга Напарника: один CTA, без лишнего меню, минимальная форма.



# 🎬 The secrets to good Pixel Art animation! (Animation tutorial) — Pixel Overload


### Планируй движение отдельно от эстетики: простые формы и скелет  ·  [01:43](https://youtu.be/J1sFBDQt8J0?t=103)
**Мысль:** Анимация — это движение, а не свет и характер. Сначала упрощённые формы и цепочки точек для ткани, потом возвращаешь «кожу». Это даёт лучший результат вдвое быстрее.

> It’s one of those things that no-one likes to do, but it really enables you to create much better animations in half the time. Don’t get me wrong, I never liked to plan out the movement for my animations, until I realised how useful it was. It’s so easy to get lost trying to create animations from a static sprite by shifting pieces around and playing with individual pixels. But this often results in an awkward animation. Animation isn’t about the lighting or the character. Animation is about movement. We need to separate the aesthetic of the static image from its movement and focus only on that movement. Once we have the really nice foundation of good movement, we can begin to put the ‘skin’ back on the character. Instead of investing time and energy into polishing every frame as we go, the best way is to simplify the subject into its most primitive shapes, and make these shapes move fluidly. For a humanoid character, that would be a different coloured shape for each limb. For fabric or cloth, create a chain of dots with delayed reactions to the movement of the dots above. Each segment reacts like links in a chain, but once you average out the movement with a curved line, it can be used for anything organic. So it’s kind of like drawing the skeleton of your character.

**Для Напарника:** Для анимации кота и пламени сначала делать грубые примитивы с правильным движением, затем детализировать.


### Нарисуй линию траектории: тогда думаешь только о скорости  ·  [02:48](https://youtu.be/J1sFBDQt8J0?t=168)
**Мысль:** Быстро набросанная линия пути убирает половину вопросов и спасает от переделки анимации с нуля.

> Sometimes, you want an object to jump or fall. Probably one the most overused cliches of animation tutorials (besides the slime) is the bouncing ball. They always show a curved parabola to demonstrate the path of the ball. I never saw myself actually drawing this line for an animation, but it’s just one of those planning things that really helps improve the final product and, more importantly, ease the process. I would 100% recommend drawing a quick line for the path of your animation. Because then you only have to think about the object’s speed and not the path. I think I’ve said enough about planning. Yes, it can be a pain but it’s a much better alternative to having to start a high-fidelity animation all over again from scratch.

**Для Напарника:** Для полёта искр, прыжка кота или появления ориентира задавать путь заранее (SVG-path/motion-path), а динамику настраивать отдельно.



# 🎬 The Weird Future Of User Interfaces — Enrico Tartarotti


### Большинство повседневных задач лежат посередине между простым и сложным  ·  [09:12](https://youtu.be/f32W5BEzWN0?t=552)
**Мысль:** Кроме таймера и аналитики, мы делаем промежуточное: читаем, пишем, бронируем; именно там не хватает звена.

> Well, I left out something really important here. See, this is not just a gradient from simple to complex. It looks more like this, a normal distribution. Most of the things we do every day with our tech are not just setting a timer and are also probably not doing analytics on huge databases. It's stuff that is in the middle. It's browsing the web, writing a presentation, booking a flight, checking your email. See what's going on in here, in the middle, is actually what's important and where our missing link is.

**Для Напарника:** Задача «разбить дело на шаги» именно такая серединная; подходит для генеративного интерфейса.


### Генеративный UI уровня 1: машина решает, какой интерфейс лучше, и строит его на месте  ·  [10:14](https://youtu.be/f32W5BEzWN0?t=614)
**Мысль:** Не заранее собранные приложения, а интерфейс, созданный под текущую задачу в стиле бренда; задержки со временем исчезнут.

> But, there's something different here. These are not apps that someone built. A machine has decided that a graphical user interface >> >> was the best interface for what the user was doing and then it went ahead and built it on the spot. And it decided the best UI would look like this. This is called generative UI. There's three levels of it and this example in Claude is just level one. Because this is direct manipulation. It is styled consistently with Claude's brand and colors, but everyone else is catching up. Google just announced that search is going to start generating little apps that help you visualize complex search or topic with generative UI. And sure, these things now are pretty slow to generate on the spot, but just like when I was a kid, a web page would take like a minute to load and now we just assume it's instant, the same thing will happen to generative UI. In the future, it would look silly to wait a minute for your app to be ready.

**Для Напарника:** Идея на будущее: ответ кота как мини-интерфейс (таймер, список шагов) вместо текста.


### Уровень 2: привычный UI, но часть элементов генеративна (поиск билетов со слайдером дат)  ·  [11:01](https://youtu.be/f32W5BEzWN0?t=661)
**Мысль:** Date picker быстрее и точнее текста; но для сложной поездки нужен UI, который никто не проектировал; Airbnb отказался от чат-бронирования.

> But, level two is where things start to get interesting. Take booking a flight. The UI we have now kind of makes sense. You can pick your airports, your dates from a date picker. And this, by the way, is way faster than typing that out on your keyboard and way more accurate than using some voice assistant to do it. But, last month I had to organize a trip to San Francisco for me and my producer Bartek to shoot a video where we went behind the scenes of Figma. I'll leave a link in the description, by the way. It's a pretty cool video. So, I had to search for flights from Italy and Poland to SF, adding a stop in New York City, and we weren't even sure about the dates. This is the kind of thing that moves the complexity from here to here. Now, I use Google Flights, for example, and it already has today an AI mode. So, I was like, great. But when I typed in my query, it told me it cannot show the results because, yes, this is an AI prompt box, but the resulting UI underneath is regular UI. It's just a grid of flights. So, level two is keeping UI that is, well, regular UI, but where some pieces of that can adapt to be generative. See, this is a mock-up I made that starts from the familiar UI of Google Flights, but still gives you the flexibility to add any weird request. But the resulting UI underneath is not going to be just a grid of flights, but something that's no designer at Google has directly designed. In this case, it's a slider that lets me move around dates and shows me with colors the most expensive flight of the bunch. Airbnb CEO Brian Chesky just said on X that they tried to implement chat-based interfaces of booking Airbnb, but they had to scrap them because they realized it just didn't work. It wasn't the right kind of interface. And so, now he just launched a new AI lab that's building AI models made purposefully for generative UI.

**Для Напарника:** На Доме: чат остаётся, но результат дробления — привычные карточки шагов, а не простыня текста.


### Дизайнеры проектируют правила и набор «LEGO-блоков», а не экраны  ·  [12:40](https://youtu.be/f32W5BEzWN0?t=760)
**Мысль:** Вместо расположения кнопок проектируются UI-кит и правила, когда показывать карту или список; при полном генеративном продукте правила и есть продукт.

> And something else happens when you get to this level two because the way you build technology changes completely. Designers and engineers are not going to design and build screens and decide where a button goes or a list or what's the layout. They're going to design rules. Going from designing this, what each screen is, to this, basically designing the UI kit, the LEGO blocks that the AI can use to generate the interface with your visual language and taste, and then rules for how to use these LEGO blocks. For example, when to show a map versus a list, what layouts are preferred. And this is a fundamental shift in how products are built. And level three is where you don't just have a regular app with pieces of generative UI, but the entire product becomes generative UI. The rules that tell the product what to show are the product.

**Для Напарника:** Зафиксировать правила Напарника (светлота, медленность, диегетичность), чтобы любой ИИ-агент генерировал в одном духе.


### Будущее — не только чат и не только GUI: выбирай лучший интерфейс под каждую работу  ·  [13:26](https://youtu.be/f32W5BEzWN0?t=806)
**Мысль:** Традиционный UI для простого, агентные системы для сложного, генеративный там, где он решает лучше всего.

> But there's something important to understand here. The future of how we interact with technology is not generative UI. It's not all UI. It's not chatbots and agents. It's all these things. We will still have regular traditional UI with direct manipulation where that is the best interface for those jobs, usually simple ones. We will still have powerful agentic systems that do things for you where that is the best interface for that complex job. And we'll have all three layers of generative UI where that is what solves the problem best. And this is the biggest shift that most products will have to go through in the coming.

**Для Напарника:** Для каждого сценария выбирать тип взаимодействия сознательно (кнопка, чат, генерируемая карточка).



# 🎬 This app made over $1M — Starter Story


### Валидация: найти пробел в отзывах существующих приложений и выпустить MVP  ·  [02:01](https://youtu.be/VfNRd5Rk0cM?t=121)
**Мысль:** Скачала конкурентов, увидела в отзывах повторяющийся пробел, собрала прототип; первые сотни пользователей просили продолжать, несмотря на баги.

> So my idea at the time was to download and try existing apps. And I noticed a common theme in the user reviews that there was this gap when it came to identifying what a panic attack is and also solutions for how to basically get through a panic attack in the moment. And I put together a really early version of Rooted, a prototype, an MVP type solution. And the first few hundred users said that even though there was still some lagging, some bugs, some incomplete parts, they really wanted me to keep going with it.

**Для Напарника:** Читать отзывы приложений для СДВГ/фокуса, искать повторяющиеся боли, проверять идеи малым MVP.


### Слушай пользователей, береги себя, празднуй мелкие победы  ·  [13:17](https://youtu.be/VfNRd5Rk0cM?t=797)
**Мысль:** Пользователи знают, на что фокусироваться; выгорание от постоянной интенсивности; жалеет, что не отмечала мелкие моменты, оказавшиеся лучшими.

> I would suggest that you listen to users very closely. They will have the answers you need. They'll help you know what to focus on. And I believe, especially in the mental health space, at the end of the day, that's who we're most responsible. Launching Rooted with the MVP and getting that original feedback. I mean, to this day, I still read all the user reviews, even though I have somebody else do the responses now. I still go in and I make sure I know what's going on and just to have a pulse on what users are saying. I'd also recommend taking care of yourself along the way. I think there were definitely quite a few points where I almost reached burnout. I was building something really intense and that level of intensity was with me at all times. And I think I looking back I wish I celebrated a few of the smaller moments more because now I realize that those were like the most awesome moments whereas really I was just focus on the next thing and ready to get to the next thing. So I would recommend taking care of yourself and also celebrating the small wins.

**Для Напарника:** Сама идея Напарника: отмечать малые победы; и команде отмечать вехи, не только гнаться за следующим.

# 🎬 This UI/UX Redesign Will Teach You More Than 100 Tutorials Combined — uxpeak


### Размещай информацию там, где пользователь в ней нуждается  ·  [06:51](https://youtu.be/GGg61sdEjeI?t=411)
**Мысль:** Рейтинг рядом с названием отвечает сразу на два вопроса: что это и можно ли доверять. Хороший UI это размещение информации, а не просто расстановка элементов.

> The rating is currently too far away from the product title. That's a problem because reviews are one of the strongest trust signals in e-commerce. When users see the product name, they should also quickly see how well it's rated. What is it and can I trust it? So, we move the rating closer to the title. Now, users immediately understand three important things, what the product is, how it's rated, and whether other people trust it. This makes the buying decision feel easier. Good UI is not just about arranging elements. It's about placing information where the user needs it most.

**Для Напарника:** Статус сессии (шаг, оставшееся время) расположить рядом с названием шага на Фокусе, а не в углу.



# 🎬 Time Blindness - An ADHD or Executive Dysfunction Trait — Therapy in a Nutshell


### Замерь реальное время: «5 минут» на обувь оказалось 17, значит начинать в 7:43  ·  [07:38](https://youtu.be/wk_iX7pXGwk?t=458)
**Мысль:** Секундомер на повторяющихся задачах даёт реалистичную оценку; её переносят в напоминание.

> To make time visible for tasks or routines is to record them. So especially with tasks where you're chronically late, rushed, or taking too long to do something, get out your stopwatch and record the actual time it took to complete the task. If you do this a handful of times for the same task, you'll eventually be able to estimate the actual time it takes to get them done. So for example, if you're chronically showing up late to drop your kids off at school, time how long it takes for them to get ready, dressed, shoes on, and in the car with their seat belts on. I would even include how long it takes you to get down your driveway. So maybe you used to think that it takes 5 minutes for your kids to put their shoes on, but after timing it you've learned that it actually takes 17. Now you have a much more realistic idea that to get them out of the door by 8:00 they have to start putting their shoes on at 7:43. So put this number on a post-it note on your fridge, set an Alexa reminder, whatever it is that's going to help you get the actual task done.

**Для Напарника:** Копить фактическую длительность шагов и предлагать «обычно у тебя уходит около N минут».



# 🎬 Time to rethink design systems — Dive Club 🤿


### Дизайн-система нужнее всего, когда не знаешь, каким будет продукт  ·  [02:16](https://youtu.be/F7yTiO8mUBk?t=136)
**Мысль:** Ценность не только в стандартизации, но и в скорости: небольшой набор базовых компонентов (кнопки, сетка, типографика, цвет) даёт гибкий фундамент, а не жёсткий.

> We often fixate on standardization as the main value prop of Design Systems and through that lens it's easy to view it as the thing that you do once you've figured out the UI and you're ready to codify it but don't forget about speed I'll suggest that Design Systems are most valuable when you don't know what the product will be that's why they're the perfect first steps for startups which at first might seem a little bit counterintuitive but that's why I want to shine a light on perplexity and how their founding designer and now head of design Henry modet thought about it any startup that I've built over the last couple years like that's always the first thing I built because that lets me go fast investing early in a set of core components minimizes the number of knobs that you need to turn in order to itate you're laying a Nimble foundation not a more rigid one the key is laser focusing on the atomic level that way you're never over architecting the system You're simply giving yourself an open-ended box of Legos that you can use to assemble uis more quickly.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник живёт в быстрых итерациях: держать компактный набор базовых токенов и компонентов, не раздувая систему.


### Базовые элементы почти одинаковы везде: две-три высоты кнопок, 40 и 48 пикселей  ·  [05:49](https://youtu.be/F7yTiO8mUBk?t=349)
**Мысль:** Ядро системы повторяется от продукта к продукту, поэтому нет причин откладывать: не нужен product-market fit.

> You don't need product Market fit to confirm that you'll probably have two to three button sizes and they'll almost certainly include 40 and 48 pixels if you're designing for web I even double checked A system that I made over 5 years ago and sure enough like the core elements are almost identical to the system that I most recently designed from Maven so don't overthink it I'm working on a new product right now and there's just no reason to delay building out these core components and colors and typography it's an easy way to explore more ideas more quickly.

**Для Напарника:** Опорные размеры кнопок (40/48 px) и тап-таргеты фиксировать в токенах с самого начала.


### Toolkit как ящик конструктора, а не связанная система  ·  [06:45](https://youtu.be/F7yTiO8mUBk?t=405)
**Мысль:** Пока определяешь сложные организмы и связи между компонентами, начинаешь строить систему и скатываешься по скользкому склону. Пусть компоненты будут отдельными кубиками.

> What do we call it then well I'm orbiting around a term that rapael used to refer to his system for KRON yeah definitely the design system like pretty much from the GetGo maybe more like a design tool kit like design tool kit feels like a little lighter weight kind of components I can kind of reuse design toolkit somehow it kind of feels fresh compared to UI kit although the extra syllable isn't exactly ideal but I like how it feels more like an open-ended box of Legos than a connected system and I think that's the most important line to draw when thinking about the right level of system to create for your company startups can't afford to Main contain anything other than the core product and as soon as you start defining more complex organisms and worrying about the relationship between components you start building a system and that's where the slope gets pretty slippery so instead of focusing on the connectivity of your system think of components as a box of one-off Legos that you can use to more quickly assemble interfaces I wanted to just give myself a toolbox so that not knowing exactly what the product would be or how it would work and make it very easy to assemble that product experience in my opinion the pendulum has swung way too far with Design Systems I believe every product can benefit from a set of really simple components and that investing in that toolkit upfront is one of the more High Roi decisions.

**Для Напарника:** Не усложнять компонентную библиотеку Напарника связностью раньше времени; компонентов хватает для трёх экранов.



# 🎬 TINY HABITS by B.J. Fogg – Animated Book Summary — Upgraded Mentality


### Сделать лёгким: чем проще действие, тем меньше зависит от мотивации  ·  [04:28](https://youtu.be/ijInaXZy0pU?t=268)
**Мысль:** Плохие привычки держатся потому, что проще альтернативы; чтобы новая закрепилась, её нужно сделать физически и умственно лёгкой и встроить в расписание.

> One of the best ways to make a new habit stick is to make it easy social media has perfected this art you want to open up the app because it is easy and the rewards are instantaneous many bad habits are formed and kept because they are simply easier than the alternative fast food is a billion dollar industry because you can just cruise through a drive-through or sit down in minutes and have food this is much easier than going home and cooking a healthy meal for yourself when trying to form a new habit you need to make it as easy as possible can you spend money make it physically or mentally easier and or make it fit into your current schedule make it easy maybe on sunday you spend the day with your spouse making food for the week this way you won't be tempted to grab a quick bite when you have something waiting for you already made at home we've already talked about how fleeting motivation is so the easier you make a new behavior the less it will rely on motivation the easier a behavior is the more likely you'll perform it over and over again.

**Для Напарника:** Дефолты и подготовка заранее: шаг уже сформулирован и сохранён (saveStepQueue), старт одним тапом.
