# 🎬 Tokyo iOS 2024 - Crafting Delight - My process of creating delightful interactions in iOS — Tim Oliver


### Прототипируй: вынеси идею из головы в руки как можно быстрее  ·  [05:58](https://youtu.be/eKl1JDfMsg4?t=358)
**Мысль:** Как приложение ощущается, понятно только когда держишь его в руках; даже слайды Keynote или рисунок на бумаге быстрее проверяют идею, чем код вслепую.

> Called prototyping. Fake it till you make it. And the idea that Apple promotes this concept a lot where it's like you don't know how an app will feel until you actually hold it in your hands and play with it. So you can try and imagine it as best as you can, but it really helps to have it in your hands and play with it. So do whatever you can to get what's in your head into the real world as quickly as possible so you can play with it and see if it actually works. that could be like make the app in Keynote or just do what like you know or just write on draw it on a piece of paper. just try and get it out of your head and on into reality and then see if it actually works in reality like you were picturing it in your head. So the CDR is like get as go as quickly as you can to kind try and create the thing you're picturing to see if it's actually feasible.

**Для Напарника:** Перед сложной анимацией (рост ориентира, финальная хореография) собрать грубый прототип и посмотреть на реальном телефоне, как рекомендует §6 CLAUDE.md.



# 🎬 Top 🔟 Executive Function skills To turn it around — Seth Perler, Executive Function, ADHD, 2e


### Мы лжём себе про время: «сделаю за пять минут» растягивается навсегда  ·  [03:40](https://youtu.be/Vyzgx0mpS1E?t=220)
**Мысль:** Самообман про длительность и «сделаю потом» - привычка, которую нужно осознанно ломать самонаблюдением.

> I told myself oh i can get this assignment done in five minutes oh i'll get it done tomorrow oh i'll get it done later tonight oh i'll put procrastinate on it but i'll get it done and i would lie to myself and i would think it would be easy and i would think i would just get it done and it would take me forever so being able to be self-aware is a skill because we want it's easier to just lie to ourselves until it's not easier to lie to yourself.

**Для Напарника:** Мягкая сверка оценки с фактом после сессии («ты думала 5, вышло 15») без оценки.


### Планируй с запасом: вместо часа на подготовку закладывай два  ·  [05:30](https://youtu.be/Vyzgx0mpS1E?t=330)
**Мысль:** Мы систематически недооцениваем время и энергию на домашку, поэтому удвоение оценки компенсирует ошибку.

> I learned to have certain planning skills like one of them was to over plan so i would think oh i'm gonna work on i'm gonna plan a half hour to study for this test well i learned that i really needed to plan two hours to study for a test to one hour because i would lie to myself or i would mis-estimate how long things would be so i learned to overplay and that was a skill.

**Для Напарника:** Предлагать длительность с запасом и дать возможность «продлить» сессию без ощущения провала.



# 🎬 Webinar: Product Led Onboarding by Pendo VP of Design & Research, Cameron Moll — Product School


### У каждой зоны продукта свой aha-момент, и под него создают поводы  ·  [07:25](https://youtu.be/jC7NNST0SGY?t=445)
**Мысль:** В Events у Facebook нашли, что интерес к мероприятию в первые дни повышает удержание создателя, и создали поводы для этого именно в эти дни.

> Now prior to pendo i was the head of design for the events platform at facebook and i can guarantee that the aha moment methodology is very much in play at facebook not just holistically these meta aha moments but even in product areas in the events team for example we had our own aha moments we knew that as a new creator on facebook of events if we could get a number of users to express interest in your event within a first you know number of days you were much more likely to retain as a new creator of the events platform and so we then created compelling opportunities for people to express interest in your in your event in those critical first few days so that we could increase the chances of you retaining as a new creator.

**Для Напарника:** Для экрана Мира отдельный aha (первый растущий ориентир), для Фокуса (первая завершённая сессия): проектировать под них раннее вознаграждение.


### Итерируй гипотезы aha и измеряй осмысленное использование, а не разовое  ·  [17:14](https://youtu.be/jC7NNST0SGY?t=1034)
**Мысль:** Мерить стоит время до первого использования ключевого действия и повторное использование (3–4 раза), плюс настроение и удержание.

> Iterate and experiment it wasn't enough for us for us to stop with the initial aha moments that we thought we identified but we had to iterate and experiment to identify what those real aha moments needed to be for pendo free and so iterate and experiment with your onboarding tactics and approaches and of course measure success what are the things that matter to you to measure successful exceptional onboarding some of these might be the ones you see on screen how much time does it take to first use those key aha moments and not just use them one time but find meaningful use from them that might mean three or four times before they really find meaningful use of that of that feature.

**Для Напарника:** Метрика Напарника: возвращается ли человек к сессиям повторно за неделю, а не просто запустил ли раз.



# 🎬 What are loops in game design? - Loops - Game Design Theory — Game Design with Michael


### Цикл Дэна Кука: ментальная модель, действие, правила, обратная связь  ·  [01:30](https://youtu.be/PMj8Q4ViKzs?t=90)
**Мысль:** Мозг использует ментальную модель, чтобы выбрать действие, система применяет правила и возвращает обратную связь, которую игрок встраивает в модель.

> Loops are a concept that can be found in the interaction design and learning fields. When the brain is interacting with something there is a process that it goes through in order to adapt to its surroundings. There are several different versions of this loop so we will be using the one proposed by Daniel Cooke of Spry Fox. The process looks like this. We start at the top where we have a store of information the brain already has. This is called the mental model. At the start of the game this is likely going to be pretty empty, although it may have common things like genre constants to draw upon. An example of a genre constant might be most first-person shooters have very similar controls. The brain uses this information to devise an action that may have an effect on the current situation and then it acts. The action in the case of a game is received by the system and run through the rules. This is the part where the game takes the action the player has taken and reacts to it with an output. This output is then fed back to the player as a response to what has happened after their action, also known as feedback. The player then receives this information, interprets it by referencing it with their current understanding and tries to fit the new feedback into their mental model. The process then repeats.

**Для Напарника:** Моделировать каждое взаимодействие Напарника как такой цикл и проверять, что игрок получает понятный отклик на своё действие.



# 🎬 What EXCEPTIONAL Onboarding Actually Does - Building Successful Apps — Philipp Lackner


### Сохранять состояние онбординга после каждого шага  ·  [11:58](https://youtu.be/kouFJkcLKAQ?t=718)
**Мысль:** Если приложение убито системой или человек вышел, потерянные данные заставляют проходить всё заново, и он бросает. Данные каждого шага записывать в локальные настройки и возвращать к месту остановки.

> And a tip for these onboarding flows that collect data, always save the current onboarding state in local preferences. Especially if you have some longer forms and the user maybe has to fill out some text fields, has to really give you your data, it is super frustrating if they maybe quit the app, the app is killed by the system because of memory pressure, pros that happens and all that data is lost or they simply quit the app, come back later, and they have to go through all that again. This makes them quit. So, it's simple, really just with every single step add a little bit of data, so exactly the data that was filled out in that in that step to your local preferences, and then just read the entire onboarding state from local preferences so you can directly lead the user to the next onboarding step where they've left at.

**Для Напарника:** Недозаполненные формы имени, чек-ина и очередь шагов хранить в localStorage (saveStepQueue уже так делает); при возврате продолжать с того же места без укоров.



# 🎬 What Is Executive Function - And Why Is Executive Dysfunction So Bad for Mental Health? — Therapy in a Nutshell


### Переформулируй проблему конкретно: «не знаю, с чего начать»  ·  [10:46](https://youtu.be/2RoWB8tYLMM?t=646)
**Мысль:** «Не могу делать домашку» превращается в «не знаю, с чего начать, и убегаю в TikTok» - навык организации, а не телефон.

> And then next we want to help you explore specific areas where you struggle. And again, get as specific as possible. So if your problem is, "I can't get my homework done," replace it with "I don't know where to start with my homework, so I avoid it by watching a bunch of TikToks." and you might think that your problem is having Instagram on your phone, and you might not be wrong, but the executive function issue is an organizational skill deficit. Or you might say something like "After a brainstorm I get overwhelmed and I don't know how to select the best idea for the paper." Or you might identify a problem by saying, "Oh, I can draft the paper, but I hate editing it" or "After I finish it I forget to turn it in and I lose it" or "I miss the deadline." So you've got to make it concrete.

**Для Напарника:** Диалог напарника при застревании: помочь сформулировать причину конкретно («не знаю с чего начать?») и перейти к первому шагу.


### Системы вместо силы воли: по одному изменению, пока не станет рутиной  ·  [12:37](https://youtu.be/2RoWB8tYLMM?t=757)
**Мысль:** Пример с ключами на крючке у двери: простая система снизила потери с 10 раз в день до раза в неделю; система становится рутиной и тратит всё меньше энергии.

> It's also just really important to build out systems and test those out and try them and see how they worked. and this includes only making one change at a time. Like I - like Atomic Habits, people. Like I love it. If you haven't read it, read it. so for example, let's say every time I do my homework I'm going to do it at my desk. And when I finish my homework I have a place to put that homework in, the red folder, and that folder goes in my backpack. And that's a system, right? Now, this may sound too simple, but seriously, like systems work. so for me I used to lose my keys all the time, like multiple times a day. I thought this was normal. I just got used to it. But when I got, when I got married, it drove my husband crazy. So we worked together to figure out a system where every time I walk in the door I just hang my keys on a hook by the door. And this has worked really well. So I probably lose my keys now like once a week instead of 10 times a day. So systems can be really important. And once you build out good ones, the systems turn into routines, and they can become almost automatic. They require less and less energy over time.

**Для Напарника:** Закреплять один ритуал за раз (одна привычка входа в Фокус), а не набор из десяти.



# 🎬 What Your Phone Is Really Doing to Your Brain — Mental Maps


### Рычаги: выключить уведомления, добавить трение, менять контекст, а не волю  ·  [07:55](https://youtu.be/-Ur8vPRhSTc?t=475)
**Мысль:** Решение о среде принимается один раз и не требует воли в моменте; трение работает, потому что мозг ленив к усилию.

> First lever, change the defaults. Turn off all non-essential notifications, not reduce them, turn them off. Notifications are interruptions by design. When you check the app on your schedule instead of responding to a buzz, you've already reclaimed a significant amount of autonomy. Studies show that notification-free phone use produces the same content consumption with dramatically lower stress and distraction. Second lever, introduce friction. The platforms remove friction to keep you in. You can add it back. Move social apps off your home screen, so opening them requires deliberate steps. Use a separate browser tab instead of the app. It is clunkier, and that is exactly the point. Some people put a rubber band around their phone as a physical reminder to pause. These are not perfect solutions, but friction works because our brains are genuinely lazy about effort. Third lever, design your environment, not just your willpower. Willpower is a limited resource that depletes throughout the day. Environmental design is permanent. Keeping your phone in another room while you work does not require any discipline in the moment. The decision was made once. This is a core insight from behavioral economics. The best way to change behavior is to change the context in which the behavior happens, not to try harder.

**Для Напарника:** Подсказка в настройке сессии: решение «телефон подальше» принимается один раз перед стартом, а не посреди работы.



# 🎬 What's your game's core loop? — Ivan Alexiev Game Design


### Простая ядровая петля; убирай всё, что её не поддерживает  ·  [06:45](https://youtu.be/TzSp47iEFh8?t=405)
**Мысль:** Определи самое частое действие игрока и поддерживай его; дизайнер за две сессии вырезал лишнее, и игра стала весёлой и понятной.

> It can be really simple. Like I've heard a lot of people talk about in the video game space about how like a lot of side sidescrollers they see jumping as their main game loop. Like they see just that how do we make moving around in the game fun cuz that's the thing that players are going to be doing most of they're going to be running around the world. And so I think in board games similarly identify those things that players are going to be doing a lot of and make sure that those things are being supported by the game. Like what happened with a designer who came to me with a game that he wasn't sure if it was fun. We identified the core game loop. We did two sessions where we just removed stuff that wasn't supporting the core game loop. And in two sessions, he went from not being sure if it's fun to absolutely the game being fun, testing really well, and it literally getting to a place where it's currently being pitched and the designer is like really proud of it. That can happen when you identify the game's loop. Take out stuff that doesn't support it. And in so many cases, that just immediately frees up the game. It makes it makes it really clear to players what they're doing.

**Для Напарника:** Ядро Напарника: шаг -> Фокус -> рост острова. Любую новую фичу сверять: поддерживает ли она это ядро; иначе убирать.



# 🎬 Why Cause and Effect is Vital for Great Game Design — Madbook


### Не набор приёмов, а общий принцип причины и следствия  ·  [04:09](https://youtu.be/SdJo-AGN4SQ?t=249)
**Мысль:** Тряска экрана работает, потому что логично следует из взрыва. Чтобы расти, нужно понимать общую идею, а не частные советы.

> So why is screenshake so popular i mean i've suggested it on this channel at least like twice well it's really easy to do and it's advice that generally applies to a lot of people about 90 percent of the games i see involve some sort of guns or explosions and if you think about it the camera shaking after a big explosion makes sense the screen shake effect is caused by the explosions it's a really clear logical flow of events but like i said i'm pretty tired of this quite generic tip it's not a bad tip at all it does really work but if we want to level up our skills as game devs we need to start to understand how to use general ideas like cause and effect instead of specific tips that are good in particular use cases like screen shade.

**Для Напарника:** Не лепить эффекты по списку; каждый эффект должен быть логичным следствием события.



# 🎬 Why Cozy Games Keep Disappointing Us - And Who's Actually Getting It Right — JaeTeeGames


### Чек-лист против вайба: UI-тест, проверка глаголов, обзоры с трением  ·  [11:22](https://youtu.be/Pu6dw07JEo4?t=682)
**Мысль:** Три вопроса: прячет ли трейлер интерфейс, можно ли описать игру без слов cozy/cute, что жалуются играющие 10+ часов. Это отделяет петлю от скринсейвера.

> I call it the tea time checklist. Before I buy any cozy game and before you back any Kickstarter, I want you to run it through these three questions. Question one is the UI test. Pause the trailer. Ignore the cinematic pan over the waterfall and look for the menus. Does the font look readable? Is the infantry grid organized? If the trailer hides the UI, it is usually because the UI is bad. If I can't see the infantry system in the promotional material, I assume the game is a vibe and not a loop. Question two is the verb check. Describe the game without using the words relaxing, cute, or cozy. What do you actually do? Do you farm? Do you cook? Do you organize? If the only way you can describe the game is it's a vibe, then there is no game there. That is a screen saver. And screen savers shouldn't cost $25. And question three is the friction review. Go to the Steam reviews. Filter by negative. Ignore the people complaining about crashes on launch day. Look for the people with 10 plus hours played. Are they complaining about movement speed? Are they complaining about inventory space? Are they saying the dialogue repeats too often? Those are the grounded reviews. These are the people telling you the truth about the loop.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг Напарника стоит строить на глаголах (что делаешь: берёшь шаг, садишься в фокус, растишь остров), а не на словах про уют и милоту.



# 🎬 Why Does Celeste Feel So Good to Play? — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Итерация: детали меняются вместе с уровнями; выброшенная работа — тоже обучение  ·  [14:07](https://youtu.be/yorTG9at90g?t=847)
**Мысль:** Нужно позволять частям игры менять друг друга и не бояться выбрасывать идеи: это знание остаётся.

> We decided early on what we wanted Madeline to be able to do, but the details of the numbers defintiely changed, and then the levels also changed, in tandem with that. So you basically get the player character to where it feels alright, you know it’s not perfect, you know you have to do testing and stuff, but you need levels to test too. So you make a bunch of levels and then you test, and you find out "oh the player character has to change", and then you have to change 20 levels. You have to let different parts change other parts of the game, and let it slowly reveal what it wants to be. NOEL: And you have to be willing to throw away stuff sometimes too. Like "this is a cool idea and a cool direction but it wasn’t working". It’s progressing what the game is because you’re learning about what the game actually is and figuring things out so even though you’re throwing work away, it’s a learning experience that ultimately makes the game better. MATT: Yeah, it’s still work that goes towards completing the game, even if it doesn’t manifest as content. It becomes part of your knowledge that you use to make the rest of the content.

**Для Напарника:** Процесс разработки Напарника: не держаться за фичу, если реальный рендер на реалистичных данных показывает проблему.

### Дизайн это не магия, а воронка: широко открыть и сужать  ·  [01:46](https://youtu.be/QO5oY_549wY?t=106)
**Мысль:** Результат рождается процессом уточнения: сначала открываем воронку, затем постепенно отбираем и доводим.

> That was enlightening for me right it opened my mind to go oh okay just like there's a process for all these other things it wasn't just magic and you say ah there it is it was really a process of refinement you know of opening the funnel at the beginning and refining down over time to get to that final.

**Для Напарника:** Для экранов Напарника: сначала несколько вариантов, затем отбор по контрасту и принципам (CLAUDE.md §9), а не правка по вкусу.


### Вместо «да-да-нет-нет» искать общее решение дизайна и реализации  ·  [02:20](https://youtu.be/QO5oY_549wY?t=140)
**Мысль:** Ограничения (объём, стоимость) решаются совместным поиском других характеристик дизайна и реализации, а не отказом.

> What can we de deliver in volume what can we do different things so you always had these constraints that you had to work with under and sometimes they and the team not just i would say we need this we're like we can't deliver that but maybe we were able to work together to find different design characteristics and different implementation characteristics that could get to that point without what they were describing and instead of yes no let's find some other way to solve the problem together.



# 🎬 Why Jony Ive put buttons in the electric Ferrari — PRNDL by Jordan Golson


### Универсальный экран в машине не работает: нужное количество кнопок для нужных действий  ·  [00:35](https://youtu.be/6Wv1btxCjVE?t=35)
**Мысль:** Телефон должен уметь всё, поэтому экран универсален; автомобилю нужны руление и несколько действий, значит физические переключатели для конкретных задач.

> And he told us that this is designed the way it is because it needs to be everything. It needs to be a camera, it needs to be for text messaging, it needs to be for email, it needs to be for watching videos, it needs to be for a million different things, and so that's what it did. The problem is it was so successful that every car maker in the world tried to copy it and said, "Let's put screens in all our cars." Except a screen in a car doesn't work because you don't need to text message in a car, you need to drive, or you need to see where you're going, or you need to change the music. And so that's why when Ive came with Ferrari to build this new user interface for their new electric car, he put switches that the phone doesn't have. And so he rethought how everyone has built the user interface for their cars, got rid of the single screen with no buttons, and now we have the exact right number of buttons to do the exact right things.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник не должен быть универсальным комбайном: несколько прямых действий (начать, пауза, завершить) вместо меню.


### Какую проблему решаем? Остальные пытались решить все сразу  ·  [14:12](https://youtu.be/6Wv1btxCjVE?t=852)
**Мысль:** Главный вопрос Айва: какую проблему вы пытаетесь решить; производители с экранами решали все проблемы и не решили ни одной.

> Do you want it to feel? How do you want it to look? How do you want to drive the experience to make the digital car feel analog? And that's what they've done with all of this. Everything is physical. Everything is tactile. Everything has a purpose. And that was the thing that Johnny wanted to talk about most was what problem are you trying to solve? And that's what he felt with all the other car makers putting screens in their car. They didn't know what problem they were trying to solve. They were trying to solve every problem. And all he wanted to do was to make a better driver's car.

**Для Напарника:** Единственная проблема Напарника: помочь начать и удержаться в коротком фокусе; всё, что к ней не относится, откладывать.



# 🎬 Why the 60-30-10 Rule is RUINING Your UI Designs — Kole Jain


### Тематизация: сдвиг светлоты и хромы нейтралей в OKLCH и смена оттенка  ·  [05:31](https://youtu.be/66oOi9OLMCw?t=331)
**Мысль:** Для каждого нейтрала немного опускают светлоту и поднимают хрому, затем меняют оттенок и получают согласованную тему.

> Last but not least, layer four, theming. If you want to convert any of your designs into a color themed version, it's actually a lot easier than you think. You could take all of your neutrals and guess, but this is where the okay LCH color system becomes super useful. For every neutral you have, plug in the hex value and drop the lightness by 003 and increase the chroma by 0.02. And then just adjust the hue to get any themed output. This means you can theme any design to red, green, blue. bang on the money every single time.

**Для Напарника:** Часовые SKY-палитры можно получать из нейтралей смещением оттенка и хромы при фиксированной светлоте.



# 🎬 Why Your App Onboarding Is Failing (And How To Fix It) — Appelune


### Честные ограничения оценки: чужие тесты, победы публикуют вендоры, пять правок разом  ·  [08:36](https://youtu.be/xs4kPi7xlFM?t=516)
**Мысль:** Автор прямо говорит, что тесты из других типов приложений, вендоры публикуют только успехи, изменений было несколько, и реальная команда должна запускать сплит-тест. (Пример Cobalt — вымышленное приложение.)

> What this can't tell you. The tests come from other kinds of apps, and vendors publish their wins. And Cobalt made 5 changes at once. We also didn't count fewer day-0 cancellations, because we found no test that measured it. A real team would ship this as a split test, and read it on onboarding complete and trial started. So onboarding, counted honestly, closes a small part of the gap.

# 🎬 Why Your Brain Can't Focus Anymore (The Neuroscience No One Explains) — Quiet Neuroscience


### Фокус — не воля, а дизайн среды и подсказок (Фогг)  ·  [06:53](https://youtu.be/1294OUlmwtA?t=413)
**Мысль:** Поведение = мотивация, способность и подсказки; среда с триггерами отвлечения гарантирует отвлечение независимо от силы воли.

> We treat focus like it's an internal resource. like you either have willpower today or you don't. Like the solution is to want it more, try harder, be more disciplined. And that framing is almost completely wrong. Focus is not primarily a willpower problem. It is an environment design problem. Your brain doesn't operate in isolation. It operates in context. And your context is constantly giving your brain signals. Is this a place to focus? Is this a place to relax? Is this a place where distractions are normal? Stanford psychologist BJ Fog has spent decades researching this. His conclusion, behavior is a function of motivation, ability, and crucially prompts. If your environment is filled with prompts for distraction, you will be distracted. Not because you're weak, because you're human. Your brain is exquisitly sensitive to environmental cues. That's a feature, not a bug. It's just currently calibrated to a world optimized for your distraction.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус-экран должен быть средой без подсказок отвлечения; AppBackdrop как сигнал «здесь место для фокуса».


### Телефон в другую комнату и убрать подсказки: инструменты среды, не костыли  ·  [10:23](https://youtu.be/1294OUlmwtA?t=623)
**Мысль:** Даже выключенный телефон в поле зрения съедает рабочую память; блокировщики убирают подсказки, чтобы мозгу не приходилось с ними бороться. Рекомендуют начинать с 45 минут.

> Stop trying to do focused work while leaving every distraction open. This sounds obvious, but almost nobody actually does it. When you start a focused work block, and I'd recommend starting with just 45 minutes, put your phone in a different room. Not face down, a different room. Research shows that even a phone that is face down, silenced, and not being used reduces working memory capacity just by being in your visual field. The brain is allocating resources to resist checking it. Close your email. Close Slack. Put your browser in full screen mode. Use a distraction blocker if you need to. Freedom, cold turkey, one focus. These are not crutches. They are environment design tools. They remove the prompts so your brain doesn't have to fight them.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист перед стартом сессии в настройке шага: «телефон подальше», с дружелюбным голосом напарника; 45 мин как вариант длительности.


### Аудит отвлечений: небольшое трение ломает автоматический цикл  ·  [12:15](https://youtu.be/1294OUlmwtA?t=735)
**Мысль:** Лёгкое трение (убрать иконку с главного экрана) достаточно, чтобы разорвать автоматику; всё, что не относится к текущей задаче, не должно быть видимым.

> Do a distraction audit right now after this video. Actually do this. Open your phone. Go through every app. Ask one question for each one. Does this app serve me or does it extract from me? The apps that extract from you that you open out of habit rather than intention, delete them or move them off your home screen. make access slightly harder. That tiny friction is enough to break the automatic behavior loop. And for your workspace, one simple rule. If it's not related to the work you're doing right now, it should not be visible. Visible things compete for attention. A clear desk is not just aesthetic. It is cognitive infrastructure.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип минимального UI на Фокусе: на экране только текущий шаг и таймер.



# 🎬 Why Your Brain Wants Videos You Don't Like — Curiocos


### Implementation intention: заранее «если..., то...»  ·  [07:23](https://youtu.be/E455eW6RqPc?t=443)
**Мысль:** План на триггер переносит вознаграждающее поведение на ту же подсказку и снижает умственные усилия выбора в момент импульса.

> Strategy comes from general behavioral psychology. It's about making a specific plan for when a cue strikes. This is called an implementation intention. It's a simple if-then statement you decide on in advance. For example, if I feel the urge to scroll after dinner, >> >> then I will read one chapter of my book. This isn't about fighting the urge in the moment. It's about pre-loading a different, more rewarding behavior onto the same trigger. By deciding ahead of time, you reduce the mental effort needed to make a better choice when the cue appears.

**Для Напарника:** В чек-ине вечера/утра предложить одну фразу «когда X, тогда первый шаг Y» голосом напарника.



# 🎬 Workflows at Scale: Design Systems, Platform Engineering, and the AI Workflows that scale — Design Systems London


### ИИ не чинит сломанную систему, а усиливает то, что есть  ·  [10:28](https://youtu.be/pI12krTC5n8?t=628)
**Мысль:** Несогласованная система даст несогласованность в масштабе, а ИИ при хардкоде токенов будет уверенно ошибаться. При структурной системе ИИ становится множителем.

> AI doesn't fix a broken design system. It amplifies what's already there. If your system is inconsistent, AI will generate inconsistency at scale. If your tokens are hard-coded and your components undocumented, AI will guess and AI will guess wrong confidently. But if your system is structured, your tokens semantic, your APIs consistent, and your documentation clear, AI becomes a genuine multiplier.

**Для Напарника:** Перед генерацией UI через ИИ в Напарнике держать токены и компоненты упорядоченными; CLAUDE.md выполняет роль такого брифа.


### Не промпт, а бриф: дай ИИ токены, компоненты и гайдлайны  ·  [11:13](https://youtu.be/pI12krTC5n8?t=673)
**Мысль:** Агент хорош ровно настолько, насколько хорош контекст; ему нужны файл токенов, компоненты, бренд-гайд, как новому дизайнеру, а не весь код без ограничений.

> Most people think scaling with AI is about writing better prompts. It isn't. It's about context. The agent is only as good as what you give it to work with. If you're If you give it your entire codebase without constraints, it will produce something that technically works but visually fails. If you give it your token file, your component file, or your brand guidelines, you're not prompting, you're briefing. And that's a very different thing. Think of it like you're onboarding a new designer. You don't just give them a laptop and say, "Good luck." Well, I hope that's not what you do. You'd give them the brand guidelines, the component library, the do's, the don'ts. AI needs the same briefing.

**Для Напарника:** Системные промпты api/companion и api/session-voice: передавать краткий бриф тона и запретов (без стыда, без вины), а не только просьбу.



# 🎬 WWDC24: Design great visionOS apps | Apple — Apple Developer


### Найди «ключевой момент» приложения, оптимизированный под платформу  ·  [01:18](https://youtu.be/9QcD2pGLwzg?t=78)
**Мысль:** Во встроенном приложении осознанности цветок мягко пульсирует и расширяется, побуждая дышать и фокусироваться; это опыт, возможный только на этой платформе.

> When we first work with a team we encourage developers to find their key moment, which is the moment in an app that’s optimized for visionOS. The Mindfulness app is an example on the system. The flower has a gentle pulse that encourages you to breathe and focus. When the flower expands it gives a sense of life to the scene.

**Для Напарника:** Определить ключевой момент Напарника (например, проявление острова в конце сессии) и вложить туда основную полировку.


### Не переносить интерфейс как есть: десятки прототипов ради лучшего опыта на платформе  ·  [02:40](https://youtu.be/9QcD2pGLwzg?t=160)
**Мысль:** Команда Loona решила не делать оконный порт, а построить много прототипов; плоские эскизы стали 3D-моделями с деталями и тонкой анимацией.

> Great solutions take time to build and they’re a result of iteration and prototyping. This is Loona. The iOS app offers gracefully animated scenes that combine relaxing activities, storytelling, and atmospheric sounds to help you calm down or fall asleep. Bringing this UI into a windowed experience on visionOS would have been reasonable. But listen to how the team approached it. This is Sergey Gonchar, the co-founder of Loona: We just put ourselves in the mindset, we’re going to build a lot of prototypes, because we want to find the best possible experience we can shape for this platform. Take a look at the early sketches of their scooter concept. The 2D prototypes felt dimensional, they were begging to be broken into pieces to play with. And it took time, but these sketches evolved into 3D models, which took on a life of their own, with tons of fun, incredible detail and subtle animations.

**Для Напарника:** Прототипировать несколько вариантов экрана Мира и награды, а не шлифовать первый.


### Тестируй на людях ложные срабатывания и убирай сложность, не нужную для главной радости  ·  [16:53](https://youtu.be/9QcD2pGLwzg?t=1013)
**Мысль:** Жест поднесения руки к уху пришлось проверять на ложные срабатывания; сложный ручной подбор темпа автоматизировали, чтобы человек сосредоточился на творческой части.

> They realized they had to account for false positives, such as when someone may be raising their hand to fix their hair or adjust the strap on Vision Pro. Here’s how Karim, the CEO and co-founder of Algoriddim, approached this: This required a lot of user testing. We had to gather feedback to ensure the gesture was accurately recognized. Getting feedback is so important. You may be surprised by the way people use your app and certain behaviors they have that may be different than your expectations. Understand the opportunities where it’s possible to abstract away the literal interpretation in order to make something more approachable and less complex. For DJay, let’s take a closer look at the turn table. Turn tables have existed in their analog form for quite some time, but the technology surrounding them has evolved tremendously. For example, matching tempos and syncing tracks can be complex on traditional hardware. There’s a barrier to entry for people who aren’t familiar with it. The Algoriddim team saw this as an opportunity. In the 3D environment, we asked ourselves, “Why a DJ would even need to manually match tempos? When we could completely automate the process under the hood?" This abstraction not only reduces complexity, but also enhances the user’s ability to focus more on the creative aspects of DJing. Removing complexity can help people enjoy the aspects of your app that resonate the most.

**Для Напарника:** Убрать из Фокуса всё, что отвлекает от главного: автоматические умолчания (25 мин) вместо настроек; проверять на реальных пользователях неожиданное поведение.



# 🎬 WWDC25: Make a big impact with small writing changes | Apple — Apple Developer


### Словарь терминов: одно слово на одно понятие, с определением  ·  [09:23](https://youtu.be/iaafpzDJHS8?t=563)
**Мысль:** Таблица: слова, которые используем, слова, которых избегаем, и короткое определение. Единый термин (алиас, а не ник или имя) убирает путаницу, а список растёт по слову за раз.

> The last small change is a little different because it’s something you do because it’s something you do outside of your app, making a word list. Once you have one, you use it to give the language in your app more consistency. Think of the words you would use in your app, and the ones you wouldn’t, and write them down. This is a great exercise to do at the start of development, but you can really do this at any stage. Your word list can take any format that works for you, but I find a table works well. In your first column, you’ll put any words you do plan to use in your app. For example, if I'm working on a game, the first thing players might have to do in that game is choose their in-game name. This game calls that an alias. I’ll add that to the first column. Then, in the second column, I’ll put in the words I don’t plan to use to refer to the same thing. In this case, words like Handle, User Name, and Title. And if it’s helpful, you can also add a simple definition. I’ll add one that says “The player’s in-game name, shown to other players, Not used to sign in.” Now, when I need to give an instruction or refer to this name in the app, I know to always call it an “alias” and not something different. That consistency helps provide clarity for the people using the app. Similarly, I might add the word “health” and avoid the words “lives”, “hearts”, “energy”, and “stamina” with a definition of “Measure of player longevity.” I’ll keep going in a similar way. Whenever I add a new term or think of one already in the app, I'll add it to the list. As this word list grows, it becomes a resource that anyone working on this app can use to know how it should sound. Don’t let the idea of creating a comprehensive word list overwhelm you. This session is about small changes, so just think about adding a word at a time. Once you have even a partial word list, you can start to use it for the UX writing in your app. For example, when someone first sets up the game, the app might tell them: “Choose your alias.” For the description, I was even able to refer to the definition I wrote, that this is the name that is shown to other players. Similarly, to find someone to play with, I might give the instruction to “Find an alias.” This helps people know they aren’t meant to search for, say, an email address or user name. Notice also I chose the word "find." That’s another word I could add to my word list. So I'll do that now. Finally, a player might receive a notification letting them know about a requested “Match-Up”. The message says, “A player with the alias, “ExampleAlias”, wants to play.” Looking at these three examples together, you can see that I was consistent about using the term “alias”. I never called it a “user name” or “player name” or anything else, so people using the app would always know what I was referring to.

**Для Напарника:** Завести словарь Напарника: «Фокус», «напарник», «остров», «шаг»; закрепить, как зовётся каждая сущность по всему продукту.


### Читай свой текст вслух, чтобы услышать лишнее и повторы  ·  [15:08](https://youtu.be/iaafpzDJHS8?t=908)
**Мысль:** Проверка на слух быстро выявляет наполнители и дубли, это простая и действенная проверка текста.

> In previous writing sessions, we have concluded with a piece of advice that I think is worth repeating. Read your writing out loud. It makes it easier to hear those filler and repetitive words and know where to tighten up your writing.

**Для Напарника:** Перед релизом читать вслух все реплики кота: если неловко произнести, значит неестественно.



# 🎬 WWDC26: Craft clear names for features and labels in your app | Apple — Apple Developer


### Упражнение: думает, чувствует, делает; стикеры, группировка по темам  ·  [05:29](https://youtu.be/UFZvFqg6few?t=329)
**Мысль:** Не называть по технологии и функции, а спросить, что человек должен подумать, почувствовать и сделать; выписать идеи без фильтра, затем сгруппировать повторяющиеся темы (лёгкость, радость, безопасность).

> When you're building something, it's natural to name it by what it does, the technology behind it, or the function. But the people using your app don't see it that way. They want to know what it does for them. Who's your app for? New parents? People training for a marathon? Start by getting clear on your audience first, and try this exercise. With the audience in mind, ask yourself when they encounter this feature, what should they think, feel, and do? So let's walk through this exercise together. Imagine you're part of the Apple Maps team, and you're naming a feature that remembers the places you've been; that new café you discovered last week, or the park with the accessible trails. We'll apply our criteria as we go. Because without the perspective of who you're naming for, the criteria can seem abstract. With it, they become specific and actionable. On your own or with your team, write down a few thoughts in each of these sections, one idea per sticky note. Think. What does the team want people to think when using this type of feature? Maybe … that it's easy, or helpful, or even clever. When you do this, write down as many ideas as you can and don't filter yet. You're not looking for the perfect word. You're looking for the themes that keep coming up. Then move on to "feel". What should people feel? Like when you find a place you couldn't remember the name of for the longest time. That can feel like a fun moment. And secure. Knowing your places are private and encrypted. Both matter here. And finally, "do". The team wants people to find this feature, use it, and share places with whomever they want. Now keep in mind that I'm only showing a few examples here. You might be surprised by how many you or your team actually come up with. More ideas are better here. Once all the ideas are down, step back. You'll start to notice things repeating different words pointing to the same feeling. That's when you start to group them by theme. For visited places, a few themes came up. Ease — finding what you're looking for without effort. Excitement — the fun surprise of rediscovering a place you loved. And security. Because this feature was built with privacy in mind, that has to come through.

**Для Напарника:** Для названий фич Напарника: сессия думать/чувствовать/делать для аудитории с СДВГ, темы: без усилий, тепло, безопасность.


### Тест названия: подставь в фразу, которую скажешь другу  ·  [08:11](https://youtu.be/UFZvFqg6few?t=491)
**Мысль:** Название вставляют в реплики вроде «зацени...» и «найди...»; естественное звучание отбирает кандидатов. Visited Places выиграло за описательность, привычное слово и перевод.

> What name could capture ease? Excitement? A sense of security? We can probably cross out a few right away, because they don't meet the criteria we've been talking about. Some of these just don't fit Apple. Some feel a little vague, while others might not translate well. You can narrow down the options even further with a simple test. Take the names and drop them into the sentences you might read or say to a friend. Something like: Hey, check out Private Memories. Or just search for Private Memories. A name that reads and sounds natural — that's a name worth exploring further. The Apple Maps team ultimately landed on Visited Places. Descriptive, clear, and already at home in an interface that uses the word places throughout. It also sets the right expectation about ownership these are your places, that can't be read by Apple. And it works in different languages. When you're evaluating your own options, look for the name that honors the themes you identified and tests well against the criteria.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять названия вслух: «зайди в Мир», «запусти Фокус», «посмотри остров».


### Подбор названия: глагол даёт контроль, но термин инженера описывает технологию, а не опыт  ·  [10:34](https://youtu.be/UFZvFqg6few?t=634)
**Мысль:** От Isolate Vocals через Clarify Speech и Enhance Playback к Enhance Dialogue: название должно отвечать, что улучшается и для кого, ещё до нажатия.

> Let's walk through an example in Apple Podcasts. Audio quality in podcasts can vary wildly, some shows are polished, while others are recorded in kitchens or cars. So here's where that feature lives, in a menu that also controls the playback speed. It isolates voices, reducing background noise without altering the original audio. There are a few ideas that pop up. Right away, vocal isolation comes to mind. But wait… For a feature like this, a verb works well because we're putting the person using it in control. The feature is something you do, not something you have. So let's try Isolate Vocals instead. But, that's an audio engineering term. It describes what the technology does, not what you experience. How about Clarify Speech? Closer. But it only tells half the story. There's more to this feature than clarity alone. Another obvious choice might be something like Enhance Playback, but it puts the feature before the person. What's being enhanced? For whom? How about Enhance Dialogue? It answers both questions — what's being enhanced and for whom before you even tap. And that's exactly what they went with. It fits the context. It sets the right expectation. And when you turn the feature on, it delivers exactly what it promised.

**Для Напарника:** Кнопки действий называть глаголом из мира человека («Разбить на шаги»), а не внутренним термином (breakdown, decompose).



# 🎬 WWDC26: Create UI prototypes using agents in Xcode | Apple — Apple Developer


### Размытый промпт даёт произвольный макет; задавай признаки, настроение и просись у агента несколько вариантов  ·  [03:04](https://youtu.be/QleOvMW9vTU?t=184)
**Мысль:** Расплывчатый запрос закрепляет случайную навигацию и угаданные функции, а потом изменения превращаются в раздутый интерфейс. Нужны конкретные функции, стилистические подсказки и множество вариантов на раннем этапе.

> If you're like me, you just want to get started. With agents right in Xcode, you might be tempted to ask, "create a UI for managing a book club that meets regularly." This could generate an interesting result and quickly but the prompt is vague and it brings a few problems. The agent generated an arbitrary layout. While it might still work, it's one of many ways of organizing an app like this. And it also took a guess at the app features because it wasn't clearly defined in the prompt. Suppose we aren't interested in polling or a photo gallery, making it easy to get stuck or anchored on a flawed starting point. For example, we're now stuck with this arbitrary navigation element that might have made more sense with a different feature set. By the time we've gotten the interface to display the features we do want, it could present feature creep, looking clunky and inelegant. To avoid this, your prompts need to be much more specific. Take some time to think through the features and high-level points you want the app to have from the get-go. When it comes to your app and what problems you want it to solve, you likely have a better idea where to start than the agent does. Give stylistic cues. If you have an intuition about the mood or feeling you want your app to evoke, express those in the prompt. For example, for a book club, do you want to capture the warm atmosphere of a coffee shop and its color palette? Or do you want to highlight the feeling of paper and beautiful typography? Lastly, and most importantly, ask for multiple options. Early on is your best opportunity to evaluate and explore multiple and divergent directions.

**Для Напарника:** При работе ИИ-агентов над Напарником: давать явные функции и настроение (тёплый, прощающий) и просить 3-5 именованных вариантов для сравнения.


### Наполнять прототип реалистичным контентом и краевыми случаями, а не заглушками  ·  [07:37](https://youtu.be/QleOvMW9vTU?t=457)
**Мысль:** Агент играет роль живого пользователя: правдоподобный контент, неограниченно растущие элементы, длинный ввод и пустые состояния. Данные лежат отдельным файлом, чтобы легко менять.

> Early on in the development of your app, it's a great idea to let different people try it and give you feedback to guide your process of refinement. For some kinds of apps and interfaces though you still might be a ways off from having it in a state where people can try it with their own content. Fortunately, the agent can help play the role of somebody using your app so you can get an impression of what your app feels like when it's lived in Combined with images that you can bring in, an agent can get you from this kind of blank template, only loosely filled with content, to something rich, lived in, and closer to real use. Here are some prompting tips when working through how to make your app feel lived in. First, and this is a recurring theme, ask for not just one preview, but many previews. Second, stop and think through edge cases yourself. For example, in my app, how does the detail area look if no meeting has been scheduled yet? Try to be specific about what parts of the interface state should be iterated, minimizing the risk that the agent overlooks something. Make sure that there's enough context that the sample content is plausible for your app's audience. In this case, that means I need to make sure that the sample app content, such as the discussions, center around books. Pay attention to interface elements that can grow unbounded: the number of members in the club, the length of the message conversations, the number of previous books, and so on... It might also include things like long input. Is it appropriate for the text to truncate? Or to take multiple lines? If applicable, ask the coding agent to make sure that the example content it is authoring is reusable and readable in its own file so that you can go in and make changes if you need to.

**Для Напарника:** Совпадает с уроком проекта о реалистичных данных: тестировать остров на 9 ориентирах, а не на одном; хранить сид-данные отдельным файлом.


### Находки на реальном контенте: пустые состояния, обрезка длинного текста, сокращение списка  ·  [09:54](https://youtu.be/QleOvMW9vTU?t=594)
**Мысль:** На реальных данных вылезают отсутствующие пустые состояния, перекрытие обложки длинным текстом и слишком длинный лидерборд; решение: всегда показывать собственную позицию и раскрывать полный список.

> Each variation gets its own tab, so I can quickly switch between them. In this example, I notice I don't have any real blank slate UI, no way of specifying a new book or even to manage my account. I address that by adding account management and call-to-action controls. In this variation, I noticed that really long next meeting descriptions might overrun the book cover. I address that by allowing truncation. In fact, I realize the title of the book is redundant with its cover, so I simplify it. Here I notice that the leaderboard, when there are too many participants, gets way too long, and it takes a long time to scroll to see the discussion. Instead, I can simply ensure that somebody can always see what their relative rank is, and with an expand control, see the full list.

**Для Напарника:** Дом и Мир: проверять пустые состояния (нет плана на сегодня) и длинные списки шагов; показывать только текущий шаг с раскрытием остальных.


### Панель настройки анимации: разбить на фазы и дать общий словарь  ·  [13:27](https://youtu.be/QleOvMW9vTU?t=807)
**Мысль:** Вместо правки констант в коде делают отдельную панель параметров; анимацию разбивают на фазы, это даёт общий словарь людям и агенту и позволяет настраивать каждую фазу изолированно.

> This can work really well, but sometimes the constants I'm interested in live in different parts of the code, and the context switching can feel clunky. You might find it easier to create a custom UI whose job it is to help you tune those specific parameters that are relevant to your interface. Here's a simple interface to demonstrate this principle. Here I'm just tossing an interface element at a goal to understand the different spring properties at play for this kind of interaction. A menu button brings up a tuning panel that allows me to try different parameters. Here, Xcode can help you immensely. Instead of building the UI directly, enlist the agent to build a UI to display tunable parameters. When you ask the agent in Xcode for help with building a tuning panel for your UI, here are some best practices. Be as detailed as possible about what you want to iterate on. Are you exploring different animation styles or the specifics of spring curves? Does your animation feature multiple elements or does it feature a transition in which elements enter and leave your view hierarchy? To keep your tuning panel easy to understand, ask for the animation to be broken into phases. This also gives you and the agent a shared vocabulary about the specific parts of the animation.

**Для Напарника:** Для RevealIsland и островной анимации роста: описать фазы (проклюнулся, вырос, осел) и вынести тайминги в отладочную панель.


### Сократить цикл обратной связи: делать панель настройки при выборе между параметрами  ·  [17:09](https://youtu.be/QleOvMW9vTU?t=1029)
**Мысль:** Когда нужно выбирать между конфигурациями вида или параметрами анимации, панель настройки сокращает цикл и быстрее приводит к ощущению оптимума.

> Let's look at it again. This is one of the most powerful flows in prototyping with coding agents in Xcode. Anytime you're trying to manage multiple configurations of a view or deciding between different animation or interaction parameters, make a tuning panel, shorten the feedback loop, and get to what feels optimal for your app.

**Для Напарника:** Инструмент разработки: тюнинг-панель для пружин и задержек пик-момента сессии.



# 🎬 WWDC26: Principles of great design | Apple — Apple Developer


### Каждая фича просит время, внимание и доверие  ·  [00:32](https://youtu.be/Xa9L2yx_QH8?t=32)
**Мысль:** Дизайн это создание с намерением; каждая функция требует от человека ценных ресурсов, поэтому выбор что строить часто есть выбор, что не включать.

> For us at Apple, design is making something with intention. It's focusing on what's most important to people, so you can build something they will truly value. Every feature you add to your product, asks something of the person using it. It asks for their time, their attention, and their trust. These are valuable things you can't afford to waste. So choosing what to build, is often a matter of deciding what not to include. Before you a draw a single sketch, or write a line of code, think about whether what you're making has purpose.

**Для Напарника:** Фильтр для любой новой механики Напарника: чего она просит у человека с СДВГ и стоит ли это; склоняться к вычёркиванию.



# 🎬 You'll Regret NOT Using The 8pt Grid in Figma (UI/UX Beginner Tutorial) — Tim Gabe


### Зачем сетка 4/8: убирает случайность и упрощает общение с разработчиком  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/ohF93_k3IMk?t=22)
**Мысль:** Фиксированный набор чисел исключает 52 здесь и 47 там, а любое значение вне шкалы сразу заметно и дизайнеру, и разработчику.

> The eight point or four point grid system is basically just you as a designer adhering to heights widths sizes that are divisible by eight or four in most cases and we'll get to some cases where you don't want to do it so that means that you'll use pixel sizes like 4 8 12 16 24 32 40 48 all the way up to whatever number you feel is needed for your designs and what this does for you it just creates consistency so it eliminates randomness and creates consistency so you can be sure that your designs are all going to look very consistent because you have a consistent sizing system you don't have to worry that oh wait a second did i use 52 here and 47 there and then 31 here no you know that maybe for most buttons i'm using 40 48 and 64. for most paddings it's 32 or 24. it's much easier to stay consistent and eliminate randomness and the second thing and the last thing for why we use it is for communication so if you're working with developers you're working with designers in your design files knowing that we just need to remember the numbers four and eight is gonna help a lot so if a developer sees in your design file that wait a second this is 51 pixels and we're using numbers divisible by 4 and 8. something is wrong here then they might address that and come back to you and talk to you about it the same with designers they're gonna see that something is wrong here and they're also gonna know that when we're designing our paddings it's not gonna be some random number i know that it's either 24 or 32. and it reduces the amount of choices you have and the amount of possibilities for error so great for communication.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать шкалу 4/8/12/16/24/32 в Tailwind и сверять скриншоты на странные значения.



# 🎬 Your App Passed the Accessibility Audit. It's Still a Nightmare to Use. — Moriel Schottlender


### Compliance theater: WCAG — пол, а не потолок  ·  [02:00](https://youtu.be/u-wJtB1ogZA?t=120)
**Мысль:** Аудит ловит alt-тексты и контраст, но не отвечает, может ли реальный человек с реальной целью пользоваться продуктом.

> The audit passes. Every checkbox is ticked. The accessibility report comes back green, and everyone breathes a sigh of relief and moves on. And the site is still functionally a cognitive obstacle course. This is what I'd call compliance theater. You've satisfied the standard, and the standard was never designed to be the ceiling. It was designed to be the floor. We've just been treating it as if it's the whole building, you know, with a pool and a sauna. WCAG is great at catching things like, "Is there an alt text on this image? Does this button have a label? Is the color contrast sufficient?" These are necessary questions, but they don't ask, "Can a real human with a real goal under real conditions actually use this thing?".

**Для Напарника:** Контраст 6.63:1 — минимум; сверять Напарник на реальных сценариях и реалистичных данных (§5.2 CLAUDE.md).


### Inclusive by default: сначала наименьшее лишнее трение, сложность — когда заслужила место  ·  [11:23](https://youtu.be/u-wJtB1ogZA?t=683)
**Мысль:** Это не про самого ограниченного пользователя, а про решения: меню «возвраты», скролл туда, куда указал, понятное имя, выход, которые принимались иначе ради эстетики и метрик.

> It doesn't mean designing for the most constrained possible user and calling it done. It means making the design decision that creates the least unnecessary friction first and then adding complexity only when it genuinely earns its place. The menu that says returns instead of connect, the scroll that goes where you point it, the name that tells you what the thing does even if it also has a clever brand name next to it, the exit ramp that's actually there. None of these are heroic accessibility work. They're just decisions. Decisions that got made the other way for reasons that usually had more to do with aesthetics or engagement metrics or what the design portfolio would look like.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип приёмки любой правки: убирает ли она лишнее трение; красота не должна его увеличивать.



# 🎬 Your Gamification Is Killing User Retention | Sahil Khanna — Litmus By Lapaas


### Пять ошибок геймификации и пять шагов внедрения: одно поведение, одна механика, путь восстановления, измерение  ·  [07:03](https://youtu.be/AiunXirNLcc?t=423)
**Мысль:** Награда за пустую активность, перегруженность механиками, петли стыда, бессмысленные награды и отсутствие измерения; выбирать одно поведение, связанное с удержанием, и сопоставить одну механику.

> The first mistake is rewarding the wrong behavior. If users can farm points without gaining real value, the system collapses. The second mistake is overcomplicating everything. Some products launch with levels, badges, streaks, leaderboards, XP, coins, challenges, and quests all at once. The result? Confusion. Most successful systems start with one mechanic. The third mistake is shame loops. Punishing users harshly for missing activity can destroy motivation. Good systems allow recovery. The fourth mistake is meaningless rewards. A badge without meaning becomes invisible. And the final mistake is not measuring impact. Teams celebrate launching gamification features, but never check whether retention actually improved. If users click badges, but don't use the product more, that's not engagement. That's decoration. How startups should implement gamification? If you're building a product, the process should look like this. Step one, identify one behavior strongly tied to retention. For example, completing a daily lesson, sending the first invoice, inviting a teammate, publishing a project. Step two, match one mechanic to that behavior. Consistency goals, streaks. Setup journeys, progress bars. Skill mastery, levels or badges. Step three, reward real progress, not empty activity. Step four, add a recovery path so users don't feel punished. And step five, measure whether retention actually improves. Because if gamification increases activity, but not outcomes, it's just noise.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист добавления фич: одно поведение (завершить шаг), одна механика (остров), путь восстановления после пропуска, замер реального эффекта.



# 🎬 Your Streak Owns You — BeanWise


### Этический вопрос задан и тут же снят выгодным ответом  ·  [04:04](https://youtu.be/__akVvonOqA?t=244)
**Мысль:** В Snap спросили, стоит ли поощрять такое поведение, и сразу ответили, что зависимость полезна для удержания; вопрос продержался одно предложение.

> Somebody in that thread did stop and ask the right question about a teenager who only opens the app to photograph a ceiling and keep a streak going. >> Is that the type of behavior we want to encourage? >> Then, in the same message, they answered themselves. If streaks turned out to be addictive, that would be healthy for long-term retention and engagement in the product. Healthy was the word they chose for a teenager who cannot go to sleep without photographing a ceiling. So, the question did get asked. It lasted one sentence. Nobody had to overrule it, and nobody had to argue, because the answer that made the number go up was sitting right there in the same paragraph.

**Для Напарника:** Нужен явный чек-лист этики для механик Напарника, который нельзя снять метрикой удержания.



# 🎬 You’re Not Weak-Willed—You’re Being Designed to Be Dopamine Addicted — MindShift Mastery


### Добавить трение там, где его нет, и окна для скролла: контейнмент вместо абстиненции  ·  [06:32](https://youtu.be/mR0tuPcEeqI?t=392)
**Мысль:** Удобство — это рулевой механизм; серый экран, выход из аккаунта, зарядка в другой комнате, два окна по 15 минут сокращают «maybe now», который держит дофаминовую систему в тревоге.

> Convenience is not neutral. It's a steering mechanism. Right now, your apps are on your home screen because they want to be the first thing your thumb hits. Move the most tempting ones off the first page. Turn your screen grayscale during work hours. Color is a reward cue, and grayscale makes the just check loop less tasty. Turn off non-human notifications. If a real person needs you, they'll call. Log out of the most addictive app so there's a password between you and the slot machine. Put your charger in another room after 9:00 p.m. or use a real alarm clock so your phone stops being the last face you see at night and the first in the morning. Friction isn't punishment. It's a seat belt for your attention. The third shift is to create designated scroll windows. This sounds counterintuitive, but humans respect boundaries they set consciously. If you give yourself two 15inute windows a day where you're allowed to scroll guilt free, your brain learns a rhythm. Novelty goes here. Focus goes there. Without windows, novelty leaks everywhere. This is not abstinence. It's containment. And containment is kinder because it reduces maybe now, which is what keeps your dopamine system on alert.

**Для Напарника:** Идея «окон» для Напарника: разрешённое время без вины в расписании дня; не запрет, а ритм.



# 🎬 Yu-Kai Chou Octalysis Framework — Josep Ramon Badia Albanell


### Чёрная шляпа может быть добровольной (будильник, сжигающий деньги); механики выбираются после определения нужного чувства  ·  [07:57](https://youtu.be/YO6uMBAnePE?t=477)
**Мысль:** Люди сами принимают потерю/штраф ради цели, но подход «налепить механик» не работает: сначала понять желаемое чувство, затем выбирать механику.

> Doesn't mean is necessarily bad a lot of people voluntarily put themselves in blackhead game vacation in order to go to the gym more often or eat healthfully so such as this alarm clock this alarm clock is a clock that's a that basically whenever you press the snooze button it'll destroy your money so now you are waking up because of core Drive eight loss and avoidance you don't want to lose money but because it's a goal that you want to accomplish you're okay with it so with octalus framework once you understand how you want people to feel and you and you understand how they're feeling right now that's when you go into the outer layer and figure out what game mechanics game elements game techniques are fit for that purpose it could be virtual Goods it could be appointment Dynamics maybe it's group Quest maybe should be social treasure maybe it's narrative the point here is that good gamification design is never a cookie cutter solution it's not like just slap things on and it works you really have to design things properly.

**Для Напарника:** Возможен добровольный «договор» с собой (необязательная фича), но по умолчанию без штрафов; сначала определять чувство, которое нужно вызвать у пользователя.



# 🎬 Как учиться с СДВГ (так чтобы запомнить) — Lina Mergold


### Нервная система перегружена мыслями; сначала освободи голову  ·  [05:02](https://youtu.be/a_5RBVncLQQ?t=302)
**Мысль:** Мозг и так работает на износ, запоминать новое сложно, пока голова забита; первый шаг - выгрузить мысли наружу.

> Во-первых, наша система, нервная система всё время перегружена количеством мыслей, переживаний, там тем, что нужно не забыть, тем, что нужно сделать. И так как наш мозг всё время работает на износ, ещё дополнительную информацию большую запомнить, это очень сложно. Поэтому в первую очередь нужно работать над тем, чтобы освободить свой мозг от количества мыслей.

**Для Напарника:** Быстрый «выгрузить из головы» перед сессией Фокуса (поле для мыслей в чате напарника).


### Мы непредсказуемы: давай несколько форматов и выбор по состоянию  ·  [11:01](https://youtu.be/a_5RBVncLQQ?t=661)
**Мысль:** «Терпи» не работает; удобнее иметь несколько источников и выбирать по настроению, играя вместе со своим мозгом, а не против него.

> Включить разный вид информации. То есть сегодня у меня настроение на дуалинга, завтра у меня настроение просто посмотреть сериальчик, послезавтра у меня настроение почитать реально текст. Я готова всё-таки вот классическую образование получить, чтобы у меня было несколько источников информации, и я могу выбрать, исходя из моего состояния. К сожалению, мы существа непредсказуемые, и просто сказать терпи, у нас так не получается. Мы пробовали, мы знаем, что это не работает. Поэтому легче играть в игру эту вместе,.

**Для Напарника:** Предлагать разные режимы сессии по состоянию («бодро», «мягко», «еле-еле») вместо одного способа.


### Подстройка темпа: в медленный день занимайся медленно, в быстрый - ускорься  ·  [13:42](https://youtu.be/a_5RBVncLQQ?t=822)
**Мысль:** Навязывать темп вопреки состоянию — стресс; подстройка под себя повышает шансы заниматься.

> У нас мозг работает очень быстро, поэтому если вы понимаете, что у вас сегодня медленный мозг, вот просто, ну, невозможно медленный, то и вы заниматься должны медленно. Потому что иначе это будет очень неприятно и стрессово. А если наоборот вы сегодня вот так вот бежите, бежите, бежите, то можете посмотреть лекцию на полторы, э, скорости, полтора, опять-таки, слушать какую-то музыку быструю, то есть подстраиваемся под себя, под свою жизнь, под свою реальность.

**Для Напарника:** Выбор длительности и интенсивности сессии каждый раз, а не фиксированный режим.

# 🎬 Приемы геймдизайна: Core loop — то, что заставляет нас играть — XYZ

# 🎬 😬 12 core strategies for ADULT ADHD & Executive Function (tips to live by) — Seth Perler, Executive Function, ADHD, 2e


### Frankenstudy: собери свою систему, доверяй себе  ·  [06:12](https://youtu.be/FpnCcfZmgO4?t=372)
**Мысль:** Нет «лучшей» системы для всех; пробуй разное, сшивай собственную, не поддавайся давлению «делай как у людей с сильными исполнительными функциями».

> Number two is called 'Frankenstudy.' This is just a term I use, long story behind that. But the gist is, when I'm working with kids, I want them to Frankenstudy, I don't want them to say "Oh, Seth has the best way of organizing papers and blah, blah, and schoolwork." No, I want to help the kids come up with their own Frankenstudy, their own frankensteined, customized, personalized tailored systems that work for them. Okay, so you should trust yourself. You probably have come up with tons of great strategies, and you will get pressure from family members or other people who really do have strong executive function, or books, or YouTube videos, or whatever. And they're again, going back to the guru syndrome, they're gonna say "This works. Do it this way." Well, you know what, that may not work for you. Trust yourself, try a bunch of different things. It's okay if they don't work, but you're ultimately going to personalize systems for yourself.

**Для Напарника:** Давать выбор (длительность, формат шага) и запоминать, что сработало именно у этого человека; не навязывать один «правильный» метод.


### Визуальные «дома» для вещей предотвращают хаос  ·  [15:47](https://youtu.be/FpnCcfZmgO4?t=947)
**Мысль:** У каждой вещи есть видимое место, цвет и контейнер; иначе вещи оказываются не там и цикл повторяется.

> Number 10, 10, 10, 10. Visual homes, visual homes, visual homes. I create homes for everything because I was so disorganized and now I have homes for everything. And it's very visual. So I have homes for I mean, the simplest homes are things like you'd find the kitchen, it is called a pantry. Where are the plates? Well they're in the home where the plates are, you know, the kitchen is kind of an easy. Where are the forks? Where's the trash can in the kitchen? Those are, even the trash can is the home for trash, right? But how can we apply that to other things, visual homes? What do you need a home for, your hobbies, your electronics, your cables? Like start making visual homes. Your files? What do you need homes for in your life? Like getting organized, just keep it simple. Just make a home for everything, get some boxes, color code, and visual, make it visual. Otherwise, you put things in the wrong place and the cycle continues.

**Для Напарника:** Метафора для острова: у каждого типа дел свой видимый «дом»/постройка на карте.
